- **Root cause:** `mapRewardSnapshots` is populated only during the RPC call.
  `LoadAllDistributeSnapshot` can read persisted requests but has no startup
  caller. The database duplicate check then rejects a repeated request.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/rewards.cpp:22,29-47,390-421`,
  `AddDistributeRewardSnapshot` and `CheckRewardDistributions`;
  `src/assets/snapshotrequestdb.cpp:202-235`, `LoadAllDistributeSnapshot`.
- **Concrete scenario:** A reward request fails for low funds, the node
  restarts, and the wallet is funded. No automatic retry occurs, while a new
  identical `distributereward` call is rejected as a duplicate.
- **Expected behavior:** Persist a wallet identity with the request, reload
  pending requests safely, and retry only with the same uniquely identified
  loaded wallet. Requests without a trustworthy owner must not spend.
- **Proposed remediation:** Add versioned wallet binding to the reward DB and
  a startup reload path. Define a conservative migration for old unbound
  requests and a wallet-unload/reload policy before enabling retry. Account
  for the crash window between transaction commit and payout-record write,
  and bind ownership snapshots to their chain identity across reorgs.
- **Regression required:** Fail a reward for low funds, restart, fund the
  initiating wallet, and verify a single payout from that wallet. Verify old
  unbound records do not charge another wallet.
- **Remediation commit:** `b3803bbc0` binds new reward requests to a unique
  wallet key, genesis, snapshot block and snapshot contents. Startup reloads
  those requests. Each payout batch persists its transaction ID before wallet
  commit. A missing or unconfirmed transaction outside the mempool, duplicate
  wallet identity, changed snapshot, or old unbound request cannot trigger an
  automatic replacement payout. The changed snapshot receives status 10 for
  operator reconciliation; an uncertain transaction receives status 6.
- **Verification:** Before the fix, the low-funds restart scenario failed
  because funding after restart produced no payout. The dedicated
  `feature_rewards_restart.py` now passes the single-payout restart and
  snapshot replacement cases. The original `feature_rewards.py` and
  `make check -j4` pass. The dedicated test is mandatory in both CI
  workflows. The first local behavioral gate could not launch that new test
  because its executable bit was missing; `405b4d55d` sets the bit. A
  subsequent gate passed the new test, then stopped on an unrelated local
  Python 3.12 `asyncore` import limitation. Exact-SHA CI qualification is
  pending.
- **Residual limitation:** Requests created before the new binding format and
  uncertain committed transactions require manual reconciliation. A retry
  occurs when the next block is connected after startup, not immediately at
  wallet load. Automatic reassignment of legacy requests is intentionally
  disallowed because the original wallet identity was never persisted.
- **Operator guidance:** Commit 0d828f8ae adds recovery instructions to
  `doc/release-notes/release-notes-4.8.1.md`: preserve the bound wallet,
  inspect the exact reward tuple and debug log, and reconcile uncertain,
  changed-snapshot, or legacy unbound requests without editing the database.
  A status value alone is not evidence that a payment was made.
- **Final status:** MITIGATED. Build Raven 39553065704 and Final Gate
  39553079553 passed the mandatory reward restart regressions on source
  d9c5b5606. Final release-SHA CI and operator review of the manual
  reconciliation cases remain due under FINDING-067.

### FINDING-101: Restricted and qualifier wallet state uses wallet zero

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Multiwallet tag and freeze views can omit the selected
  wallet's events and expose another wallet's address associations.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d5ff39d1ad71cfb42dbba9cba48766e17eeefd35`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet-scoped asset views must not use a
  different wallet, including future PQ asset ownership views.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`; consensus restricted-asset
  validation uses separate state and is not shown to diverge here.
- **Root cause:** Restricted events are filtered and signaled through
  `vpwallets[0]`, but the restricted-asset view database is node-global.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:5255-5259`,
  `src/validation.cpp:3022-3034`, `src/rpc/messages.cpp:423,467`,
  `src/qt/myrestrictedassettablemodel.cpp:89-104`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Wallet B receives a qualifier tag while wallet A is
  loaded first. B misses its event; querying wallet B can show A's stored
  address/tag associations from the shared view database.
- **Expected behavior:** Collect relevant events for every loaded wallet,
  notify each owner, and filter wallet-facing views by the selected wallet.
- **Proposed remediation:** Separate node-global indexing from per-wallet
  filtering and backfill historical wallet B state after upgrade.
- **Regression required:** Two wallets in both load orders receive distinct
  tag/freeze events. Each RPC and GUI view shows only its own data before and
  after restart.
- **Remediation commit:** `4f026d02b` records tag and freeze events in a
  node-global index independent of wallet load order. The selected wallet
  filters RPC and Qt rows by address ownership. Startup rebuilds historical
  events before loading wallets; a missing pruned block marks the index
  incomplete and the wallet RPC refuses to show a partial history. Per-block
  undo restores rows on reorg; an index mismatch invalidates the index and
  queues a Qt model reload. Reindex explicitly resets the index state.
- **Verification:** The new `wallet_restricted_multiwallet.py` failed on the
  prior binary because wallet B inherited wallet A's tagged address. It
  passes after the fix across both wallet load orders, restart, index deletion
  and rebuild, reorg and reconsideration, and full reindex. Existing
  `feature_restricted_assets.py` and `feature_restricted_verifydb.py` pass.
  `make -j4` and mandatory `make check -j4` pass. The new regression is
  registered in the test runner and both CI workflows. A separate review of
  the fallback reorg path found and then cleared a stale Qt cache risk.
- **Residual limitation:** Qt's test entry point is disabled in this branch,
  so the GUI display itself still needs manual qualification. Pruned nodes
  without the required historical blocks cannot rebuild the wallet view;
  they report incomplete history instead of presenting a partial result.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI and manual GUI qualification
  remain due under FINDING-067.

### FINDING-102: Message channel subscription uses wallet zero

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A non-first wallet may miss asset-channel messages
  and shared RPC state may cross wallet boundaries.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d5ff39d1ad71cfb42dbba9cba48766e17eeefd35`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet-specific asset notification behavior
  must remain correct if assets move to PQ ownership.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** Historical scan and transfer hooks inspect only
  `vpwallets[0]`; a one-time global startup flag does not account for wallets
  added later. Message RPCs use a shared channel database.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/messages.cpp:221`,
  `src/coins.cpp:284,310`, `src/init.cpp:2034-2044`,
  `src/rpc/messages.cpp:152`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Wallet B owns a message-channel token but wallet A
  is loaded first. B is not automatically subscribed and can miss messages.
- **Expected behavior:** Scan every loaded wallet, backfill on wallet load,
  and distinguish global channel data from wallet-scoped RPC results.
- **Proposed remediation:** Track subscription scan state per wallet and use
  the selected wallet for automatic subscription and display filtering.
- **Regression required:** Two wallets with different channels in both load
  orders, including wallet addition after startup and restart.
- **Remediation commit:** `212610f62` gives each wallet a file-bound random
  scope, records scoped channel preferences, automatic subscription events,
  seen addresses, messages, tombstones, and scan checkpoints, and uses the
  selected wallet for messaging RPCs. Startup replays the required history
  before certifying a wallet inbox. Asset reorgs durably invalidate all
  persisted wallet scopes, including unloaded wallets. A full replay first
  orphans saved messages, then restores active matches. Message rows and
  channel rows are synced before a single synced checkpoint and completion
  batch is published. Manual subscribe and unsubscribe events retain their
  order and block anchors; an orphaned unsubscribe is reanchored with its
  suppression state. Automatic events are seeded at their original heights
  during incremental scans. Block-template probes cannot record automatic
  channel events as confirmed blocks.
- **Verification:** The original multiwallet regression failed before wallet
  scoping. Targeted red runs separately exposed an unsafe scan checkpoint,
  an incomplete reorg replay, a missed automatic resubscription after manual
  unsubscribe, and stale suppression after its later resubscription was
  orphaned. The final `wallet_messaging_multiwallet.py` passes with two
  wallets, reversed load order, late loading, copied wallet isolation,
  manual and automatic subscriptions, reorg, unloaded-wallet recovery, and
  a deterministic crash after coins flush but before message flush.
  `make check -j4` and the structural and behavioral invariant gate passed;
  the new regression is mandatory in both CI workflows.
- **Residual limitation:** A full historical scan requires unpruned block
  data. A missing block leaves the wallet inbox explicitly incomplete.
  Manual Windows and macOS GUI validation and exact-SHA CI remain release
  gates under FINDING-067.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source 8e87426de in both exact-head
  workflows. Final-SHA qualification after FINDING-187 remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-103: Asset test fails to compile in no-wallet builds

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A required build and test configuration cannot
  complete, so release qualification is incomplete.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `a1cca8037948db68b972804139c097531e5842d0`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Mandatory asset compatibility tests must run
  in wallet-enabled and no-wallet configurations.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This is a new asset-branch test defect.
- **Root cause:** The test constructs `CAssetOutputEntry` outside
  `ENABLE_WALLET`, but includes the header defining it only when the wallet
  is enabled.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `src/test/assets/asset_tx_tests.cpp:22-24,120,167,200`, asset script tests.
- **Introducing commit:** `a828ffaaa6`.
- **Concrete scenario:** A clean no-wallet `make check` fails to compile
  `test/assets/test_raven-asset_tx_tests.o` with an incomplete type error.
- **Expected behavior:** Consensus asset tests compile and run without wallet
  support, without skipping their assertions.
- **Proposed remediation:** Include the type definition unconditionally while
  retaining wallet-only database headers under the wallet guard.
- **Regression required:** Clean no-wallet build and `make check`, plus the
  wallet-enabled asset test suite.
- **Remediation commit:** `bfb1d5440`.
- **Verification:** A clean no-wallet `make check` failed before this fix with
  three incomplete-type errors in `asset_tx_tests.cpp`. The same configuration
  compiled and passed `make check` after the fix. Wallet-enabled `make check`
  also passed; no asset assertion was skipped or weakened.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA GitHub qualification pending.

### FINDING-104: Transfer-script activation survives a reorg incorrectly

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest processes on the same best chain can
  parse the same asset transfer differently and disagree on block validity.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `75d349b53fbe3d0bd6ba81ee3c50e20667349071`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Contextual activation and reorg safety for
  PQ asset outputs, especially transfers that use `OP_PUSHDATA1`.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The transfer-script-size deployment on bit 8
  and its parser gate are inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** `AreTransferScriptsSizeDeployed` sets a process-global
  `fTransferScriptIsActive` latch after seeing an ACTIVE tip and never clears
  it after a reorg. `TransferAssetFromScript` chooses a dynamic asset-data
  offset only when that latch is true; otherwise it uses hard-coded offset
  31. The offset differs for `OP_PUSHDATA1` asset scripts.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:6074-6083`,
  `AreTransferScriptsSizeDeployed`; `src/consensus/consensus.h:54`, latch;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:663-690`, `TransferAssetFromScript`;
  `src/script/script.cpp:245-263`, dynamic offset;
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:269,716`, asset validation callers;
  `src/validation.cpp:683,2726`, mempool and block entry points.
- **Concrete exploitability:** A node observes bit-8 ACTIVE and then follows
  a reorg to a pre-ACTIVE tip. Its latch remains true. A freshly started node
  at that same tip has a false latch. A transfer script whose payload crosses
  the direct-push boundary can be parsed at offset 32 by the first process
  and at offset 31 by the second. The candidate bit-13 PQ asset deployment
  can activate before bit 8 on regtest unless an explicit dependency is added.
- **Expected behavior:** Every consensus parser decision uses the versionbits
  state of the candidate block's own parent. Mempool policy uses the current
  active-chain tip and updates on reorg. No process-history latch may control
  block validity.
- **Proposed remediation:** Thread contextual bit-8 activation through the
  consensus asset parser, preserving historical pre-activation semantics.
  Require bit 8 as an effective prerequisite of the separate PQ asset
  deployment. Keep non-consensus callers explicitly tip-contextual.
- **Regression required:** `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer vectors at activation
  minus 2, minus 1, activation, plus 1 and across invalidate/reconsider.
  Compare long-running and restarted nodes on the same best chain; both
  must accept or reject identically. Test both mempool and block connection.
- **Reproduction:** On regtest, `feature_transfer_script_reorg.py` reached
  bit-8 ACTIVE, tested a long transfer, invalidated the first ACTIVE tip,
  and compared the live process with a restart on the identical LOCKED_IN
  chain. The live process returned `missing-inputs`, meaning serialization
  passed; the restarted process returned
  `16: bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize` for the same raw transaction.
  The vulnerable binary failed the equality assertion as intended.
- **Local mitigation:** The monotonic latch has been removed. The same
  `feature_transfer_script_reorg.py` vector passes on the corrected binary;
  it failed on the pre-mitigation binary. A clean wallet-enabled `make check`
  also passes. The parser is still tip-contextual in candidate-block and
  disconnect paths, so this is not a complete consensus correction.
- **Mitigation commit:** `cb86a3ca566bfc884313eb747cbae2cd6d224b65`.
- **Additional boundary regression:** `edcd4ddbb` checks the identical
  raw `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer at LOCKED_IN tips 861 and 862, at the
  first ACTIVE next-block state at tip 863, and after a real transfer is
  mined at height 864. Both pre-ACTIVE tips reject the transfer payload;
  the ACTIVE tips reach the expected missing-input result. Invalidation,
  restart, reconsideration, and the real transfer block remain in the
  same passing functional test. The private run log is
  evidence/f104-expanded-boundary-final.log. This extends the previously
  recorded vulnerable-binary red and corrected-binary green regression. It does
  not yet supply a mined `OP_PUSHDATA1` accept/reject vector at every
  requested height around the boundary.
- **Additional mined positive boundary regression:** `87965b3a4` extends
  the same functional test with actual long-name transfer outputs mined
  at the first three ACTIVE heights, 864 through 866, and checks those
  outputs through rollback, restart, reconsideration, and `verifychain`.
  Its direct Python 3.10 run passed; private log:
  `evidence/f104-hminus2-hplus2-local.log`. The earlier pre-ACTIVE
  candidate-block rejection and vulnerable-binary red test remain in
  place. This does not claim a connected pre-ACTIVE transfer block.
- **Final coverage disposition:** The bit-8 regression now checks the same
  long transfer at the two pre-ACTIVE tips, rejects pre-ACTIVE mined
  side-branch candidates, and connects the first three ACTIVE transfer
  heights. Rollback, restart, reconsideration, mempool evaluation, and
  `verifychain` pass. The original vulnerable-binary red result and the
  corrected-binary green result remain preserved. All 33 mandatory
  functional tests, local `make -j4 check`, Build Raven `38713686026`,
  and Final Gate `38713685824` passed on `0c33cc92c`.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the bit-8 contextual activation defect.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains tracked by FINDING-067.

### FINDING-105: Asset deployment activation survives a reorg incorrectly

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. A live process accepts an asset issue on a
  pre-activation best chain while a freshly started process on that same
  chain rejects it.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `f7b4b5704`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A PQ asset extension must never rely on
  process-history activation state for asset issuance or validation.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The original bit-6 asset deployment and its
  consensus gate are inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** `AreAssetsDeployed` sets `fAssetsIsActive` on the first
  ACTIVE tip and never resets it after a reorg. Mempool and block connection
  use this process-local latch rather than the candidate chain context.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:6038-6048`,
  `AreAssetsDeployed`; `src/consensus/consensus.h:52`, latch;
  `src/validation.cpp:671-685,2712-2729`, mempool and block gates;
  `src/rpc/assets.cpp:420`, issue RPC gate.
- **Concrete exploitability:** On regtest, reach bit-6 ACTIVE, call an asset
  RPC to set the latch, and invalidate the first ACTIVE tip. A new, fully
  signed issue transaction funded by an older RVN UTXO passes
  `testmempoolaccept` in the live process. A restart on the identical
  LOCKED_IN chain rejects the exact raw transaction. The same stale gate is
  called from `ConnectBlock`, so block validity is process-history-dependent.
- **Expected behavior:** Activation checks for a block use its own parent;
  mempool and RPC checks use the current active tip, with reorg-safe cleanup.
- **Proposed remediation:** Remove the monotonic latch for consensus use,
  thread contextual bit-6 state through block validation, and revalidate
  mempool asset transactions after crossing the boundary in either direction.
- **Regression required:** `feature_asset_activation_reorg.py` must fail
  against the vulnerable implementation and pass after correction. Add
  block-connection vectors at activation minus 2 through plus 2 and test
  invalidate/reconsider and fresh restart.
- **Reproduction:** The vulnerable binary returned `allowed: 1` in the live
  process and `allowed: 0` after restart for the same signed issue on the
  same LOCKED_IN chain. The equality assertion failed as intended.
- **Local mitigation:** The monotonic latch has been removed. The corrected
  `feature_asset_activation_reorg.py` test builds a signed issue while ACTIVE,
  then tests that identical transaction after a reorg and after restart. It
  passes on the corrected binary and fails on the pre-mitigation binary with
  live `allowed: 1` versus restarted `allowed: 0`. A clean wallet-enabled
  `make check` also passes. Candidate-block and disconnect paths still need
  explicit parent context, so this is not a complete consensus correction.
- **Mitigation commit:** `cb86a3ca566bfc884313eb747cbae2cd6d224b65`.
- **Additional mined boundary regression:** `241d1517f` mines a signed
  issue in the first block after the tip reports ACTIVE and verifies the
  next block. On rollback, a hand-built issue block at the pre-ACTIVE
  boundary is rejected with `bad-txns-assets-not-active`. The same raw
  issue is rejected in the mempool at two earlier tips, then checked
  after restart and accepted again when the ACTIVE branch is
  reconsidered. The direct Python 3.10 functional run passed. The
  private run log is evidence/f105-expanded-boundary-final.log. The
  original vulnerable-binary red and corrected-binary green reorg
  comparison remains recorded above. A complete mined H-2 through H+2
  and alternate-side-branch matrix is still due.
- **Additional side-branch boundary regression:** `9ded737c7` mines
  real issue outputs at the first three ACTIVE heights and submits the
  same signed issue in pre-ACTIVE sibling candidates at heights H-2 and
  H-1 while the active tip is past activation. Those short branches
  remain `inconclusive` until eligible for connection; after rewinding
  the main chain, they are marked invalid. A separate candidate built
  on the current pre-ACTIVE parent rejects with
  `bad-txns-assets-not-active`. Restart, reconsideration, and
  `verifychain` pass. The direct Python 3.10 run passed; private log:
  `evidence/f105-hminus2-hplus2-local.log`. The deferred short-branch
  result is recorded explicitly rather than treated as immediate
  block-connection proof.
- **Connected active competing branch:** `2fd13fc9e` builds a distinct
  issue block at the first bit-6 active height on a side branch, extends
  that branch beyond the original three-issue tip, and verifies its connected
  asset state before and after restart. Invalidating the side branch
  restores the original two later asset issues. Reconsidering both
  branches after a rollback to LOCKED_IN again selects the longer branch,
  then restores the original branch on invalidation. The direct Python
  3.10 test, all 33 mandatory functional tests, and local `make -j4 check`
  passed. Private logs are
  `evidence/required-functional-f107-sidebranches-local.log` and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f107-sidebranches.log`.
- **Final coverage disposition:** The bit-6 test rejects the same signed
  issue on pre-ACTIVE candidate parents, mines real issues at H through
  H+2, connects a distinct longer ACTIVE side branch, and compares asset
  state after rollback, restart, and reconsideration. The original
  vulnerable-binary red and corrected-binary green results remain
  preserved. All 33 mandatory functional tests, local `make -j4 check`,
  Build Raven `38713686026`, and Final Gate `38713685824` passed on
  `0c33cc92c`.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the bit-6 contextual activation defect.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains tracked by FINDING-067.

### FINDING-106: Additional Core 4.8.0 deployment latches survive reorgs

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. The affected predicates reach consensus validation.
- **Initial status:** OPEN in the unchanged helper implementations at
  `f7b4b5704`; identified before any remediation to these three helpers.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical block validation and candidate
  PQ asset validation must not depend on process history.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Enforced asset values, coinbase asset
  checks, and restricted/qualifier asset deployment.
- **Root cause:** `AreEnforcedValuesDeployed`,
  `AreCoinbaseCheckAssetsDeployed`, and `IsRip5Active` set process-global true
  flags at LOCKED_IN or ACTIVE and never clear them on reorg. The exact latch
  implementations are present in the official Core 4.8.0 source at
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774:src/validation.cpp:5804-5869`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:6014-6036,6054-6064`,
  the three helpers; `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:313-322,377-389,590,690-705,830-855,884-892`,
  transaction and asset validation; `src/validation.cpp:2927-2932`, coinbase
  value check; `src/assets/assets.cpp:5391-5445`, restricted-transfer check.
- **Concrete divergence scenario:** After a node observes the deployment
  threshold and then returns to an earlier best chain, its flag stays true.
  A fresh node on that same chain derives false. Reissue output values,
  coinbase asset outputs, and restricted/qualifier transactions are therefore
  evaluated under different rules. `feature_rip5_activation_reorg.py`
  reproduced the restricted-asset policy divergence: `listglobalrestrictions`
  was available on the live node but unavailable after restart on the same
  LOCKED_IN chain. Dedicated transaction/block reproductions for the
  enforced-value and coinbase subcases are still required; static control
  flow establishes the process-history dependency but not every transaction's
  exploitability.
- **Unit reproduction:**
  `rip25_versionbits_tests/asset_deployment_queries_rewind_after_active_tip`
  constructs independent LOCKED_IN, ACTIVE, and STARTED branches for each
  deployment using the real regtest thresholds. Before the final two-helper
  correction, it failed only for `enforced values remained active after
  rewind` and `coinbase assets remained active after rewind`; the three
  previously corrected queries passed. It also asserts the special
  enforced-value LOCKED_IN behavior.
- **Expected behavior:** Each block uses its own predecessor's VersionBits
  state; mempool and P2P policy use the current active-chain state.
- **Proposed remediation:** Remove process-history latches and pass explicit
  candidate-parent context through consensus validation and undo. Preserve
  the unusual LOCKED_IN-or-ACTIVE rule for enforced values.
- **Regression required:** ACTIVE-to-preactive reorg and restart vectors for
  all three deployments, including valid and invalid block connection.
- **Local mitigation:** All three process-history latches are removed in the
  local worktree, while the test-only enforced-value and coinbase overrides
  remain available. The RIP5 functional vector
  `feature_rip5_activation_reorg.py` failed before the change with live
  `True` versus restarted `False`, then passed after the change. All three
  activation-reorg tests passed through `test_runner.py --require-tests`,
  and the enlarged synthetic-chain unit test failed only for the two remaining
  latches before the final patch, then passed after it. Fresh wallet and
  no-wallet builds, both `make check` suites, and the three required
  functional tests passed after the final patch and the FINDING-108 test
  isolation correction. The block-context issue is separately FINDING-107.
- **Mitigation commits:** `1a7a2030a8f6fde4d3e04e6140df75463cd16b0e`
  (RIP5), `8a4cd56cac2af9d124033118c69406d29e692ec4` (boundary test),
  `b912239dfd494bcb594cb0190161140b45fcb15d` (value and coinbase).
- **Additional candidate-block boundary regression:** `209cd937a` extends
  the synthetic VersionBits test to the last LOCKED_IN parent, first ACTIVE
  parent, and two later ACTIVE parents for all three predicates. It also
  isolates bit 7 from already-active bit 6 in the RIP5 functional test:
  the same valid signed restricted issue is rejected by a mined candidate
  block immediately before bit 7 activation, accepted and mined at its
  first active height, then verified after rollback, restart, and
  reconsideration. Nine VersionBits unit cases and the direct Python 3.10
  functional run passed. Private logs are
  evidence/f106-107-versionbits-241d-final.log and
  evidence/f106-rip5-boundary-241d-final.log. Enforced-value and
  coinbase mined-block vectors are still due; this finding remains open.
- **Mined enforced-value and coinbase regression:** `fde4d21e8` adds
  mandatory `feature_enforced_coinbase_activation.py`. A signed
  nonzero-value reissue candidate is accepted at height 575 and
  rejected at the first bit-9 LOCKED_IN height 576 with
  `bad-txns-asset-reissued-amount-isn't-zero`. A coinbase asset
  candidate is accepted at height 1499 and rejected at the first
  bit-10 ACTIVE height 1500 with
  `bad-txns-coinbase-contains-asset-txes`. The test includes sibling
  branches, rewind, restart, reconsideration, and `verifychain`.
  Its direct Python 3.10 run and all 33 required functional cases
  passed locally, as did `make -j4 check`; private logs:
  `evidence/f106-f107-enforced-coinbase-local.log`,
  `evidence/required-functional-local-final-candidate.log`, and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f173-test-candidate.log`. Build Raven
  `38592937851` passed its security job, but the paired Final Gate
  stopped before tests for the separate FINDING-174 ccache race.
- **Expanded mined boundary matrix:** `76983ad18` submits the same signed
  nonzero reissue at bit-9 heights 574 and 575, where the old rule accepts
  it, and at 576, 577, and 578, where the enforced-value rule rejects it.
  It also submits an asset-bearing coinbase at bit-10 heights 1498 and
  1499, where the old rule accepts it, and at 1500, 1501, and 1502, where
  the active rule rejects it. The test connects competing pre-activation
  branches, rewinds, restarts, restores the active branch, and verifies
  chain state. The direct Python 3.10 run passed; all 33 mandatory
  functional tests and local `make -j4 check` passed afterward. Private
  logs are `evidence/f107-bit9-bit10-matrix-local.log`,
  `evidence/required-functional-f107-expanded-local.log`, and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f107-expanded.log`.
- **Final coverage disposition:** The signed enforced-value reissue and
  asset-bearing coinbase candidates now cover H-2, H-1, H, H+1, and H+2
  under their respective old and new rules. The bit-7 restricted-asset
  test covers the same boundary, real ACTIVE issue outputs, competing
  branches, rollback, restart, and reconsideration. The unit test
  preserves the enforced-value LOCKED_IN rule. All 33 mandatory
  functional tests, local `make -j4 check`, Build Raven `38713686026`,
  and Final Gate `38713685824` passed on `0c33cc92c`.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the three inherited deployment latches.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains tracked by FINDING-067.

### FINDING-107: Context-free block checks consume active-tip asset state

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Side-branch or historical blocks can be judged
  using deployment rules from an unrelated active tip.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `f7b4b5704`, before any contextual refactor.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Candidate blocks, reorgs, and restart/IBD
  must derive one contextual activation state from the candidate parent.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Bit-6 assets, bit-8 transfer-script size,
  enforced values, coinbase asset checks, and RIP5 restricted assets.
- **Root cause:** `CheckBlock` is called before the candidate parent is looked
  up and caches `block.fChecked`, but it calls `CheckTransaction`, which uses
  active-tip predicates and the transfer parser. `DisconnectBlock` also reads
  tip state while undoing a block whose own predecessor can straddle an
  activation boundary. Removing the monotonic latches alone cannot fix this.
- **Introducing baseline:** The official Core 4.8.0 commit
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774` already calls
  `CheckTransaction` from `CheckBlock` at `src/validation.cpp:4032`, with the
  tip-dependent transfer and enforced-value checks at
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:269,313`. RIP-25 integration did not invent
  the block-context error, but its separate PQ asset deployment increases
  the number of activation boundaries affected.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:4168-4173,4225,4241-4243,4620-4623,4674-4683`,
  block checks and admission; `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:269,313`,
  tip-dependent checks; `src/assets/assets.cpp:663-690`, parser;
  `src/validation.cpp:1939,1979,2002,2139,3319-3360`, undo context.
- **Concrete divergence scenario:** A node validates a side-branch candidate
  whose parent is pre-bit-8 while its active tip is post-bit-8, or the reverse.
  A long transfer has different parse offsets. The same candidate can then
  be accepted or marked invalid depending on which branch happens to be
  active when `CheckBlock` runs.
- **Direct block-check reproduction:**
  `rip25_versionbits_tests/checkblock_asset_result_does_not_depend_on_unrelated_tip`
  builds one identical candidate block containing an asset-bearing coinbase,
  then calls `CheckBlock` with the same block under synthetic ACTIVE and
  STARTED active tips for bit 10. The test fails on the vulnerable code:
  `CheckBlock judged the same candidate differently after the active tip
  changed`. It bypasses PoW and merkle checks only to isolate this consensus
  predicate; it does not establish network-level exploitability or successful
  block connection. A mined block-level reproduction remains required.
- **Expected behavior:** Context-free checks never consult deployment state;
  contextual asset checks receive the candidate `pindexPrev`; undo uses the
  disconnected block's own parent.
- **Proposed remediation:** Derive immutable asset-rule context from
  `VersionBitsState(pindexPrev, ...)`, move contextual checks out of
  `CheckBlock`, and thread that context through block connection, asset
  parsing, and undo without altering historical activation predicates.
- **Regression required:** Side-branch blocks on both sides of each boundary,
  activation minus 2 through plus 2, disconnect/reconsider, and fresh
  restart comparisons.
- **Partial remediation commit:** `50ef33f7781db0436825a5968c0b83b5e6223680`.
  `CheckBlock` now performs only context-free transaction checks;
  `ContextualCheckBlock` derives asset predicates from the candidate parent
  and runs the full transaction check on admission, reconnection, and
  `VerifyDB`. The outdated block-version check also uses the candidate
  parent. The existing direct block-check red test and a new explicit
  transfer-payload boundary test pass. Both wallet and no-wallet `make check`
  pass on the combined local implementation. The required three activation
  reorg tests and `feature_assets.py`, `feature_assets_reorg.py`, and
  `feature_raw_restricted_assets.py` all pass in one six-test run. The
  extended raw-asset and restricted-asset functional tests also pass when
  invoked directly with Python 3.10.
- **Additional remediation commit:**
  `a361997550ba9d5db274e5ed17462bd891dc0d92` passes an immutable
  candidate-parent deployment context through block connection, asset
  parsing, UTXO and asset-cache updates, disconnection, and rollforward.
  Three new unit cases cover enforced-value, transfer-parser, and messaging
  boundaries. The three cases, wallet and no-wallet `make check`, the RIP-25
  VersionBits unit suite, and a six-test functional activation and asset
  regression batch passed on this source state. One wallet `make check`
  attempt hit the independently tracked FINDING-112 intermittent recovery
  failure; a repeat `make check` and full direct unit suite passed.
- **Mined-boundary test commit:** `8a2e577f44a5c46fa5b9058d9643e22791a177e7`
  mines a real issue at height 863 and an `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer at height
  864, then verifies the chain before and after invalidate, restart, and
  reconsider. The test passed on the out-of-tree wallet build.
- **Side-branch test commit:** `d144c9eee` mines a real height-862 sibling
  of a height-861 LOCKED_IN parent while the verifier's active tip is at
  height 864 and bit 8 is ACTIVE. The sibling contains a signed long-name
  `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer whose inputs exist on both branches and whose
  locktime is zero. `AcceptBlock` rejects it specifically with
  `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize`; 26 of 26 required functional
  tests passed on the corrected binary. The new test is mandatory in both
  workflows. It covers candidate-parent admission, not side-branch
  `ConnectBlock` or historical replay after overtaking the active chain.
- **Side-branch connection test commit:** `eb46048f7` extends the same test
  through a longer competing branch containing a valid post-bit-8 transfer,
  actual connection and reorganization, rollback to LOCKED_IN, restart,
  reconsideration, and `verifychain`. It passed twice in the required
  functional runner and once by direct invocation with the clean build's
  `ravend` selected explicitly. One initial direct invocation accidentally
  selected an older executable on `PATH` and returned `inconclusive`; that
  was a test-command error, not a result from the audited binary.
- **Additional side-branch boundary regression:** `3e2d65d89` submits the
  same signed long-name transfer in candidate blocks at heights 862 and
  863 while the verifier's active tip is past activation; both candidates
  are rejected with the old-parser reason. The competing branch mines
  the transfer at height 864 and the verifier checks its asset name,
  amount, message, expiry, and `OP_PUSHDATA1` encoding after reorganization.
  The direct Python 3.10 functional run and nine VersionBits unit cases
  passed; private log: evidence/f107-sidebranch-boundary-241d-final.log.
  Side-branch block vectors around the other deployment
  boundaries remain due; this finding remains open.
- **Additional cross-deployment side-branch evidence:** `9ded737c7`
  covers bit-6 issue candidates on pre-ACTIVE siblings and confirms
  they become invalid when that branch is connected. `fde4d21e8`
  covers bit-9 enforced-value and bit-10 coinbase candidates on both
  sides of their respective boundary, with rewind, restart, and
  reconsideration. These complement the earlier bit-7 RIP5 and bit-8
  transfer candidates. Direct functional runs passed, and all 33
  required functional cases passed locally. The complete active-chain
  testnet replay is separate evidence for historical compatibility;
  it cannot establish unknown side-branch behavior. Private logs:
  `evidence/f105-hminus2-hplus2-local.log`,
  `evidence/f106-f107-enforced-coinbase-local.log`, and
  `evidence/required-functional-local-final-candidate.log`.
- **Expanded bit-7 side-branch matrix:** `65461d0b5` rejects the same
  signed restricted-issue transaction in candidate blocks at heights
  574 and 575 while the process has already observed bit 7 ACTIVE.
  Distinct restricted issues then enter connected blocks at the first
  three active heights 576, 577, and 578. A rewind across the boundary,
  restart under the old rule, reconsideration, and `verifychain` preserve
  all three issues and reject pre-activation admission. The direct Python
  3.10 run, all 33 mandatory functional tests, and local `make -j4 check`
  passed. `76983ad18` separately extends the bit-9 and bit-10 competing
  branch checks to H-2, H-1, H, H+1, and H+2. Private logs are
  `evidence/f107-bit7-matrix-local.log`,
  `evidence/required-functional-f107-expanded-local.log`, and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f107-expanded.log`.
- **Connected bit-7 side branches:** `cb9bbbbe8` submits restricted-issue
  candidates at H-2 and H-1 while an unrelated active tip is at H;
  each candidate remains on a short side branch, then becomes invalid
  when its competing main-chain child is removed. It also connects a
  distinct valid issue branch at H, extends it beyond the original H+2
  tip, verifies its restricted asset state after restart, and restores
  the original H+1 and H+2 issues after invalidation. Reconsideration
  after a rollback to LOCKED_IN again selects the longer valid branch.
  The direct Python 3.10 test, all 33 mandatory functional tests, and
  local `make -j4 check` passed. Private logs are
  `evidence/required-functional-f107-sidebranches-local.log` and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f107-sidebranches.log`.
- **Final coverage disposition:** Bits 6, 7, 8, 9, and 10 now have
  candidate-parent boundary and competing-branch regressions. The bit-6
  and bit-7 valid alternate branches actually overtake the selected
  chain and survive restart; invalid pre-ACTIVE candidates are judged
  when their own branches become eligible for connection. The bit-8
  long-transfer branch also connects and reorganizes. All 33 mandatory
  functional tests and local `make -j4 check` passed. The clean a16a05bdb
  binary replayed public testnet history from genesis with
  `-assumevalid=0`, then passed a full active-chain scan and a deep
  2016-block check. No production source file outside `src/test/`
  changed between that build and `0c33cc92c`, whose Build Raven
  `38713686026` and Final Gate
  `38713685824` both passed. The previously open scoped boundary and
  side-branch proof obligations are met.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the candidate-parent consensus-context
  defect. Final release-SHA qualification remains tracked by FINDING-067.

### FINDING-108: Synthetic VersionBits test reuses stale cache entries

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. This affects reliability of a required security test,
  not production validation directly.
- **Initial status:** OPEN in the wallet-enabled full suite after the
  FINDING-106 regression test was added.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The Core 4.8.0 bit-11 reorg regression must
  run reliably as part of `make check`.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Transfer-overflow activation testing only;
  the production bit-11 fix is not shown to be defective by this failure.
- **Root cause:** `transfer_overflow_active_tip_policy_is_not_sticky` creates
  short-lived synthetic `CBlockIndex` objects, but the process-global
  `versionbitscache` can contain entries for addresses reused from earlier
  tests. The test neither clears the cache before using those synthetic
  pointers nor removes their cache entries before destroying them.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/test/rip25_versionbits_tests.cpp:161-201`,
  `transfer_overflow_active_tip_policy_is_not_sticky`; `src/validation.cpp:2350`,
  global cache.
- **Introducing commit:** `92ff8206793956c40be8cf028ba2f026788a2eed`
  introduced this test during the earlier RIP-25/Core 4.8.0 remediation.
  It is not inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Reproduction:** The individual test passed, while a wallet-enabled full
  `make check` failed at its assertion that bit 11 returns to STARTED. The
  no-wallet full suite passed in that run, consistent with allocator-dependent
  pointer reuse rather than a stable production rejection.
- **Expected behavior:** Synthetic test chains have an isolated VersionBits
  cache and preserve the same ACTIVE-to-STARTED assertion.
- **Remediation:** Clear the global cache under `cs_main` before installing
  synthetic tips and after restoring the original tip. Apply the same isolation
  to the new five-deployment regression test.
- **Regression required:** Wallet and no-wallet full `make check`, with the
  existing bit-11 assertion unchanged.
- **Verification:** Both full suites passed after cache isolation; the new
  five-deployment boundary test also passed in both variants.
- **Remediation commit:** `8a4cd56cac2af9d124033118c69406d29e692ec4`.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA GitHub qualification pending.

### FINDING-109: Empty per-address asset name reaches undefined string access

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. An untrusted malformed transaction reaches undefined
  behavior during transaction validation; a crash has not yet been reproduced
  on the current build.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `58fbe4c301032f2965335f19738a2a1895a0c4b3`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** None directly. This is an asset-validation
  memory-safety issue that must be resolved before release qualification.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Per-address restricted and qualifier null
  data validation. The official Core 4.8.0 source has the same unchecked
  `.front()` call, so this defect is inherited rather than introduced by
  the RIP-25 integration.
- **Root cause:** `AssetNullDataFromScript` accepts the serialized empty
  `asset_name`; `CheckTransaction` stores it in `mapNullDataTxCount`, then
  calls `entry.first.first.front()` without checking whether the string is
  empty. The analogous global null-data path already checks emptiness.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:206-228,349-351`,
  `CheckTransaction`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:854-876`,
  `AssetNullDataFromScript`; `src/script/script.cpp:331-335`, null-script
  recognition.
- **Concrete exploit scenario:** A peer or RPC caller submits a transaction
  with a zero-value `CNullAssetTxData("", 0)` output and a non-null input.
  Normal mempool validation enters `.front()` on the empty name. Whether it
  crashes depends on the standard-library build and compiler behavior.
- **Expected behavior:** Reject the malformed null-data output with a
  deterministic reason before any empty-string indexing or expensive
  contextual asset processing.
- **Proposed remediation:** Add an explicit empty-name guard at the narrow
  validation point and retain the existing name/ownership checks for
  nonempty records.
- **Regression required:** A real `CheckTransaction` negative vector for an
  empty per-address null-data asset name, plus wallet and no-wallet unit
  gates. Run a sanitizer variant if available.
- **Remediation commit:** `7e034d6ffcd9e9e2a8b2908bf60d6e8c74a80e46`.
- **Verification:** The new test failed before the guard because validation
  returned the generic missing-transfer reason after touching the empty
  string. It passed after the guard. Full wallet and no-wallet `make check`
  passed on the combined local implementation. The guard is at
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:210`; sanitizer qualification remains pending.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; sanitizer and exact-SHA CI pending.

### FINDING-110: Block-load exception suppresses transfer-deserialization rejection

- **Severity:** HIGH. The block admission path can store a block even after
  contextual asset validation rejects it. Full block-connection acceptance
  has not been demonstrated, so this is not classified as invalid-block
  consensus acceptance.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `58fbe4c301032f2965335f19738a2a1895a0c4b3`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Reindex, restart, and live admission must
  apply the same candidate-parent consensus rules.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Transfer-script-size deployment. The
  official Core 4.8.0 `AcceptBlock` has the same `fFromLoad` exception.
- **Root cause:** `AcceptBlock` resets a rejected `CValidationState` to valid
  when loading a block from disk and the rejection reason is
  `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize`. The old active-tip parser error
  likely motivated this workaround, but candidate-parent parsing now makes
  it unsafe to retain without a historical compatibility proof.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:4637-4651`,
  `AcceptBlock`; `src/validation.cpp:5539-5640`, `LoadExternalBlockFile`.
- **Concrete divergence scenario:** A malformed transfer is rejected during
  live block admission but its contextual rejection is suppressed when the
  same block is loaded from a block file. `VerifyDB` level 1 then rejects
  it, and later connection may reject it under a different reason.
- **Expected behavior:** Loading an identical block under an identical
  candidate parent cannot turn a consensus validation failure into success.
- **Proposed remediation:** Remove the special exception with explicit
  candidate-parent parsing in place, then validate historical activation
  boundaries before release. Preserve evidence for any historical block
  that relies on the exception.
- **Regression required:** A block-file reload test with a malformed
  transfer, plus historical activation-boundary replay and restart checks.
- **Remediation commits:** `86e45c90b` adds the red block-file regression;
  `911ff7cb3` removes the exception. The non-consensus tooling fix needed for
  out-of-tree execution is FINDING-111.
- **Verification:** An identical malformed block returned
  `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize` through live `submitblock`, but
  the loader stored its bytes before the fix. The test failed because
  `getblock` unexpectedly returned those bytes. After the fix, the same
  test passed and the imported node kept the exact valid 100-block tip.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; replay of the historical mainnet boundary
  and exact-SHA CI remain pending.

### FINDING-111: Block-file test cannot run from an out-of-tree build

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A required live-versus-import regression cannot run
  against a clean out-of-tree build, weakening the release test gate.
- **Initial status:** OPEN while constructing the FINDING-110 regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The exact candidate source must be tested
  without copying or generating executables in the source tree.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The same source-relative hash-helper path
  exists in official Core 4.8.0, so the build-layout defect is inherited.
- **Root cause:** `linearize-data.py` always executes
  `src/test/test_raven_hash` relative to its own source-tree location, while
  the out-of-tree build places that binary under the configured build root.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `contrib/linearize/linearize-data.py:52-55`,
  `calc_hash_str`; `test/functional/feature_loadblock.py:75-81`, loader test.
- **Reproduction:** The FINDING-110 red regression attempted to run the
  existing block-file test against the clean out-of-tree build and failed
  before block validation with `FileNotFoundError` for the source-tree helper.
- **Expected behavior:** The test supplies the exact hash helper built from
  the current source to the linearizer without placing binaries in the
  source tree or changing consensus code during tests.
- **Proposed remediation:** Allow the linearizer configuration to specify an
  executable hash-helper path and have the functional test pass the current
  build's helper. Keep its existing source-relative default for other users.
- **Regression required:** The block-file functional test must run from a
  clean out-of-tree build and still test linearization plus invalid-block
  rejection.
- **Remediation commit:** `86e45c90b`.
- **Verification:** The test initially failed with `FileNotFoundError` for
  the source-tree helper. With the configured build helper, linearization
  completed for 101 block hashes and the live-versus-import regression ran
  red before FINDING-110's fix and green after it.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI pending.

### FINDING-112: Full wallet unit suite intermittently fails recovery tests

- **Severity:** MEDIUM pending root-cause classification. This is a release
  test-gate reliability problem, not proof of a production wallet-recovery
  failure.
- **Initial status:** OPEN after two failures on the same source state.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet recovery and encryption regressions
  must execute reliably as required release tests.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Unknown. These particular PQ wallet tests
  were introduced during RIP-25 hardening, but the possible underlying
  Berkeley DB issue has not been attributed.
- **Observed failure:** Two successive `make check` runs of the same wallet
  build failed, first at
  `recovery_handles_dump_larger_than_locked_pool_limit`, then at
  `partial_recovery_installs_atomically_and_preserves_backup`. Each named
  recovery test passed alone, the PQ wallet suite passed alone, and a later
  complete `test_raven` run and third `make check` passed without a source
  change between the failures and successes.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `src/wallet/test/pq_wallet_tests.cpp:1640-1666,1699-1709`, recovery tests;
  `src/wallet/db.cpp:285-523`, `CDB::RecoverInternal`. The failing return
  branch has not yet been identified.
- **Expected behavior:** Identical source and fixtures produce consistent
  pass/fail results, with enough failure detail to diagnose a recovery
  failure without weakening security assertions.
- **Proposed remediation:** Capture the exact `RecoverInternal` failure path
  under repeated full-suite execution, distinguish test isolation from a
  production recovery race, then fix the responsible layer with a red/green
  regression. Do not skip or relax either test.
- **Regression required:** Repeated full wallet `make check` runs and the
  affected recovery cases with the original assertions intact.
- **Additional evidence:** Five later full `make check` runs passed all 511
  unit cases without a source change. Private logs are
  `evidence/f112-repeat-1.make.log` through
  `evidence/f112-repeat-5.make.log`, with matching `.unit.log` files.
  Neither historical failure yielded a retained stage-specific Berkeley
  DB diagnostic, so the passing repeats do not establish a root cause.
- **Owner disposition:** On 2026-10-15 the owner accepted classifying the
  historical cause as not reconstructible from the retained evidence,
  documenting the residual test-gate risk, and requiring a green
  mandatory `make check` on the final 4.8.1 SHA. This disposition is
  separate from FINDING-166 and does not prove a wallet production
  defect or waive either recovery assertion.
- **Final status:** DISPOSED, historical cause unknown. Any renewed
  recovery failure must be diagnosed and tracked before release;
  FINDING-067 retains the final-SHA `make check` gate.

### FINDING-113: Crash replay rolls UTXO state forward without asset state

- **Severity:** HIGH. Asset ownership and issuance state can disagree with
  the recovered UTXO tip after an interrupted coins flush or reorganization.
  Whether a particular recovered state accepts an invalid block remains to
  be demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN on the uncommitted FINDING-107 context-propagation
  patch based on the existing `6d2a3f69f32bb295bf867e5cf7e872f6d5d15e69`
  tree. This finding was frozen before its remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart and replay must produce the same
  asset state as uninterrupted block connection. The future PQ asset
  extension also depends on this invariant.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Chainstate-ahead recovery and its asset DB
  rebuild requirements. The missing asset-cache argument is already present
  in official Core 4.8.0 commit
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`, so it is inherited.
- **Root cause:** `ReplayBlocks` creates an asset cache and passes it to
  old-branch disconnection, but omits it when calling `RollforwardBlock` for
  the new branch. `RollforwardBlock` therefore passes a null asset cache to
  `SpendCoin` and `AddCoins`; their asset updates are gated on a nonnull cache.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5270-5287`,
  `RollforwardBlock`; `src/validation.cpp:5295-5354`, `ReplayBlocks`;
  `src/coins.cpp:103-105,264-265,383-385`, asset-cache gates.
- **Concrete scenario:** After an interrupted flush during a reorganization,
  replay undoes old-branch asset changes, advances the UTXO set to the new
  branch, and flushes an asset cache without applying new-branch issuance,
  transfers, or restrictions. A restarted node can then report or validate
  asset state differently from an uninterrupted peer.
- **Expected behavior:** Replay applies both UTXO and asset-cache changes for
  each rolled-forward block using that block's candidate-parent deployment
  context, then flushes coherent state.
- **Proposed remediation:** Pass the existing `assetsCache` to
  `RollforwardBlock` and verify an interrupted-flush or reorg replay against
  uninterrupted asset state. Do not redesign the replay mechanism.
- **Regression required:** A red/green replay test containing an asset
  issuance or transfer on the forward branch, including restart and asset DB
  comparison. Repeat with restricted state if practical.
- **Reproduction:** `feature_asset_dbcrash.py` generated an asset-active
  chain, flushed the parent, issued `REPLAYCRASHASSET` in block 433, and used
  `-dbbatchsize=1 -dbcrashratio=1` to exit after the partial coins batch.
  The debug log confirmed `Simulating a crash`, `Replaying blocks`, and
  rollforward of block 433. The block hash recovered, but `getassetdata`
  returned null instead of the previously observed issuance metadata.
- **Remediation commits:** `7f7b030209fdedbfe364ef54995c303f2486ff82`
  adds the red functional regression;
  `8e2a7037d57f78450e312429f0676711018eb0fd` passes the existing
  `assetsCache` into `RollforwardBlock`;
  `28b41f0824fe0e517bdaf0ed8ece4a328d487148` adds a second abrupt
  restart assertion after normal recovery startup.
- **Verification:** The focused functional test failed before the pointer
  fix and passed after it in two seconds on the same out-of-tree wallet
  build. The second abrupt restart, wallet `make check`, and no-wallet
  `make check` passed. A restricted-asset replay vector and exact-SHA CI
  remain due.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the demonstrated one-crash loss. The separate
  replay durability window in FINDING-114 remains open for release.

### FINDING-114: Coins flush clears recovery state before assets are durable

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. A power loss
  after the coins best-block write but before the asset DB dump can leave two
  honest nodes on the same block chain with different asset state and hence
  different asset-validation results. Invalid-block acceptance has not yet
  been demonstrated at the RPC or P2P boundary.
- **Initial status:** OPEN during adversarial review of the FINDING-113 fix.
  This finding was frozen before any remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart, repeated interruption, and IBD
  recovery must produce coherent asset and UTXO state.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The official Core 4.8.0 code has the
  same ordinary flush ordering and replay ordering, with no durable marker
  spanning the coins and asset databases. This defect is inherited;
  FINDING-113 makes the replay variant visible in memory.
- **Root cause:** Ordinary `FlushStateToDisk` finalizes the coins best block
  and clears its head-block recovery marker before `DumpCacheToDatabase`
  writes the asset and restricted databases. `ReplayBlocks` likewise calls
  `cache.Flush()` before `assetsCache.Flush()`, which only merges changes into
  global memory. There is no durable asset-state proof linking the stores.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5351-5355`,
  `ReplayBlocks`; `src/coins.cpp:482-486`, coins cache flush;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:2844-2856`, asset cache flush;
  `src/validation.cpp:3211-3223,5444`, ordinary and eventual asset DB flush;
  `src/txdb.cpp:211-218`, coins final batch.
- **Concrete scenario:** A block issues or reissues an asset. During an
  ordinary full flush, the coins final batch records the new best block and
  erases the head marker. A power loss before the asset DB dump leaves the
  asset DB at the prior state. Restart sees complete coins state and skips
  replay. The same gap appears if a first crash invokes `ReplayBlocks` and
  a second crash occurs before `RewindBlockIndex` dumps recovered assets.
- **Expected behavior:** A complete durable coins tip must never be visible
  on restart without matching durable asset state or a durable instruction
  to rebuild or replay both stores.
- **Proposed remediation:** Include an asset-commit-pending marker in the
  coins DB transition, retain it through the coins final batch, synchronize
  both asset and restricted databases after their dumps, and only then clear
  the pending marker. Startup with an uncertain complete coins tip must fail
  closed or rebuild after checking that required block data is available.
  Do not merely reverse the write order: an interruption during a partial
  asset DB dump may make replay non-idempotent.
- **Regression required:** Fault injection after the ordinary coins final
  batch and before asset DB persistence, plus a second interruption during
  recovery. Compare issuance, balances, and restrictions with an
  uninterrupted reference node and cover pruned-node failure handling.
- **Direct reproduction:** A regtest-only height hook stopped the node just
  after the ordinary coins flush for block 435 and before the asset dump.
  On the vulnerable startup path, the recovered block hash matched the
  mined block but `getassetdata(POSTCOINCRASHASSET)` returned null rather
  than the observed issuance metadata. The failing run was retained in the
  private audit evidence.
- **Remediation commit:** `ecd4ca0771a3e6d8a33bfe6b239ab9ed2e172e96`.
  The coins DB now records a pending asset-commit
  marker in both partial and final coins batches. A full flush dumps the
  asset cache, synchronizes the asset and restricted databases, then
  atomically clears pending and records a versioned validated tip with a
  synchronized coins DB batch. Startup rejects an incomplete or uncertified
  nonempty chainstate and rebuilds only when the block data is available.
  On a pruned or missing-data chainstate it stops without wiping databases.
  Legacy chainstates without a validated marker require one full rebuild.
- **Verification:** The same post-coins-flush functional test now
  passes, including issuance and reissue recovery. A new unit test checks
  pending, validated, stale, and unknown-version marker states. Wallet
  `make check`, no-wallet `make check`, and a five-test activation/crash
  functional batch pass. The additional vectors compare asset transfer
  balances and UTXO state with an uninterrupted node, reconstruct a
  restricted asset verifier and qualifier tag, and prove that truncated
  block data is detected before any automatic chainstate wipe.
  An independent source review found no additional ordering gap for the
  canonical asset or restricted state. A pruned-node fault test, exact-SHA
  CI, and final second audit remain due. Full-block preflight before a
  legacy rebuild adds substantial one-time I/O and must be assessed for
  deployment.
- **Test stabilization:** `87a7726b1` waits for the asynchronous
  restricted-state rebuild to reach the exact expected tip before the
  existing hash, metadata, and qualifier assertions. The test still
  fails if the rebuild does not complete within 60 seconds.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the reproduced inconsistent-state
  scenarios. Release qualification is still pending, including pruned-node
  handling and independent second review.

### FINDING-115: Reissue replay dereferences absent asset undo storage

- **Severity:** HIGH. A crash-interrupted coins flush whose forward block
  changes reissue metadata causes a startup segmentation fault, preventing
  normal recovery until code or chainstate is repaired.
- **Initial status:** OPEN after the FINDING-113 pointer fix, before this
  finding's remediation. The defect was frozen before a code correction.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart and IBD must reconstruct asset
  state safely and identically to uninterrupted block connection.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Asset-state crash replay. This precise
  null dereference was introduced by `8e2a7037d57f78450e312429f0676711018eb0fd`:
  official Core 4.8.0 left the asset cache null in rollforward, which
  avoided this dereference while losing asset state as FINDING-113 describes.
- **Root cause:** `RollforwardBlock` now passes a nonnull asset cache to
  `AddCoins` but still passes a null `undoAssetData`. On a reissue changing
  IPFS data, units, or verifier, `AddCoins` unconditionally writes the old
  metadata through that null pointer.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5279-5287`,
  `RollforwardBlock`; `src/coins.cpp:124-188`, `AddCoins`.
- **Concrete scenario:** After a clean asset issuance, reissue it with a
  changed units field. Force a partial coins DB flush with
  `-dbbatchsize=1 -dbcrashratio=1`. The next startup enters replay and
  exits with signal 11 before RPC becomes available.
- **Expected behavior:** Replay may discard newly computed undo metadata
  because the block's original undo was already written, but it must pass
  valid scratch storage to `AddCoins` and must not crash.
- **Proposed remediation:** Supply a fresh local undo pair for each replayed
  transaction, or make the `AddCoins` undo output explicitly optional with
  guarded writes. Preserve the existing block undo semantics.
- **Regression required:** The real crash-replay functional test must
  include a reissue that changes units, IPFS data, or verifier, fail with
  the old code, and pass after the fix. Repeat under wallet and no-wallet
  unit gates.
- **Reproduction:** An extended `feature_asset_dbcrash.py` on the unmodified
  FINDING-113 fix failed at restart with `ravend exited with status -11 during
  initialization` after reissuing 5 units and setting the asset's units
  field to 1.
- **Remediation commits:** `06b93874d6f6b901d84dea85d58625598c5f4b18`
  adds the red reissue replay vector;
  `553c0b5a2c4209466bda75600d0de544675a319a` supplies transaction-local
  scratch undo storage to replay `AddCoins`.
- **Verification:** The same functional test failed before the fix with
  signal 11 and passed after the fix, including recovered amount 42 and
  units 1. Wallet and no-wallet `make check` both passed after the fix.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the reproduced crash; broader
  qualification and FINDING-114 release review remain pending.

### FINDING-116: Partial coins replay can double-count asset transfers

- **Severity:** HIGH. An interrupted
  coins batch can leave the asset balance/index cache different from a clean
  connection of the same block. A consensus-invalid block acceptance path
  has not yet been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN during adversarial review after FINDING-113.
  This finding was frozen before remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart and reorganization must yield the
  same asset ownership and amount state as uninterrupted validation.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The underlying partial-coins replay path
  is inherited. Official Core 4.8.0 passed no asset cache in rollforward and
  lost asset updates entirely; the FINDING-113 integration fix exposes a
  distinct non-idempotent transfer path.
- **Root cause:** `RollforwardBlock` ignores the return from `SpendCoin`.
  When the input coin was already erased by the interrupted DB batch,
  `SpendCoin` returns before `TrySpendCoin` can debit the old asset address.
  `AddCoins` then applies the transfer output asset credit unconditionally,
  even when replaying an already-applied output.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5279-5288`,
  `RollforwardBlock`; `src/coins.cpp:361-389`, `SpendCoin`;
  `src/coins.cpp:257-280`, `AddCoins`.
- **Concrete scenario:** The partial coins batch has already erased the
  input UTXO of an asset transfer but has not completed the block. Replay
  sees the input missing, skips its asset debit, and credits the transfer
  output. The old and new address balances can both remain credited.
- **Expected behavior:** Crash recovery must reconstruct asset state from
  a known consistent parent or reject the ambiguous state; it cannot infer
  which asset-cache effects were applied from a partially written UTXO set.
- **Proposed remediation:** Fail closed on asset-active interrupted coins
  batches and rebuild coins, asset, and restricted state from indexed blocks
  after a pruned-data preflight. Do not make the asset-cache update depend
  only on whether an input UTXO happened to survive the partial batch.
- **Regression required:** A deterministic partial-batch transfer test that
  compares both address balances and chainstate against an uninterrupted
  reference, plus restart and pruned-node handling.
- **Regression:** `feature_asset_dbcrash_transfer.py` forces a partial coins
  batch crash while moving an asset from one address to another. After
  restart it compares both address balances, supply, metadata, and UTXO
  statistics to an uninterrupted reference node. The test passes with the
  safe-rebuild candidate; its expected startup log confirms that unsafe
  `ReplayBlocks` rollforward did not execute.
- **Remediation commit:** `ecd4ca0771a3e6d8a33bfe6b239ab9ed2e172e96`.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the reachable interrupted-startup path
  by the FINDING-114 rebuild guard. Final adversarial and cross-platform
  qualification remain due. The original `RollforwardBlock` remains
  non-idempotent, so the startup guard is security-critical.

### FINDING-117: Deep chain verification rejects a valid qualifier-tag block

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid chain containing a recently added qualifier
  tag fails `verifychain(4, 1)`. Nodes configured to perform level-4 startup
  verification can fail to start despite having a valid chainstate. A live
  consensus-split or invalid-block acceptance path has not been shown.
- **Initial status:** OPEN before remediation. The focused functional test
  failed on a normal uninterrupted chain, independently of FINDING-114's
  crash/recovery path.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical verification and reorganization
  must evaluate restricted-asset state at the block being checked.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** `CVerifyDB` copies the active asset cache
  then disconnects blocks in memory. The qualifier checker skips that local
  cache and consults the still-current global cache. Both behaviors are
  present in official Core 4.8.0, so this finding is inherited.
- **Root cause:** `VerifyQualifierChange` passes `fSkipTempCache=true` to
  `CheckForAddressQualifier`. This deliberately ignores modifications made
  within a connecting block, but during `CVerifyDB` reconnect it also
  ignores the historical state reconstructed in its private cache. The
  global cache still shows the tip's tag, making the original add appear
  to be a duplicate.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5193-5194,5238-5244,5258-5268`,
  `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:4832-4900`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressQualifier`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:5155-5167`,
  `VerifyQualifierChange`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb.py` activates
  restricted assets, issues a qualifier, mines one valid tag assignment,
  and calls `verifychain(4, 1)`. The call returns false. The debug log
  reports `bad-txns-null-data-add-qualifier-when-already-assigned` while
  reconnecting that same valid tip block. The tag is confirmed present by
  `checkaddresstag` before verification.
- **Expected behavior:** Level-4 verification uses the reconstructed
  pre-block restricted state while preserving the established rule that
  transactions inside a block do not make a qualifier change appear
  pre-existing to other transactions in that same block.
- **Proposed remediation:** Separate the historical pre-block cache from
  the current block's temporary changes in restricted-asset validation.
  Do not simply disable `fSkipTempCache` globally, because that could alter
  consensus checks for multiple restricted transactions in one block.
- **Regression required:** The red level-4 vector, a level-4 startup test,
  add/remove and freeze/unfreeze historical reconnection, and same-block
  ordering vectors.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` reconnects each historical block
  through a fresh child asset cache whose parent is the reconstructed
  pre-block state. The within-block skip now reads that parent.
- **Verification:** The previously red level-4 test, including startup
  verification, passes. The selected 12-test functional security gate
  and wallet-enabled and no-wallet `make check` pass locally.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-118: Historical restricted verifier can remain stale during deep verification

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid restricted-asset transfer can make
  `verifychain(4, 7)` fail after the verifier string changes twice. A node
  configured for deep level-4 startup verification can reject a valid local
  chain. A live consensus-split path has not been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The regression was found while adversarially
  extending the candidate remediation for FINDING-117. The candidate is
  uncommitted and does not resolve this case.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical validation must use the asset
  verifier in force at the block being reconnected. This also affects
  restricted-asset handling independent of PQ signing.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The cache insertion logic and
  `CVerifyDB` memory-only reconnect design exist in official 4.8.0. This is
  inherited, although the new pre-block cache path exposes it in a focused
  regression.
- **Root cause:** `CAssetCacheRestrictedVerifiers` set ordering keys only
  on asset name. `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier` inserts a new verifier
  without removing the existing same-name entry. During a deep VerifyDB
  reconnect, the issuance verifier remains in the accumulated local cache;
  a later reissue's replacement insertion is a no-op.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assettypes.h:531-534`,
  `CAssetCacheRestrictedVerifiers::operator<`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:2276-2285`,
  `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier`;
  `src/validation.cpp:5193-5273`, `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb.py` issues a
  restricted asset under a qualifier verifier, reissues it with verifier
  `true`, transfers to an untagged address, then reissues it back to the
  qualifier verifier. `verifychain(4, 3)` succeeds, but depth 7 fails while
  reconnecting the valid transfer at height 444 with
  `bad-txns-null-verifier-address-failed-verification`.
- **Expected behavior:** Each verifier update replaces the preceding local
  cache entry for that asset, including during deep historical reconnection.
- **Proposed remediation:** Replace an existing same-name add or remove entry
  during VerifyDB's memory-only replay, while preserving normal block
  connection semantics. Retain the deep level-4 regression.
- **Regression required:** Exercise the transfer on both sides of a
  verifier change at shallow and deep verification depths.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` replaces older verifier entries only
  in the historical aggregate, while each reconnected block uses normal
  first-insert semantics in its own child cache.
- **Verification:** The shallow/deep verifier test and the same-block
  first-wins unit test pass, as does the selected 12-test gate.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-119: Later subqualifier tag corrupts historical root lookup

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid restricted-asset transfer to an untagged
  address fails level-4 verification after that address receives a
  subqualifier. Startup with deep verification can reject a valid chain.
  No live invalid-block acceptance path has been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The focused regression failed against the
  current FINDING-117 candidate before further remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical asset verification must not
  retroactively apply a later tag to an earlier transfer. This is also a
  pre-existing restricted-asset behavior independent of PQ signing.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The root/subqualifier lookup and
  memory-only VerifyDB replay are inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** Undoing a `#ROOT/#SUB` tag records the subtag in the local
  `mapRootQualifierAddressesRemove`. `CheckForAddressQualifier` never
  consults that map before the live tip's root-add cache or database root
  prefix scan, so a historical `#ROOT` lookup can return true.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:2165-2182`,
  `CAssetsCache::RemoveQualifierAddress`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:4834-4927`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressQualifier`;
  `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:148-169`,
  `CRestrictedDB::CheckForAddressRootQualifier`;
  `src/validation.cpp:5193-5273`, `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb_root.py` issues
  a restricted asset with verifier `!#VERIFYROOT`, transfers it to an
  untagged address, then assigns `#VERIFYROOT/#SUB` in the next block.
  `checkaddresstag` confirms the root tag at the tip, while
  `verifychain(4, 2)` returns false reconnecting the earlier valid
  transfer.
- **Expected behavior:** Root qualification is computed from the
  reconstructed pre-block set of exact and subqualifier tags, excluding
  tags undone from later blocks.
- **Proposed remediation:** Make historical lookup account for local
  subqualifier removals before consulting live tip state, without changing
  ordinary consensus validation of same-block tag ordering.
- **Regression required:** The focused root/subtag vector, plus cases
  with multiple subtags so removing one does not hide another.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` checks exact-tag add/remove overlay
  for each root/subqualifier candidate instead of trusting a root map or
  live-tip database result alone.
- **Verification:** Historical root replay, multiple-subtag, persisted
  root, and two-node tests pass in the selected security gate.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-120: Revoked subqualifier causes a live restricted-asset consensus split

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest nodes at the same best block can
  disagree about the validity of the next restricted-asset transfer. The
  dirty-cache node accepted and mined an unauthorized transfer; a restarted
  peer rejected the same block.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The two-node red regression demonstrated the
  split before remediation. No release or push is permitted with this open.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** All nodes must validate restricted-asset
  ownership and transfers identically. The bug also affects Core 4.8.0
  independently of RIP-25 and predates the PQ asset extension.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Exact same bad insertion is present in
  official Core 4.8.0 commit `b60f50` at `src/assets/assets.cpp:2983-2986`.
  This is inherited, not introduced by the RIP-25 integration.
- **Root cause:** `CAssetsCache::Flush` merges local
  `mapRootQualifierAddressesRemove` entries into the global
  `mapRootQualifierAddressesAdd`. Removing the only `#ROOT/#SUB` tag leaves
  a false-positive root qualification in the dirty in-memory cache. A
  full asset database dump clears that cache and erases the exact subtag,
  so a restarted node correctly sees no root qualification.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:3025-3029`,
  `CAssetsCache::Flush`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:4834-4937`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressQualifier`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:1635-1647`,
  `CAssetTransfer::ContextualCheckAgainstVerifyString`;
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:736-743`, `Consensus::CheckTxAssets`;
  `src/validation.cpp:2756-2762`, `ConnectBlock`.
- **Concrete exploit:** `feature_restricted_root_split.py` creates two
  nodes with an identical tip. It removes the sole subtag from recipient
  R, then restarts one node. The uninterrupted node reports root
  qualification true; the restarted node reports false. The former
  accepts and mines a restricted transfer to R. The restarted node's
  `submitblock` returns `rejected` for that exact block. A separate
  single-node regression records `True` before restart and `False`
