  after restart for the same chain tip.
- **Expected behavior:** Removing a subtag removes it from the global
  root-add cache, records its removal, and makes root qualification false
  unless another exact or subqualifier tag remains. Flush and restart
  must not change consensus validation.
- **Proposed remediation:** Correct the root-cache merge, then audit
  local/global add/remove precedence and database fallback. Preserve
  legitimate root qualification when one of multiple subtags remains.
- **Regression required:** The two-node accept/reject vector, same-tip
  pre/post-restart lookup, multiple-subtag boundary, and level-4
  historical verification after later tag changes.
- **Partial remediation commit:** `a4f2117e0` corrects both root-add and
  root-remove cache merge directions. It passes the in-memory split vector,
  but a stronger vector that persists the tag before revocation still
  fails: the database root-prefix lookup sees a now-revoked subtag.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` overlays the exact active state on
  each candidate subtag. The symmetric merge in `a4f2117e0` remains.
- **Verification:** The original dirty/restarted two-node split and the
  stronger persisted-tag variant pass. Removing one of two subtags keeps
  the root true; removing the last makes it false across dump/restart.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-121: Serialized-key ordering splits persisted root qualification

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest nodes at the same block can disagree
  about a valid restricted-asset transfer when one has dumped its asset
  cache. The dirty node accepts the transfer; the flushed node rejects its
  block solely because an unrelated qualifier sorts before the subtag in
  LevelDB's serialized key order.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. A red two-node test demonstrated divergent
  qualification and block rejection during the second adversarial review.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restricted-asset validation must be
  independent of cache flush timing and unrelated tags.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Official Core 4.8.0 commit `b60f50`
  has the same unsafe seek and early break in
  `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:148-166`. This is inherited, not introduced
  by the RIP-25 integration. The initial replacement iterator inherited
  the same assumption and was rejected before commitment.
- **Root cause:** The database key contains a serialized `std::string`.
  Its CompactSize length precedes the string bytes, so a LevelDB seek to
  the textual root/prefix does not imply textual-prefix ordering. The
  iterator can encounter an unrelated same-length qualifier first and
  stop before a longer active subqualifier key.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:148-169`,
  `CRestrictedDB::CheckForAddressRootQualifier` (inherited);
  `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:156-185`,
  `CRestrictedDB::AnyAddressSubQualifier`;
  `src/dbwrapper.h:139-153`, serialized iterator seek;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:4846-4925`, root qualification.
- **Concrete exploit:** `feature_restricted_root_prefix_split.py` tags
  a recipient with `#PREFIXROOT/#SUB` and an unrelated same-length
  `#ZZZZZZZZZZZ`. Two nodes share the same tip; one dumps the asset DB.
  The dirty node reports root qualification true and mines a restricted
  transfer. The flushed peer reports false and returns
  `duplicate-invalid` on submission of that block. The single-node
  cache test also changes from true to false immediately upon DB dump.
- **Expected behavior:** An active subqualifier remains visible regardless
  of other keys and whether the asset cache has been dumped.
- **Proposed remediation:** Seek separately for each valid serialized
  qualifier length, filter full qualifier names, overlay dirty exact-tag
  state, and stop as soon as a qualifying active subtag is found. Do not
  assume textual prefix order across different serialized lengths.
- **Regression required:** Two-node dirty/flushed block acceptance and
  equal-length unrelated-key boundary vectors.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` uses length-aware seeks in
  `AnyAddressSubQualifier` and exact-tag overlay for every candidate.
  `165f60448` isolates peer block submission in the regression test.
- **Verification:** The dirty/flushed two-node split, unrelated
  equal-length qualifier, cache dump, and restart cases pass.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-122: Historical freeze overlays read the live tip before replay state

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Level-4 verification rejects valid restricted
  transfers after a freeze/unfreeze cycle followed by a reorganization.
  A node configured for deep startup verification may reject a valid
  chain. No live accept/reject split from this replay-only path has been
  demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. Three focused red regressions independently
  reproduce address-level freeze, global freeze, and verifier variants.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical asset checks must use the
  block's reconstructed pre-state while preserving the same-block skip
  policy. This is independent of PQ signature validity.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The checker precedence and VerifyDB
  memory-only reconnect design are inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** A historical cache can contain a local unfreeze add,
  while the live global cache contains a later freeze undo. The address
  and global restriction checkers inspect the live global remove set
  before the reconstructed local add, so the valid historical transfer
  appears frozen. The verifier-string checker has the same precedence
  pattern and selects a later live verifier over the historical one.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:4763-4831`,
  `CAssetsCache::GetAssetVerifierStringIfExists`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:4940-5002`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressRestriction`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:5003-5058`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForGlobalRestriction`;
  `src/validation.cpp:5193-5278`, `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb_freeze.py` and
  `feature_restricted_verifydb_global.py` mine freeze, unfreeze, valid
  transfer, freeze, unfreeze, then invalidate the last unfreeze. The
  active tip is the second freeze; `verifychain(4, 4)` returns false
  while replaying the valid transfer. The address case logs
  `bad-txns-restricted-asset-transfer-from-frozen-address`.
  `feature_restricted_verifydb_verifier.py` changes the verifier from
  A to B, invalidates a later reissue, and observes
  `verifychain(4, 3)` reject a transfer valid under A.
- **Expected behavior:** During VerifyDB only, reconstructed local
  add/remove entries override the still-live global cache. Normal
  block connection and within-block consensus rules remain unchanged.
- **Proposed remediation:** Apply replay-scoped local-add precedence to
  verifier, address freeze, and global freeze checks, then run direct
  depth/reorg regressions for each.
- **Regression required:** Both frozen-scope red vectors and the verifier
  reissue/live-remove vector.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` scopes historical lookup precedence
  to the reconstructed parent while leaving normal block rules intact.
- **Verification:** Both freeze tests and the verifier/reorg test pass,
  including level-4 replay of valid historical transfers.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-123: Mempool reissue map changes block consensus validation

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest nodes with the same chain but different
  pending reissues can accept and reject the same otherwise valid block.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The focused test is red before remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Block validity must depend on chain context,
  not a node's mempool. The issue is independent of PQ signatures.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0;
  `b60f50:src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp` contains the unconditional lookup.
- **Root cause:** `Consensus::CheckTxAssets` consults the global
  `mapReissuedAssets` mempool anti-chaining map even when its
  `fCheckMempool` argument is false in `ConnectBlock`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:808-819`,
  `Consensus::CheckTxAssets`; `src/validation.cpp:2758`, `ConnectBlock`.
- **Introducing commit:** `f2a0d74c09` added the unconditional lookup;
  `080c1d5509` changed the rejection text.
- **Concrete reproduction:** After a reorg, an invalidated tip reissue
  returns to the mempool. A historical block containing a different valid
  reissue is rejected by `verifychain(4, 3)` with
  `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`. The test asserts the pending
  reissue is present in the mempool before the rejected replay. A live
  block carrying the historical reissue would likewise be rejected only
  by a node with the conflicting pending reissue.
- **Expected behavior:** The anti-chaining map may constrain mempool
  admission, but must never influence block acceptance or historical
  verification. Existing independent consensus checks remain intact.
- **Proposed remediation:** Apply the map conflict check only when
  `fCheckMempool` is true. Keep block-side reissue collection independent
  of the mempool map. Review block and mempool entry points separately.
- **Regression required:** The red reorg/mempool/VerifyDB test, a direct
  two-node block-acceptance split test, and existing asset reissue tests.
- **Remediation commits:** `b9b918b72` gates the map lookup on
  `fCheckMempool`; `927f8b9af` adds the live block test. The earlier
  verifier replay test acquired an explicit mempool precondition in
  `141527c49`.
- **Verification:** The live two-node test is red on the pre-fix binary
  (`submitblock` returns `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`) and green
  on the corrected binary. The verifier replay test is red before and
  green after the fix, with the conflicting reissue confirmed in mempool.
  `feature_restricted_assets.py` and `feature_raw_restricted_assets.py`
  also pass on the corrected binary.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; broader qualification and final-SHA
  review remain pending.

### FINDING-124: Historical replay changes same-block verifier ordering

- **Severity:** HIGH. Level-4 verification can reject a valid asset block,
  preventing startup when deep chain checking is enabled.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. A focused unit regression is red against the
  uncommitted VerifyDB remediation candidate.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The verifier state used to review a block
  must match the state used by normal block connection. This is separate
  from PQ cryptographic verification.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The first-insert verifier set behavior
  is inherited from official Core 4.8.0. The mismatch is introduced by
  the current historical-replay remediation candidate, not by 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier` normally leaves
  the first same-name verifier in its per-block set. Replay mode erases
  that entry on every later add, so the second verifier wins even when
  both transactions are in one block.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:2276-2289`,
  `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier`; `src/validation.cpp:5264-5274`,
  `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`; `src/test/assets/asset_reissue_tests.cpp:205-227`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Two chained, consensus-valid restricted-asset
  reissues occupy one block; the second spends the first's returned owner
  token. They set different verifier strings. Normal connection retains
  the first value in its per-block cache, but replay substitutes the
  second. A later same-block reissue or transfer can therefore be
  accepted normally and rejected by VerifyDB. The unit regression
  observes replay `ACCREDITED` versus normal `KYC` and exits 201.
- **Expected behavior:** VerifyDB must use a fresh per-block asset cache
  with the same lookup and merge ordering as normal block connection,
  while accumulating historical state separately across blocks.
- **Proposed remediation:** Reconnect each block with a fresh child cache
  whose historical parent is the reconstructed cache; merge the child
  only after successful connection. Do not alter live consensus ordering.
- **Regression required:** The red same-block verifier unit test, the
  earlier historical verifier functional tests, and a multi-reissue
  full-block vector when practical.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` reconnects into a fresh per-block
  child and merges it into the historical parent after success. No full
  asset-cache copy occurs for each block.
- **Verification:** The unit test is red on the earlier candidate
  (replay `ACCREDITED`, normal `KYC`) and green after the fix; its
  next-block assertion verifies the later value replaces the earlier
  one. Wallet-enabled and no-wallet `make check` and the historical
  verifier tests pass. A full multi-reissue block vector remains desirable.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the demonstrated mismatch;
  full-block adversarial coverage remains pending.

### FINDING-125: Required CI gate omits new asset consensus regressions

- **Severity:** HIGH. A green security job could coexist with reopened
  asset consensus splits because the new tests were not in its required
  functional list.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The required workflow list contains none of
  the F117-F124 root, replay, or mempool block-split tests.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The security qualification gate must run
  the actual consensus regression tests, not just inspect their files.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The omitted tests cover several
  inherited Core 4.8.0 asset-validation defects, plus integration fixes.
- **Root cause:** The tests were added after the explicit workflow list;
  most new scripts also lacked executable mode required by the runner.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml:80-88`, required functional step;
  `.github/workflows/rip25-v48-final-gate.yml:102-109`, matching step;
  `test/functional/test_runner.py:65-75`, registered extended tests;
  executable modes of new `feature_restricted_*` scripts.
- **Concrete reproduction:** Before gate integration, neither workflow
  names the new tests. After adding names but before correcting file
  modes, `test_runner.py --require-tests` executes the first test then
  raises `PermissionError` opening the next script. This fails closed,
  but prevents the required gate from qualifying the branch.
- **Expected behavior:** Both workflows explicitly name every security
  test, the runner knows each name, all scripts are executable, and
  `--require-tests` fails if any test is absent, skipped, or red.
- **Proposed remediation:** Register the new scripts, add them to both
  required workflow commands, set executable file modes, and run the
  exact selected list through the runner.
- **Regression required:** Execute the selected gate successfully and
  prove an unknown or failing named test produces a nonzero result.
- **Remediation commits:** `b3bcd5101` registers and requires the tests in
  both workflows and makes the new scripts executable; `7900d8ab4`
  corrects the previously included multiwallet script's executable mode.
- **Verification:** The selected 12-test `--require-tests --jobs=2` gate
  passes in 14 seconds locally. The complete 19-test workflow selection
  also passes. Before the second mode correction, that complete selection
  failed closed with `PermissionError` on `wallet_asset_multiwallet.py`.
  An unknown required test exits nonzero; the workflow YAML parses.
  GitHub exact-SHA execution remains pending.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub gate remains pending.

### FINDING-126: Inherited Python escape warnings fail the required asset test

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The mandatory functional gate reports failure
  even though the asset RPC assertions pass, blocking qualification.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The complete selected gate runs 11 of 12
  tests successfully, then flags `feature_restricted_assets.py` because
  it writes four `SyntaxWarning` messages to stderr.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Required security tests must execute and
  report their actual assertions, without false failures or skipped cases.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The four string literals are inherited
  unchanged from official Core 4.8.0 at `b60f50`.
- **Root cause:** Four Python string literals contain an unrecognized
  `\/` escape. Python 3.12 emits `SyntaxWarning`; the functional runner
  correctly treats unexpected stderr output as a failed test.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_restricted_assets.py:47,145,173-174`, invalid
  asset-name RPC vectors; `test/functional/test_runner.py`, stderr gate.
- **Expected behavior:** Keep exactly the same invalid asset-name bytes
  and the same RPC assertions, but express the literals without a warning.
- **Proposed remediation:** Prefix only these four literals with `r`.
  This preserves the backslash-plus-slash payload exactly.
- **Regression required:** Run the same selected `--require-tests` gate;
  confirm the asset test executes with no stderr and all 12 tests pass.
- **Remediation commit:** `c18d4b582` prefixes only the four literals
  with `r`, preserving their exact value and all RPC assertions.
- **Verification:** The same selected 12-test gate passes with no stderr
  from `feature_restricted_assets.py`.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub gate remains pending.

### FINDING-085: Chainstate recovery test observes an unfinished startup rebuild

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The required functional security gate can reject a
  correct rebuild intermittently and block qualification. This is a test
  synchronization defect, not evidence that invalid chainstate is accepted.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `513a3bfc8db61c5539fb0c306508b8308397af85`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The mandatory Core 4.8.0 chainstate-ahead
  regression must test the completed rebuild without silently skipping it.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Automatic rebuild of coins, asset, and
  restricted-asset state when the persisted coins tip is ahead of the index.
  The defect is in the integration regression test, not inherited Core code.
- **Root cause:** `start_node()` waits for one successful `getblockcount()`
  RPC, while `ThreadImport` subsequently runs `ActivateBestChain` on a
  background thread. The test immediately compares the tip to the old index
  checkpoint even though the rebuild can still be connecting blocks.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_chainstate_ahead.py:63-75`, `run_test`;
  `test/functional/test_framework/test_node.py:94-107`,
  `wait_for_rpc_connection`; `src/init.cpp:1935,2027`, startup order.
- **Introduced by:** `22845c314c046a53800505a82960bce7d46b1cdf`.
- **Concrete scenario:** GitHub Final Gate run `36459106698` at
  `9d8b89a2d8a2fe1c3b26e6d1e861e3776b6c2c55` sees the recovery log but
  reads a noncheckpoint tip at line 71. A separate GitHub release security
  job on the same SHA passes, as do 12 repeated local three-test gate runs.
  The discrepancy is consistent with observing an intermediate startup tip.
- **Expected behavior:** Wait for the exact checkpoint tip within a bounded
  startup interval, then retain exact height and asset-state assertions. A
  persistently wrong tip or stale asset DB must still fail.
- **Proposed remediation:** Use the functional framework's bounded wait for
  the expected tip before the existing exact assertions. Do not relax the
  assertions or skip the test.
- **Regression required:** The original GitHub failure is red evidence;
  rerun the exact functional gate locally and in both GitHub workflows on
  the corrected SHA. Confirm the test still fails for a permanently wrong
  tip or asset state.
- **Remediation commit:** `cf4aea1d2`.
- **Verification:** The exact three-test functional gate passes locally.
  Twelve prior repeated runs passed, explaining why local-only validation
  missed the race. The bounded framework wait raises for a permanently false
  predicate; exact tip, height, and asset-state assertions remain unchanged.
  Both GitHub workflows still need a corrected-SHA rerun.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub exact-SHA rerun pending.

### FINDING-086: Legacy fontconfig source host rejects aarch64 release fetch

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The aarch64 release artifact is unavailable, while
  the checksum-pinned dependency build fails closed and does not substitute
  a different source.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `cf4aea1d21bd85e78ccfe7e5ffecb31a714b174f`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Every official release target must build
  from an identical audited commit and checksum-verified dependency set.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The source URL and archive checksum are
  inherited unchanged from the official Core 4.8.0 dependency recipe. The
  observed HTTP rejection is an external availability problem in 2026, not
  evidence that the URL was broken at the 4.8.0 release date.
- **Root cause:** The upstream `www.freedesktop.org` archive endpoint returned
  HTTP 418 to GitHub's aarch64 runner; depends had no functioning fallback
  mirror and attempted an empty URL after that response.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `depends/packages/fontconfig.mk:3-5`,
  source acquisition for the 2.12.1 archive.
- **Concrete scenario:** GitHub Build Raven run `36459106688` at
  `9d8b89a2d8a2fe1c3b26e6d1e861e3776b6c2c55` built earlier dependencies,
  then failed fetching `fontconfig-2.12.1.tar.bz2` with HTTP 418 and no
  release artifact for aarch64. Other targets passed their build steps.
- **Expected behavior:** Obtain the exact 2.12.1 archive reliably and verify
  the existing `b449a3e10c47e1d1c7a6ec6e2016cca73d3bd68fbbd4f0ae5cc6b573f7d6c7f3`
  SHA256 before extraction. No version or contents change is authorized.
- **Proposed remediation:** Use an independently reachable archive mirror
  for the same checksum-pinned tarball. Do not remove or bypass checksum
  verification.
- **Regression required:** Fetch the candidate archive and compare SHA256
  with the official recipe; run the aarch64 GitHub artifact build on the
  corrected SHA and verify its produced archive and embedded source commit.
- **Remediation commit:** `580f408f5`.
- **Verification:** The MIT MacPorts mirror supplied the byte-identical
  `fontconfig-2.12.1.tar.bz2` archive with SHA256
  `b449a3e10c47e1d1c7a6ec6e2016cca73d3bd68fbbd4f0ae5cc6b573f7d6c7f3`.
  The depends recipe keeps the original version, filename, and checksum.
  GitHub aarch64 jobs passed and uploaded an unsigned artifact in Build Raven
  run `36518540863` at `5beba01c6b5b513b1a59a915e96cb6e65a5a3172`.
  The ultimate release SHA and artifact contents still require verification.
- **Final status:** FIXED; final-SHA artifact verification pending.

### FINDING-087: No-wallet release matrix cannot link the mandatory PQ derivation KAT

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Three supported no-wallet cross-builds fail, blocking
  exact-SHA qualification. The defect does not bypass consensus validation.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d6bfe2608defff885a67890927443ca74cfa60c5`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The deterministic PQ derivation and full-chain
  KAT must execute in mandatory test binaries without silently being skipped.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This was introduced by RIP-25 test
  integration, not inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** `test/pqkey_hardening_tests.cpp` is compiled in all builds
  and calls `pqderivation::GetKeypath` and `DeriveSeed`, but
  `wallet/pqderivation.cpp` is linked only through `libraven_wallet.a` when
  wallet support is enabled. The no-wallet test binary has unresolved calls.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/Makefile.test.include:73,119-134`,
  mandatory test source and conditional wallet link;
  `src/Makefile.am:324`, wallet-only derivation object;
  `src/test/pqkey_hardening_tests.cpp`, `rip25_production_full_chain_kat`.
- **Introducing provenance:** Post-checkpoint full-chain KAT/build wiring.
- **Concrete scenario:** Exact-SHA Final Gate run `36515177222` links
  `test/test_raven` in linux-disable-wallet, arm32v7-disable-wallet, and
  aarch64-disable-wallet jobs and fails with undefined references to both
  derivation functions. A wallet-enabled job links successfully.
- **Expected behavior:** The no-wallet test binary includes the small
  derivation implementation for its KAT while production no-wallet targets
  remain free of wallet linkage. Do not skip the test.
- **Proposed remediation:** Compile `wallet/pqderivation.cpp` into
  `test/test_raven` only when wallet support is disabled. Keep the existing
  wallet-library implementation for wallet-enabled builds.
- **Regression required:** Reproduce the no-wallet link failure from the
  GitHub log, then run a clean no-wallet configure/build and execute the
  full-chain KAT. Rerun all no-wallet GitHub matrix targets.
- **Remediation commit:** `08699b85f`.
- **Verification:** Clean out-of-tree no-wallet configure/build succeeded.
  The mandatory `rip25_production_full_chain_kat` executed 79 assertions
  successfully in the no-wallet binary; its complete `make check -j4`
  passed. All three no-wallet cross-target jobs passed in Final Gate run
  `36518540883` at `5beba01c6b5b513b1a59a915e96cb6e65a5a3172`.
- **Final status:** FIXED; final-SHA matrix confirmation pending.

### FINDING-088: Native release matrix test failure is hidden by the CI log

- **Severity:** MEDIUM pending root-cause classification. Exact-SHA
  qualification cannot pass or identify the failing case from the job log.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d6bfe2608defff885a67890927443ca74cfa60c5`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Mandatory test execution must produce an
  auditable pass or actionable failure on the release matrix.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Unknown until the failed test case is recovered.
- **Root cause:** The release matrix executes plain `make check`. Automake
  redirects test-case detail into `src/test-suite.log` on failure, and the
  workflow neither prints nor uploads that file.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `.github/workflows/rip25-v48-final-gate.yml:222-226`, native test step.
- **Introducing provenance:** Integration final-gate workflow.
- **Concrete scenario:** Exact-SHA Final Gate run `36515177222` passes the
  required security-tests job, then its wallet-enabled Linux build compiles
  and reports `FAIL: test/test_raven` under `make check`. The live job log
  shows only the Automake summary and says to inspect `src/test-suite.log`,
  which is not exposed by the workflow. The failing test is not yet known.
- **Expected behavior:** Keep `make check` mandatory and failing, but display
  its test log on failure so the root cause can be identified and corrected.
- **Proposed remediation:** On `make check` failure, print bounded test logs
  and exit nonzero. Classify and separately remediate the underlying failed
  test after obtaining the evidence.
- **Regression required:** Verify the corrected workflow still fails for a
  failing `make check`, exposes test-case details, and rerun the exact-SHA
  native matrix. Do not mark the finding fixed solely from adding diagnostics.
- **Remediation commit:** `be3fd9e10` for log visibility only.
- **Verification:** The YAML parses, and a simulated failed `make check`
  exits nonzero while displaying the test log. Both Linux jobs passed in
  Final Gate run `36518540883`; the earlier underlying test failure was not
  reproduced, so its specific cause remains unclassified. The diagnostic
  correction remains in place for subsequent runs.
- **Final status:** MITIGATED; transient underlying failure under observation.

### FINDING-089: macOS packaging loses the pinned Python path inside make

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The macOS release artifact cannot be produced, but
  consensus code and the dependency checksums are unchanged.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `5beba01c6b5b513b1a59a915e96cb6e65a5a3172`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Every supported release artifact must be
  built from the exact audited source with pinned dependency provenance.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The Makefile environment precedence is
  inherited from Core 4.8.0; the incorrect package-script assumption was
  introduced in this integration's CI workflow.
- **Root cause:** The package script exports the correct pinned two-directory
  `PYTHONPATH` and directly imports `ds_store`, `mac_alias`, and `biplist`
  successfully. The generated Makefile also assigns `PYTHONPATH` from
  configure and exports it to recipe subprocesses, overriding the shell
  environment when `make deploydir` runs. Its value omits the pinned
  `python3/dist-packages` directory containing `ds_store`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `.github/scripts/06-package.sh:101-123`,
  macOS package branch; `Makefile.am:14`, exported configure variable;
  generated `Makefile.in`, `PYTHONPATH = @PYTHONPATH@`.
- **Introducing provenance:** Integration macOS package-script path setup.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `36515178744` at
  `d6bfe2608defff885a67890927443ca74cfa60c5` passes the pinned-module
  preflight and compiles the app, then `make deploydir` executes
  `custom_dsstore.py` with the configured path and raises
  `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ds_store'`.
- **Expected behavior:** Both `make deploydir` and `make deploy` propagate
  the exact preflight-checked pinned `PYTHONPATH` into recipe subprocesses.
- **Proposed remediation:** Pass `PYTHONPATH` as a command-line make variable
  for these package targets; leave dependency acquisition and source intact.
- **Regression required:** Demonstrate an exported shell `PYTHONPATH` loses
  to the configured Makefile assignment, demonstrate command-line override
  wins, and rerun the complete macOS artifact job on the corrected SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `87b38a86e`.
- **Verification:** Direct make evaluation proves an exported environment
  `PYTHONPATH` loses to the configured Makefile value, while a command-line
  make assignment wins. The macOS package script now uses that assignment
  for both affected targets. `bash -n` and diff whitespace checks pass.
  Build Raven run `37852896085` passed its macOS build and packaging job
  `113576631280` and uploaded artifact `11585626159`, named
  `raven-unsigned-osx-72a68f0f898ec794f3bdc38d0621124e2f88b54c`.
  The job log reports packaged source commit
  `72a68f0f898ec794f3bdc38d0621124e2f88b54c` and artifact zip digest
  `2704829474edb114b249899c945b6649aa35452233a385f3988e9295d3ea032b`.
  This was the pull-request merge SHA. Build Raven run `37928136449`
  subsequently succeeded on `870a0bee36f4ec4b4c52f1c0e238df83834025e2`
  with all five release targets, including the macOS artifact named
  `raven-unsigned-osx-870a0bee36f4ec4b4c52f1c0e238df83834025e2`.
  Paired Final Gate run `37928133299` also succeeded at that exact SHA.
  Build Raven `38296258242` subsequently built the macOS artifact
  `11771367693` on the PQ-only candidate SHA `9151946ad`; paired Final
  Gate `38296262444` succeeded at that exact SHA. The eventual 4.8.1
  release SHA still requires artifact qualification.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the PQ-only candidate SHA `9151946ad`;
  final release-SHA artifact verification remains due.

## Findings frozen after the merged asset-phase CI review

### FINDING-149: Transfer crash-recovery test checks the tip before peer synchronization

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The mandatory Final Gate fails and blocks release
  qualification. The available evidence does not show a consensus bypass.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at merged commit
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The release gate must verify that a node
  recovers the asset transfer state after an interrupted coins flush.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The coins and asset database durability repair
  addresses an inherited Core 4.8.0 defect. This test timing problem was
  introduced by the later integration test in `ecd4ca077`.
- **Observed failure:** Final Gate runs `37852895999` at
  `1e1c4533f0b2d70ed4a8f326cd30bbdaf6a5b498` and `37852951929` at
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd` both fail at
  `feature_asset_dbcrash_transfer.py:66`: the restarted source node reports a
  different tip immediately after RPC startup. The preceding asset database
  recovery log assertions pass. The other required functional tests pass.
- **Likely root cause:** The test restarts the source node and reads its tip
  before it has synchronized the transfer block from its still-running peer.
  Related crash-recovery tests use a bounded wait for the expected tip.
  This hypothesis needs a green rerun; a timeout would instead expose a
  remaining recovery defect.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_asset_dbcrash_transfer.py:59-75`, recovery check.
- **Expected behavior:** Wait a bounded time for the exact transfer block,
  then retain the UTXO and asset balance comparisons. A missing block must
  still fail the test and the gate.
- **Regression required:** Treat both failed Final Gate runs as the red
  regression. Rerun the transfer recovery test after the bounded wait and
  confirm the complete Final Gate passes at the same source SHA as the
  cross-platform Build Raven artifacts. Keep `make check` mandatory.
- **Remediation commit:** `8d4e86d4b` adds a bounded wait for the exact tip.
- **Verification:** The focused crash-recovery test passed locally. Final
  Gate run `37888456288` at
  `b8bc089f3e3d2b395279e23ec5fdd8e1a9036633` passed its required
  functional tests, mandatory `make check`, fuzz smoke, invariants and all
  seven matrix builds. The paired Build Raven run had a separate macOS runner
  failure recorded as FINDING-156. Final release-SHA rerun remains due.
- **Final status:** FIXED; final release-SHA qualification pending.

### FINDING-150: Ordinary RVN coin selection can consume a protected asset anchor

- **Severity:** MEDIUM operational availability. Consensus still rejects an
  asset spend without a matching PQ input.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN during the merged asset-phase review at
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The wallet should preserve or refresh the
  native PQ anchor needed to spend a protected asset later.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None; native PQ anchors are part of this asset
  phase.
- **Root cause:** Generic native RVN coin selection includes funded PQ anchor
  outputs among available non-asset UTXOs, even when the transaction spends
  no asset. Asset-specific refresh logic is not entered in that path.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:2971-2975,
  3178-3186,4101-4104,4234`, native coin selection.
- **Concrete scenario:** An ordinary payment may consume the sole anchor
  for a protected asset and return change to a different destination.
  The asset and keys remain in the wallet, but a later protected spend needs
  another RVN payment to the original PQ witness-v2 address.
- **Expected behavior:** Preserve a last required anchor during generic
  spending or atomically recreate a funded anchor for the same program.
  Avoid silently overriding a user-selected change destination.
- **Regression required:** Fund one protected asset and its sole anchor,
  issue an ordinary RVN payment that selects the anchor, verify the later
  asset spend fails for lack of a matching funded anchor, then top up that
  PQ address and verify spending recovers. Confirm no consensus bypass.
- **Remediation commit:** None; automatic last-anchor reservation remains a
  wallet design task. `193d26483` documents manual recovery.
- **Verification:** `3424ddc5e` forces ordinary RVN coin selection to
  consume the sole matching anchor. The asset balance remains in the wallet,
  a subsequent protected spend fails, and a matching PQ top-up restores it.
  The focused functional test passed locally.
- **Final status:** DOCUMENTED LIMITATION for 4.8.1. The anchor-consumption
  and matching top-up vector passed in both required security jobs at
  `9151946ad`; final release-SHA qualification remains due.

### FINDING-151: Raw transaction funding does not select asset PQ anchors

- **Severity:** MEDIUM interoperability and availability. Consensus rejects
  the unanchored protected asset transaction.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN during the merged asset-phase review at
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Each protected asset input needs a funded
  native PQ input for the same program.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None; Core 4.8.0 has no asset PQ anchor rule.
- **Root cause:** `fundrawtransaction` funds native RVN through generic coin
  selection and does not derive required PQ programs from raw asset inputs.
  Its native change also does not automatically refresh a supplied anchor.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:3869-3918,
  3999-4016,4100-4104,4250-4309`, raw funding and coin selection.
- **Concrete scenario:** A raw protected asset transfer is funded and signed
  without a matching anchor. Mempool and block checks reject it. The caller
  can supply a funded matching anchor explicitly and return enough RVN to
  that same PQ address for future spends.
- **Expected behavior:** Either derive and fund matching PQ anchors with
  accurate fee accounting, or clearly document the manual raw transaction
  construction sequence. Merely marking anchors as selected is insufficient:
  the raw append loop omits selected inputs.
- **Regression required:** Demonstrate rejection of a funded raw protected
  transfer lacking the matching anchor, then acceptance after adding the
  anchor explicitly and preserving its PQ destination for change.
- **Remediation commit:** None; automatic raw anchor selection remains a
  wallet design task. `193d26483` documents the explicit construction path.
- **Verification:** `3424ddc5e` proves that generic raw funding omits the
  matching anchor and consensus rejects the signed transaction. Supplying
  the anchor and an RVN return output to its PQ address succeeds, and a
  later protected spend remains possible. The focused test passed locally.
- **Final status:** DOCUMENTED LIMITATION for 4.8.1. The raw-funding
  rejection and explicit-anchor recovery vector passed in both required
  security jobs at `9151946ad`; final release-SHA qualification remains due.

## Findings frozen by the independent merged asset-phase review

### FINDING-152: Default asset issuance RPCs choose an invalid destination after activation

- **Severity:** MEDIUM operational availability. The transaction constructor
  rejects the classical-only default; consensus is not bypassed.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at merged commit
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** New asset and owner-token outputs after bit 13
  activation must carry a canonical PQ destination.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None; the classical defaults predate bit 13.
- **Root cause:** `issue`, `issueunique`, and `issuequalifierasset` fill an
  omitted address with a fresh P2PKH address, while active issuance requires
  a `classical|PQ` descriptor.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/rpc/assets.cpp:510-526,675-691,
  2483-2499`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:4266-4275`.
- **Concrete scenario:** An ordinary RPC call without an explicit address
  fails after bit 13 activation, including an owner-token issuance path.
- **Expected behavior:** Generate and persist a wallet-owned PQ asset
  descriptor by default when the rule is active. Preserve classical
  defaults before activation.
- **Regression required:** Exercise all three default-address RPCs before
  and after activation. Confirm their new asset and owner-token outputs are
  tagged, wallet-owned, and spendable with a matching funded anchor.
- **Remediation commit:** `7a2502499` generates and persists a wallet-owned
  composite destination for active default issuance calls.
- **Verification:** The new functional assertions failed before the code
  change for all three RPCs with the canonical-destination error. After the
  change, `feature_pq_asset_wallet_ownership.py` passes locally and checks
  preactivation classical behavior and postactivation owned PQ pairs.
  Both required security jobs passed this functional test at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-153: Asset index RPC aggregates distinct PQ programs by classical address

- **Severity:** MEDIUM interoperability and custody interpretation. No
  consensus bypass or loss of asset keys is shown.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at merged commit
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The earlier dual-signature candidate
  identified an active asset recipient by a classical address and PQ program
  together. The current PQ-only candidate derives a distinct synthetic
  identifier from each PQ program.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None; the index predates bit 13.
- **Root cause:** The earlier candidate let `listaddressesbyasset` aggregate
  outputs under one classical-address index row even when their PQ programs
  differed. That output format has been superseded for new protected assets.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/rpc/assets.cpp:787-791,1138-1157`;
  `src/coins.cpp:291-302`.
- **Concrete scenario:** In the earlier candidate, two protected descriptors
  shared a classical key and the RPC reported one aggregate row. In the
  current PQ-only format their program-derived identifiers are distinct.
  Historical classical outputs retain their original index semantics.
- **Expected behavior:** Expose descriptor-aware outpoint information, or
  explicitly label this legacy aggregate as non-destination data.
- **Regression required:** Retain the earlier aggregate vector as historical
  evidence. Verify that current PQ-only outputs under different programs
  have distinct identifiers and that a mismatched identifier/program pair
  is rejected.
- **Remediation commit:** `193d26483` documented the earlier aggregate
  semantics. `0b6d74bd8` updates the asset design note for the PQ-only
  candidate. The format redesign is tracked by FINDING-158.
- **Verification:** `588ef7ec8` exercises the superseded dual-signature
  format and its fail-safe recipient check. The current source RPC functional
  vector asserts distinct synthetic identifiers for two PQ programs and
  rejects an identifier/program mismatch. Both required security jobs
  passed the current source RPC test at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** SCOPE SUPERSEDED for new PQ-only outputs. Historical
  classical index rows remain descriptive data, not proof of PQ custody.

### FINDING-154: Qt asset control displays and copies a classical-only address

- **Severity:** MEDIUM usability and destination integrity. Sending to the
  copied address fails for active protected assets.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at merged commit
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet UI must distinguish protected
  destinations with different PQ programs.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None; asset coin control predates bit 13.
- **Root cause:** `WalletModel::listAssets` and the coin-control table group
  by the extracted classical destination; Copy address uses that same field.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/qt/walletmodel.cpp:737-756`;
  `src/qt/assetcontroldialog.cpp:282-288,649-705`.
- **Concrete scenario:** In the earlier dual-signature candidate, two tagged
  outputs could share a classical key and Copy address yielded a
  classical-only string. The PQ-only candidate instead uses distinct
  program-derived identifiers; the list, tree and clipboard required
  interactive qualification on an unmodified release artifact.
- **Expected behavior:** Show the full protected descriptor per active
  output and copy a valid descriptor, without relabeling historical outputs.
- **Regression required:** GUI exercise with two distinct PQ programs and
  synthetic identifiers, including list and tree labels and clipboard
  content. Build the Qt executable against depends Qt 5.12.11.
- **Remediation commit:** `879a818c6` groups coin-control outputs by their
  contextual descriptor and copies the complete protected destination.
- **Verification:** The existing contextual formatter unit test covers
  historical and active output heights. At 870a0bee3, the Linux
  x86_64-pc-linux-gnu depends prefix reports Qt 5.12.11 in
  Qt5Core.pc; make -j4, make check, and raven-qt -version pass with
  that prefix. The local GCC 13 build needed -Wno-error=array-bounds
  while compiling the pinned libxkbcommon dependency; the Qt recipe
  and version were not changed. A later local Xvfb session with that
  pinned Qt version showed two PQ-only asset outpoints under distinct
  descriptors in list and tree views; Copy address returned each full
  `classical|PQ` descriptor. Screenshots and copied strings are retained
  with the private audit evidence. The local GCC 13 executable required
  a process-local workaround for a Qt 5.12.11 `readlink` fortify crash;
  the workaround is not part of the repository or release artifact.
  Build Raven `38296258242` produced Linux artifact `11770728793` at
  `9151946ad`, alongside a green Final Gate `38296262444`. The archive's
  published SHA-256 file and full-SHA provenance verified locally. Its
  unmodified `raven-qt` opened a copied regtest wallet under Xvfb without
  the workaround. A private fixture issued two units of `PQGUIQA915` to
  one PQ-only descriptor, then transferred one unit to a second descriptor.
  Asset Control list mode displayed two separate rows; tree mode displayed
  two separate groups. Copy address on both rows and on the second tree
  group returned the complete corresponding descriptor byte-for-byte.
  Screenshots, the fixture, and exact clipboard comparisons are retained
  outside the repository with the private audit evidence.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified PQ-only candidate SHA;
  final release-SHA artifact verification remains due.

### FINDING-155: Partial migration of a legacy asset UTXO needs explicit PQ change

- **Severity:** MEDIUM migration availability. The wallet fails safely;
  whole-UTXO migration remains possible.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at merged commit
  `7dc44845309ae36e33a55d3122e62603fcaed9dd`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** New asset change after activation must be
  protected even when its input was a historical classical asset UTXO.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None; legacy asset outputs predate bit 13.
- **Root cause:** The wallet can infer protected change from an existing
  tagged source, but a historical input has no tag. The transfer RPC accepts
  an explicit asset change descriptor; the GUI does not expose that field.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:4334-4387`;
  `src/rpc/assets.cpp:1252-1285`; `src/qt/assetsdialog.cpp:897-955`.
- **Concrete scenario:** After activation, sending part of an old asset
  holding in the GUI fails when it would create unprotected asset change.
- **Expected behavior:** Migrate an entire legacy UTXO to a protected
  descriptor, or supply an explicit protected change descriptor through RPC.
  Neither path may silently create classical-only asset change.
- **Regression required:** Show the partial GUI-equivalent path fails
  without change, then show whole-UTXO migration and RPC partial migration
  succeed with canonical tags. Confirm later spends require a funded anchor.
- **Remediation commits:** `193d26483` documents whole-UTXO migration and the
  explicit asset-change RPC path; `12200477b` adds a protected change field
  and wallet-owned PQ-only descriptor chooser to the transfer dialog.
- **Verification:** `588ef7ec8` proves that a partial legacy spend without
  protected change fails, while whole-UTXO asset and owner-token migration
  and explicit PQ change succeed. A later protected spend needs and accepts
  a funded matching anchor. The focused source RPC test passed locally and
  in both required security jobs at `9151946ad`.
- **Further verification:** The focused pinned Qt 5.12.11 regression failed
  before the GUI field existed and passed after it was added. It checks that
  empty or invalid entries leave no classical asset-change destination in
  coin control. The dialog accepts a canonical descriptor only if it belongs
  to the selected wallet. The pinned Qt GUI binary builds and `make check -j4`
  passes. The upstream Qt test runner still exits before its test cases;
  the focused test used a temporary local runner and host Qt compatibility
  shim. The controlled Linux GUI transfer below supplements this focused
  test. Native Windows/macOS GUI exercise follows the owner's later
  community-testing disposition under FINDING-067.
- **Controlled GUI verification:** The pinned Linux `raven-qt` from the
  `07016ff16` release package completed an isolated regtest transfer with
  bit 13 active. The GUI spent a historical 3-unit asset output and sent
  1 unit to a PQ-tagged recipient with 2 units of PQ-tagged asset change.
  Transaction `5b606df5237d6a8fc43650767c2e91324adf04a1fe32339457efe52b740ab5cb`
  was confirmed at height 864. Both decoded asset outputs matched the
  selected PQ programs, the wallet retained 3 total units, and the owner
  token remained unchanged. Screenshots, binary hashes, RPC assertions,
  and execution notes are in the private report's
  `evidence/f155-gui-07016ff16/` directory. Both destinations belonged
  to the test wallet. This did not exercise an owner-token GUI send or
  native Windows/macOS GUI behavior.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the tested GUI transfer path. Final release-SHA
  CI and operator qualification remain under FINDING-067. Native Windows
  and macOS GUI exercise follows the owner's community-testing disposition.

### FINDING-156: macOS release runner disconnects during the artifact build

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability. The failed job produced no
  macOS artifact at the tested SHA; no code or consensus defect is established.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN after Build Raven run `37888464222` at
  `b8bc089f3e3d2b395279e23ec5fdd8e1a9036633`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** All release artifacts must be produced and
  checked from the same SHA as a green Final Gate.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None known; the cause of runner loss is not yet
  classified as product code or CI infrastructure.
- **Observed failure:** Security tests and the Linux, aarch64, arm32v7 and
  Windows artifact jobs passed. The macOS job `113688195436` built pinned
  dependencies and configured successfully, then its hosted runner lost
  communication with GitHub during `make -j2`. GitHub left that step without
  a conclusion, skipped packaging and emitted no macOS artifact. Its available
  log ends before the build output, so it does not identify a compiler error.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml:216-240`,
  dependency and product build steps; no defective source line is established.
- **Expected behavior:** The macOS job must complete its pinned Qt build,
  package an artifact and verify the embedded source SHA. A runner loss must
  remain a failed qualification, not be treated as a code pass.
- **Regression required:** Rerun the failed macOS job at the exact same SHA.
  If runner loss recurs, inspect resource use and reduce parallel build load
  or otherwise correct the CI environment, then rerun. Final qualification
  must repeat at the eventual release SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** None. The failure was a hosted-runner disconnect,
  with no demonstrated product-code defect.
- **Verification:** Build Raven run `37888464222`, attempt 2, succeeded on
  the unchanged SHA `b8bc089f3e3d2b395279e23ec5fdd8e1a9036633`.
  Its macOS job completed and uploaded the artifact named
  `raven-unsigned-osx-b8bc089f3e3d2b395279e23ec5fdd8e1a9036633`.
  The other four release targets and required security job also succeeded.
  Paired Final Gate run `37888456288` succeeded at the same SHA. A newer
  exact-SHA pair, Build Raven `37928136449` and Final Gate `37928133299`,
  also succeeded at `870a0bee36f4ec4b4c52f1c0e238df83834025e2`, with
  five artifacts. Build Raven `38296258242` also produced all five on
  `9151946ad` with paired green Final Gate `38296262444`. These are
  candidate builds, not the final release SHA.
- **Final status:** RESOLVED as a transient CI runner loss at the tested
  SHAs; final release-SHA artifact qualification remains pending.

### FINDING-157: Historical asset spends remain classical after bit 13

- **Severity:** HIGH for the newly requested legacy-signature sunset.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-09 at e0893a6b6, before any
  sunset design or consensus change.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** After a separately defined sunset point,
  no historical asset or owner-token UTXO may be spendable with a
  classical signature alone.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. Core 4.8.0 had no PQ asset rule.
- **Root cause:** The bit-13 input rule requires a matching native PQ
  anchor only for asset coins created at or after the effective bit-13
  activation height. Historical asset scripts commit to a classical
  P2PKH key but contain no PQ program. The current migration path spends
  them classically to create a tagged output.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:626-676,
  especially the origin-height gate at 661-667;
  src/assets/assets.cpp:3632-3707, canonical program extraction;
  src/wallet/wallet.cpp:2998-3007,4257-4267, historical-input selection.
- **Concrete scenario:** A historical asset or ASSET! token remains
  spendable by its classical key after bit 13 activates. A later blanket
  ban would make every unmigrated holding and its owner authority
  unspendable unless a separate authenticated recovery rule exists.
  Adding an arbitrary PQ anchor at spend time would not prove ownership
  because an attacker could supply an anchor under its own PQ key.
- **Expected behavior:** Choose and specify a sunset deployment and an
  explicit fate for unmigrated asset and owner-token UTXOs. If recovery
  remains possible after sunset, its PQ authorization must be bound to
  the historical owner before the classical-only path closes.
- **Regression required:** Prove ordinary and owner-token migration
  before sunset; reject a classical-only historical spend after sunset
  in mempool and blocks; cover the activation boundary, reorgs, restart,
  assumevalid, and any authorized recovery path. Wallet and GUI balances
  must not imply that frozen UTXOs are spendable.
- **Remediation commit:** None. The owner superseded the sunset request;
  no sunset consensus change is planned for 4.8.1.
- **Verification:** The existing unit vector at
  src/test/assets/asset_tx_tests.cpp:839-872 proves the current
  origin-height exemption. The functional migration vector at
  test/functional/feature_pq_asset_source_rpc.py:71-93 proves that
  asset and owner-token migration currently depend on a classical
  historical spend. No sunset regression exists yet.
- **Final status:** SCOPE SUPERSEDED on 2026-10-09. The owner withdrew the
  requested sunset and chose voluntary migration, as for native RVN.
  Historical classical spending remains a documented property, not a fix.

### FINDING-158: New protected asset outputs still require a classical signature

- **Severity:** HIGH release requirement mismatch. The existing dual-key
  rule still requires ML-DSA, but it does not provide PQ-only ownership.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-09 at 73dfc7f8a, before any
  redesign of asset scripts, consensus, wallet, RPCs, or Qt.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** After bit 13, new asset and owner-token
  outputs must be spendable with ML-DSA alone. Historical outputs retain
  their classical spending rule until their owners migrate them.
- **Owner-approved authorization model:** A matching native witness-v2 RVN
  input may carry the ML-DSA signature for the asset spend. The asset input
  itself must require no ECDSA signature.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. Its asset ownership format was P2PKH.
- **Root cause:** The candidate asset envelope retains a P2PKH ownership
  prefix and appends a 32-byte PQ program. Its input rule additionally
  requires a matching native witness-v2 anchor, but the asset script itself
  still executes ECDSA. The wallet similarly requires both keys.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/script/script.cpp:245-281,
  src/script/standard.cpp:75-83, src/script/sign.cpp:90-123,
  src/assets/assets.cpp:3632-3707, src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:626-676,
  src/wallet/wallet.cpp:1941-1951,2998-3007.
- **Concrete scenario:** A holder of a new tagged asset or ASSET! output
  cannot spend it with the ML-DSA key alone. Loss of its classical key
  prevents spending even when the PQ key is available. This contradicts
  the confirmed 4.8.1 ownership rule; it is not evidence of an ML-DSA
  bypass in the present dual-key rule.
- **Expected behavior:** Specify and implement a consensus-safe PQ-only
  asset output and spend format for all asset classes and owner authority.
  Preserve the authenticated classical spend of historical outputs for
  voluntary migration, without a sunset. Prove old-node validity and
  activation behavior before selecting a deployment path.
- **Regression required:** Before the fix, demonstrate that a new asset
  and owner-token output cannot be spent with ML-DSA alone. After the fix,
  prove ML-DSA-only spending and rejection of missing or mismatched PQ
  signatures in mempool and blocks. Cover historical migration, partial
  change, issuance, reissuance, restricted and qualifier administration,
  raw RPCs, wallet backup and recovery, Qt, reorgs, and exact-SHA CI.
- **Remediation commits:** `198b93029` introduced the parser and script
  format; `802bf2433` added the PQ-only consensus and signing rules;
  `5f34f8cbf` added wallet custody, recovery and contextual ownership;
  `b7153328d` switched asset RPC and Qt construction to PQ-only outputs.
- **Verification:** The previous dual-signature consensus check and asset
  signing path establish the pre-fix behavior. The
  `pq_only_asset_prefix_is_legacy_valid_and_program_bound_test` was red
  before `198b93029` because the parser rejected the proposed prefix. It
  now passes with an empty scriptSig and a program-bound identifier.
  `pq_asset_creation_height_and_anchor_rule_test` covers new-output and
  anchored-input rejection. The asset anchor, source RPC, wallet ownership
  and authority functional tests pass at `6ed46c91c`; local `make check`
  passes. Both required security jobs passed the PQ-only unit and functional
  vectors at `9151946ad`. In a two-binary regtest, Core 4.8.0
  `b60f50e04` synchronized the same 932-block chain as the candidate
  artifact `9151946ad` while bit 13 was active on the candidate. Blocks
  864 through 932 contained 66 PQ-only asset outputs and 35 later spends.
  The old node accepted both the example output in block 864 and its
  scriptSig-empty anchored spend in block 865; both nodes reached the
  same tip `3ec9956751d3d8e43fb3995bb96df5929a678155f03c0124d216141d8c65cebe`.
  The ordinary-sized example spend block weighed 5,602 WU on both nodes.
  This establishes old-node acceptance of the asset format, not old-node
  enforcement of ML-DSA. Public activation remains blocked by the
  incompatible native block rules in FINDING-160 and final release-SHA
  qualification.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the PQ-only implementation on the qualified
  candidate SHA; public activation and release remain blocked by
  FINDING-160 and the remaining deployment qualification.

### FINDING-159: Proposed PQ-only asset input permits scriptSig malleability

- **Severity:** MEDIUM transaction integrity and relay consistency.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-09 at 17053c0bc, before an
  input scriptSig rule was added to the PQ-only consensus candidate.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A PQ-only asset spend has one canonical
  empty asset scriptSig; its ML-DSA signature resides in a matching native
  witness-v2 input.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This is a risk introduced by the new
  PQ-only prefix under consideration for 4.8.1.
- **Root cause:** The proposed prefix leaves one true stack item when the
  asset scriptSig is empty. Consensus block script flags do not include
  CLEANSTACK, so a pushed extra true value could leave a nonempty stack
  and still pass script evaluation. The input rule currently checks the
  matching PQ anchor but not that the asset scriptSig is empty.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/validation.cpp:2550-2583,
  `GetBlockScriptFlags`; src/script/script.cpp:245-281, asset marker;
  src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:626-676, `CheckTxPQAssets`.
- **Concrete scenario:** A third party can add a push of true to a proposed
  PQ-only asset input scriptSig, changing its txid without changing its
  authenticated asset movement or the anchor signature. Standard mempool
  policy may reject it, but a block rule must reject it independently.
- **Expected behavior:** At active bit 13, every PQ-only asset input must
  have an empty scriptSig in mempool and blocks. Historical classical asset
  inputs retain their previous scriptSig rules.
- **Regression required:** Show an otherwise valid anchored PQ-only spend
  with a nonempty asset scriptSig is accepted before the new check, then
  rejected with a specific reason afterward. Empty scriptSig with the
  matching anchor and historical classical inputs remain valid.
- **Remediation commit:** `802bf2433` rejects nonempty scriptSig for a
  protected asset input after bit 13 while retaining historical input rules.
- **Verification:** The unit vector at
  src/test/assets/asset_tx_tests.cpp:995-1003 failed when the new check was
  temporarily removed: the malleable spend was accepted and had no reject
  reason. Restoring the check made the same vector pass. The full local
  `make check` also passed at `6ed46c91c`. Both required security jobs
  passed at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-160: Native RIP-25 block limits can split Core 4.8.0 nodes

- **Severity:** HIGH consensus compatibility and release blocker.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-09 at `2e07c1a14`, before a
  compatibility remedy or a revised deployment plan.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A deployment described as a soft fork must
  not make a block valid under the new rules when Core 4.8.0 rejects it.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. Core 4.8.0 enforces the RIP-2 8 MWU
  block limit; the candidate changes that limit after bit 12.
- **Root cause:** The candidate allows 12 MWU immediately after native
  bit-12 activation and 16 MWU after one nominal year. It also applies a
  contextual PQ witness discount. Core 4.8.0 retains the 8 MWU rule and
  its original weight accounting. The original RIP-25 proposal explicitly
  calls for 12 MWU at PQ activation and 16 MWU one year later, but also
  describes the deployment as a soft fork. The block expansion and that
  compatibility description cannot both hold against Core 4.8.0.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/consensus/consensus.h:22-34,
  limits; src/validation.cpp:2448-2474, contextual limits;
  src/validation.cpp:4494-4507, block checks; doc/RIP-0025-PQ-Signatures.md:19,492,
  soft-fork claim. Core 4.8.0 baseline is
  `b60f50e04f1fba425b28804e61be2694faaf3469`.
- **Concrete scenario:** After bit 12, an upgraded miner may produce a
  block whose old-accounted weight exceeds 8 MWU but whose candidate
  accounting is within the new 12 MWU limit. An upgraded node can accept
  it while a Core 4.8.0 node rejects it.
- **Owner decision:** Preserve the original 12 MWU limit at bit-12
  activation and 16 MWU one year later. This requires an explicit
  incompatible-rule deployment plan and release documentation before
  public activation; miner signaling alone does not upgrade old nodes.
  On 2026-10-13 the owner approved the documented plan: distribute 4.8.1
  before STARTED, identify Core 4.8.0 incompatibility, require operators
  to upgrade before ACTIVE, apply 12 MWU and PQ discount at ACTIVE and
  16 MWU after one nominal year, and retain testnet-history and 8 MWU
  boundary proofs as pre-activation gates. This is not approval to tag or
  publish the release.
- **Expected behavior:** Prove old-node acceptance of every post-activation
  block allowed by the candidate, or explicitly choose and approve a
  deployment that accounts for the incompatible block rules. Correct the
  protocol and release documentation before public activation.
- **Regression required:** Run old Core 4.8.0 and candidate nodes against
  blocks on both sides of the 8 MWU boundary, including PQ witness data
  whose discounted and old-accounted weights differ. Verify block and
  chain outcomes, not only isolated weight functions.
- **Remediation commits:** `73cbc93fd` documents the public upgrade plan;
  `3c3bf8385` removes residual claims that BIP9 signaling alone makes
  the combined deployment compatible with Core 4.8.0; `948b1ea20`
  records the chosen mainnet window in protocol and operator release
  notes. No consensus limit was changed by these documentation commits.
- **Verification:** Static comparison of the baseline 8 MWU constant and
  candidate contextual 12/16 MWU checks establishes the incompatible
  acceptance ranges. In a local two-binary regtest at height 540 after
  candidate bit-12 activation, both Core 4.8.0 `b60f50e04` and the
  candidate `90a16eff8` accepted a 7,600,636-WU block. Core 4.8.0
  rejected the next 8,801,320-WU block with `bad-blk-length`, while the
  candidate accepted it and advanced one block. The two transactions
  in the larger block were each below 8 MWU. The baseline was built
  from unchanged source with a compiler-only GCC 13 header workaround.
  A second two-binary test spent a valid ML-DSA witness-v2 output in a
  block weighing 8,000,697 WU by the Core 4.8.0 calculation and about
  7,998,831 WU with the candidate's 1,866-WU PQ witness discount. Core
  4.8.0 rejected it with `bad-blk-weight`; the candidate accepted it and
  advanced one block. This proves the discount is independently
  incompatible at the 8 MWU boundary, even without using the 12 MWU
  phase-1 allowance. On 2026-10-13 the two tests were repeated with the
  versioned 4.8.1 binary from `55be9a0f2`, SHA-256
  `19de029e001adddfa4eb6143cc18c012a6e88d13d5b5dbf234a33be184edd378`.
  Both binaries accepted the 7,600,636-WU control block. Core 4.8.0
  rejected the 8,801,320-WU block with `bad-blk-length` and the
  8,000,697-WU old-accounted PQ witness block with `bad-blk-weight`;
  4.8.1 accepted both. The private logs are
  `evidence/f160-v481-55be-plain.log` and
  `evidence/f160-v481-55be-pq-witness.log`. These are candidate checks;
  the final release SHA must repeat them after the remaining evidence
  updates.
- **Final status:** ACCEPTED as an incompatible consensus upgrade with an
  owner-approved plan. The 4.8.1 release and public bit-12 activation
  remain gated by FINDING-067: testnet history, final-SHA qualification,
  release instructions and checksums, and operator readiness. The
  2027-07-01 to 2029-01-01 mainnet bit-12 timing was implemented in
  `2df4d2d25` after a red-then-green BIP9 parameter regression.

### FINDING-161: Pre-activation PQ-only lookalike output has no PQ custody

- **Severity:** HIGH asset custody ambiguity.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-09 at `2e07c1a14`, before any
  creation rule, wallet classification, or origin-bound regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** An output described as PQ-only must have
  enforceable ML-DSA authorization when spent. The historical exception
  must not silently label an anyone-can-spend lookalike as PQ protected.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The lookalike prefix is introduced by
  the 4.8.1 candidate and remains valid to old script evaluation.
- **Root cause:** Before bit 13, `CheckTxPQAssets` does not apply the new
  anchor rule. After activation, the origin-height exception still exempts
  an output created before activation. The proposed script prefix is
  satisfiable with an empty scriptSig under historical script rules.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:626-676,
  activation and origin checks; src/script/script.cpp:245-281, legacy asset
  parser; src/script/script.h:579-586, asset-tail evaluation; wallet asset
  ownership and address generation paths.
- **Concrete scenario:** A pre-activation transaction creates the new
  prefix and asset payload. The output can later be spent without an
  ML-DSA anchor because its origin predates bit 13. A wallet that labels
  or credits it as a protected PQ-only holding would misstate custody.
- **Expected behavior:** Define and enforce an activation-safe creation
  and classification policy. Historical lookalikes must not appear as
  PQ-protected wallet balance. Preserve valid historical spends without
  trapping funds or silently treating them as authenticated PQ spends.
- **Regression required:** Create a lookalike before activation, spend it
  before and after activation without an anchor, and prove the exact
  consensus outcome and wallet classification. Cover a post-activation
  creation and anchored spend as a separate case.
- **Remediation commits:** `802bf2433` adds a consensus origin-height
  vector; `5f34f8cbf` refuses generic PQ-only wallet ownership without
  confirmed activation context; `ea104132e` adds premature-output relay
  policy and reorg cleanup.
- **Verification:** The functional regression failed before `ea104132e`:
  a PQ-only output transaction remained in the mempool after a rollback
  to the pre-activation tip. It passes after the policy change. The test
  also directly mines a lookalike in the last pre-activation block,
  confirms it is absent from PQ wallet balance, spends it without an
  anchor after activation, and proves that its new post-activation output
  cannot be spent again without an anchor. Unit vectors cover the
  pre-activation spend rule. Local `make check` passes. Historical
  directly mined lookalikes remain valid and unprotected by design;
  operators must not present them as PQ custody. Both required security
  jobs passed at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA, with the
  historical-block limitation documented.

### FINDING-162: PQ-only owner and message assets miss wallet subscriptions

- **Severity:** MEDIUM wallet messaging regression.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-10 at `99101c0f7`, before a
  contextual wallet ownership fix in the message subscription path.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Owner tokens and message channel assets
  received after bit 13 must retain the existing wallet subscription
  behavior when the PQ key controls them.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The regression follows the new
  context-dependent PQ-only ownership rule.
- **Root cause:** `WalletOwnsAssetForMessageSubscription` calls the generic
  `CWallet::IsMine(output)` method. The generic method deliberately returns
  `ISMINE_NO` for PQ-only asset scripts because it has no origin height.
  The helper returns before using the block height and PQ key it already
  has available.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/coins.cpp:20-39,
  `WalletOwnsAssetForMessageSubscription`; src/coins.cpp:309-333, transfer
  and new-asset subscriptions; src/script/ismine.cpp:159-203, generic
  ownership classification.
- **Concrete scenario:** A wallet receives a post-activation owner token
  or message channel at its PQ-only destination. The asset is spendable
  under the contextual wallet rule, but the message subscription helper
  reports that it is not owned and skips channel enrollment.
- **Expected behavior:** Evaluate wallet ownership using the output's
  confirmed block context. Do not enroll pre-activation PQ-only lookalikes
  as PQ-protected subscriptions.
- **Regression required:** With messaging enabled, confirm post-activation
  PQ-only owner-token and message-channel outputs and verify the expected
  subscription. Confirm a pre-activation PQ-only lookalike is excluded.
- **Remediation commit:** `5f34f8cbf` uses confirmed output context in
  the message subscription path.
- **Verification:** The owner-token subscription assertion in
  `feature_pq_asset_wallet_ownership.py` failed before the contextual
  lookup and passed afterward. The full functional test and local
  `make check` pass at `6ed46c91c`. Both required security jobs passed
  the wallet ownership functional test at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-163: Synthetic PQ asset identifier leaks as a native RVN address

- **Severity:** HIGH potential loss of native RVN funds.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-10 at `767e1b2b8`, before
  address-book filtering or storage changes.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The 160-bit asset identifier derived from
  a PQ program must never be presented as a wallet-owned RVN receive
  address. No ECDSA key exists for its P2PKH interpretation.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The synthetic identifier is new to the
  PQ-only 4.8.1 candidate.
- **Root cause:** New PQ asset destination creation adds the synthetic
  `CKeyID(Hash160(program))` to the wallet address book with purpose
  `pqasset`. `getaddressesbyaccount` enumerates every address-book entry
  with the matching account name without checking purpose or spendability.
  Qt hides the purpose, but the RPC exposes the identifier as an ordinary
  Ravencoin address.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/wallet/rpcwallet.cpp:280-290,
  PQ asset destination creation; src/wallet/rpcwallet.cpp:503-513,
  `getaddressesbyaccount`; src/wallet/rpcwallet.cpp:1572-1610,
  `listreceivedbyaddress`; src/wallet/rpcwallet.cpp:791-800 and
  src/wallet/wallet.cpp:5290-5375, `listaddressgroupings` inputs;
  src/qt/walletmodel.cpp:219-229, analogous Qt creation;
  src/qt/addresstablemodel.cpp:54-68, Qt purpose filtering;
  src/wallet/walletdb.cpp:1332-1350 and src/wallet/wallet.cpp:5675-5682,
  key-only recovery of v2 metadata.
- **Concrete scenario:** An application asks `getaddressesbyaccount` for
  RVN receive addresses after `getnewpqassetaddress` used the same account.
  It pays RVN to the returned synthetic P2PKH identifier. The wallet has
  no matching ECDSA private key, so that RVN is not spendable by the
  holder of the PQ program. A key-only recovery also drops the address
  purpose, so Qt can reclassify the synthetic identifier as a send address.
- **Expected behavior:** Native RVN address listings must exclude the
  synthetic asset identifier, including account, received-address and
  grouping APIs. Asset-specific listings must preserve the complete
  descriptor and PQ key association.
- **Regression required:** Generate a PQ-only asset destination for an
  account; prove the synthetic identifier appears in the native address
  listing before correction and is absent afterward, while the witness-v2
  native address remains present and the asset descriptor survives reload.
  Cover `listreceivedbyaddress` with empty entries and
  `listaddressgroupings` after receiving an asset. Recover keys only and
  verify the Qt address-table classification remains hidden.
- **Remediation commit:** `5f34f8cbf` filters the canonical synthetic ID
  from native address listings and groupings, and preserves its hidden
  address-book purpose after key-only recovery.
- **Verification:** The new `getaddressesbyaccount` assertion failed
  before the filter and passed afterward. The source RPC test also checks
  `listreceivedbyaddress` and `listaddressgroupings`. The wallet unit
  vector checks recovered purpose and canonical ID classification;
  both tests pass at `6ed46c91c`. Both required security jobs passed the
  source RPC test and wallet unit vectors at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-164: PQ-only restricted-asset status misses wallet ownership

- **Severity:** MEDIUM wallet and GUI state regression.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-10 at `767e1b2b8`, before a
  contextual PQ ownership correction for restricted-asset events.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Tag, untag, freeze and unfreeze operations
  affecting a wallet's PQ-only asset identifier must update its local
  restricted-asset state and GUI after a confirmed block.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The synthetic identifier without an
  ECDSA key is new to the PQ-only candidate.
- **Root cause:** `ContextualCheckNullAssetTxOut` decides whether to record
  an address event for the private wallet database with generic
  `IsMine(wallet, DecodeDestination(address))`. That detects classical
  key ownership but cannot identify a PQ-only asset identifier derived
  from an owned ML-DSA program. Only collected events reach the private
  restricted-asset database.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** src/assets/assets.cpp:5704-5710,
  wallet ownership test; src/validation.cpp:3097-3111, event storage and
  `UpdateMyRestrictedAssets`; restricted-asset RPC and Qt listings.
- **Concrete scenario:** A valid tag or freeze for an owned PQ-only asset
  identifier confirms on chain, but the wallet does not record the event.
  Its personal restricted-asset views can then disagree with the chain.
- **Expected behavior:** Recognize the synthetic identifier through its
  owned canonical PQ-only descriptor and preserve the existing update and
  restart behavior. Do not claim unrelated identifiers as wallet-owned.
- **Regression required:** Tag and freeze a wallet-owned PQ-only
  descriptor, mine the operations, and check the private DB, relevant
  RPCs and Qt view. Repeat after restart and cover an unrelated program.
- **Remediation commits:** `b7153328d` adds tag and freeze regressions;
  `6ed46c91c` records owned PQ-only identifiers in the private restricted
  database and Qt history.
- **Verification:** `feature_pq_asset_anchor.py` failed before the
  private-DB change because the tagged identifier was absent from
  `viewmytaggedaddresses`. It passes afterward for tag, untag, freeze,
  unfreeze, restart and an unpaired PQ program excluded from private
  history. The full local `make check` passes at `6ed46c91c`. Both
  required security jobs passed at `9151946ad`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-165: PQ asset functional tests require optional Python RIPEMD160

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release qualification failure.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-12, before changing the
  functional tests or their hash assertions.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The required asset custody and source RPC
  regressions must run on the pinned CI environment so a green final gate
  proves the protected asset paths on the release SHA.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The dependency entered with the new
  PQ-only functional assertions.
- **Root cause:** Two functional tests call the test framework's Python
