  `hash160` helper. It requires `hashlib.new('ripemd160', ...)`, which the
  Ubuntu 22.04 runner's Python/OpenSSL combination does not provide.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  test/functional/feature_pq_asset_anchor.py:776, unrelated program ID;
  test/functional/feature_pq_asset_source_rpc.py:63, descriptor ID check;
  test/functional/test_framework/script.py:33-34, hash helper.
- **Concrete scenario:** Final Gate run `38047870174` on
  `3ed32302d37fb343231c20905d178016ab1a07c2` passes `make check`
  but fails its behavioral security invariant step. Both functional tests
  stop with `ValueError: unsupported hash type ripemd160`. Required
  functional tests and the build matrix are skipped.
- **Expected behavior:** Preserve descriptor script and unrelated identifier
  assertions without relying on an optional Python hash provider. Keep the
  program-to-identifier binding check in the C++ consensus vectors.
- **Regression required:** Reproduce the failure with RIPEMD160 unavailable,
  then run both tests successfully under the same restriction and in the
  regular local test environment. Keep the C++ consensus binding vectors.
- **Remediation commit:** `3c1d8c091` removes the optional Python hash
  dependency from both functional tests.
- **Verification:** Red CI run `38047870174`; both functional tests pass
  locally with `hashlib.new('ripemd160', ...)` forced to raise, and local
  `make check` passes. Both required security jobs ran the two functional
  tests successfully at `9151946ad` on the pinned runner.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-166: Build Raven hides the failing unit test diagnosis

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release qualification failure.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-12, before changing the
  workflow's mandatory unit-test step.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** All release artifact builds must be gated
  by a diagnosable, successful `make check` on their exact source SHA.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The gate is specific to this release
  workflow.
- **Root cause:** Build Raven previously ran `make check` without printing
  `src/test-suite.log` or `src/test/test_raven.log` on failure. Automake
  printed only the aggregate `FAIL: test/test_raven` result. The original
  unit assertion cannot be reconstructed from the retained CI log.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** .github/workflows/build-raven.yml:65-68,
  mandatory unit-test step.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `38047871989` on
  `3ed32302d37fb343231c20905d178016ab1a07c2` builds successfully
  but fails `make check` with one failing test binary. It skips all release
  artifact jobs. Final Gate on the same SHA passes its unit tests, so the
  particular Build Raven test failure has no retained diagnostic log.
- **Expected behavior:** Preserve `make check` as a hard gate and print its
  bounded failure logs before exiting nonzero. If a failure recurs, use
  its log to identify and remediate the underlying test defect.
- **Regression required:** Simulate a failing `make check` and prove the
  workflow reports the specific test failure and still fails; then rerun
  both workflows on one exact SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `2c9576001` prints bounded test logs on a
  nonzero `make check` result and keeps the artifact gate mandatory.
- **Verification:** Red CI run `38047871989`; a local simulated failure
  showed the original step hid a test assertion, while the revised step
  prints it and still fails. Local `make check` passes. Build Raven
  `38296258242` and Final Gate `38296262444` passed their mandatory
  `make check` at `9151946ad`. Build Raven `38335505917` and Final Gate
  `38335508527` also passed at `6bdbae2b4`. The original run has no
  preserved test log or artifact, so its particular assertion remains
  unknown. On 2026-10-13 the owner accepted this documented uncertainty
  after reviewing the diagnostic repair and repeated green paired runs.
- **Final status:** DISPOSED as an unclassifiable historical unit failure;
  the diagnostic defect is FIXED and qualified on candidate CI. Mandatory
  `make check` on the final 4.8.1 SHA remains a release gate. Any new
  failure requires its own diagnosis and remedy.

### FINDING-167: Pinned FreeType source host blocks the ARM64 artifact

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability; the artifact gate fails closed.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-12 at `a939b1629`, before
  changing the FreeType recipe.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** All five Build Raven release targets must
  produce artifacts from the same SHA as a green Final Gate.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The FreeType 2.7.1 version, Savannah
  redirecting download URL and SHA-256 pin are inherited from Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** On the ARM64 runner, repeated connections to
  `download.savannah.gnu.org` timed out. The generic depends fallback URL
  was empty, so the final curl call failed with an invalid URL.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `depends/packages/freetype.mk:1-5`,
  pinned source URL and digest; `depends/funcs.mk:22-35`, fetch fallback.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `38234048442` on
  `1df43214f8159826cb8a27cda29c23c299be3873` passed its mandatory
  security gate, then the `build (aarch64, aarch64-linux-gnu)` job failed
  in Build Dependencies with six curl connection timeouts and a final
  empty-URL error. The same SHA's Final Gate run `38234051822` passed.
- **Expected behavior:** Retrieve the same pinned FreeType archive from a
  reachable official mirror, verify its existing SHA-256 digest, and fail
  closed if the bytes differ. Requalify all release targets on one SHA.
- **Regression required:** Preserve the red CI log; fetch the archive from
  the replacement source and verify its digest against the recipe; run a
  clean ARM64 depends fetch and rerun Build Raven and Final Gate on one SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `829e895e3` switches to the official Savannah
  direct mirror without changing the archive name, version or digest.
- **Verification:** The official Savannah direct mirror and the upstream
  FreeType SourceForge release both supplied an archive matching the pinned
  digest `3a3bb2c4e15ffb433f2032f50a5b5a92558206822e22bfe8cbe339af4aa82f88`
  in independent local fetches. A clean ARM64 depends fetch through the
  revised recipe passed and checked the same digest. The red CI run is
  `38234048442`. Build Raven `38296258242` built the aarch64 artifact
  `11771315730` and the other four release targets at `9151946ad`;
  paired Final Gate `38296262444` succeeded at that SHA. After the
  recurrence and fallback repair in FINDING-171, Build Raven
  `38433449366` completed the ARM64 artifact `11837205453` and the
  other four release targets on exact versioned SHA
  `bfa2efbb3f47dca3e32df3c73b74625d5c64b5ee`; Final Gate
  `38433455013` passed on the same SHA.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the paired versioned candidate SHA after
  the fallback repair in FINDING-171. Final release-SHA artifact
  qualification remains due.

### FINDING-168: Wallet rewrite recovery can leave PQ plaintext in the database file

- **Severity:** HIGH confidentiality and release qualification failure.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-12 at `471deaeea`, before
  changing the wallet or its regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** After wallet encryption and recovery, a
  plaintext ML-DSA secret must not remain in the physical wallet database.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. Core 4.8.0 uses the same Berkeley DB
  optimization; the 2,560-byte ML-DSA secret and recovery test are new to
  this phase.
- **Root cause:** Pinned Berkeley DB 4.8.30 sets the new BTREE page clear
  length to 32 bytes when its own encryption is off. Its memory pool can
  reuse buffers containing a deleted 2,560-byte PQ secret, and the rest of
  a newly allocated 4,096-byte page is not cleared before it is written.
  Logical record deletion and a successful rewrite therefore do not prove
  the active wallet file is free of those bytes.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/wallet/test/pq_wallet_tests.cpp:255-263`
  and lines 2969-3133, physical secret scan after restart recovery;
  `src/wallet/db.cpp:788-960`, database rewrite and file replacement;
  pinned Berkeley DB `db/db.c:545-557`, page clear-length policy, and
  `mp/mp_fget.c:804-814`, new-page initialization.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `38238492777` on
  `471deaeead7533d238d03e675235a07f13e41e1e` passed mandatory
  `make check`, then failed `pq_wallet_tests/rewrite_failure_quarantines_until_restart_recovery`
  at line 3085 because the physical wallet file still contained the exact
  ML-DSA secret after startup rewrite recovery. Final Gate run
  `38238496806` passed the same test and SHA, so the condition appears
  intermittent. Build Raven skipped every release artifact.
- **Expected behavior:** Recovery keeps the wallet quarantined on rewrite
  failure. Once recovery reports success, the physical wallet file and
  any exportable backup contain no plaintext PQ secret.
- **Regression required:** Preserve the red CI run. Assert that the
  Berkeley DB wallet page clear length equals its page size, retain the
  exact physical secret scan after recovery, then demonstrate a red-to-green
  result with the patched dependency and rerun both CI workflows at one
  exact SHA. Keep `make check` mandatory.
- **Remediation commits:** `77036141c` adds the deterministic red
  clear-length regression. `5f21bb18f` patches the pinned Berkeley DB
  Btree, Recno and Hash page policy to clear every new page in full.
  `d64c34830` makes `CDB::Rewrite` refuse a dependency whose new database
  pages are not fully cleared, preserving wallet quarantine on failure.
- **Verification:** Red Build Raven run `38238492777`; green Final Gate
  run `38238496806` is conflicting evidence, not closure. The new
  `wallet_database_clears_entire_new_page` regression failed against the
  original pinned library with `32 != 4096`, then passed after rebuilding
  against the patched dependency. Fifteen local full wallet suites and 250
  isolated recovery tests passed on Ubuntu 24.04 before the patch,
  confirming that repetition alone is not a reliable release gate. After
  the patch, local `make check`, the full RIP-25 behavioral invariant gate
  with Python 3.10, and all nine required functional tests passed. These
  checks cover the active wallet file and backups, not forensic erasure of
  old filesystem blocks or Berkeley DB transaction logs. Build Raven
  `38296258242` passed the new full-page-clear unit regression and the
  restart-recovery physical-secret scan at `9151946ad`, then produced all
  five artifacts. Paired Final Gate `38296262444` passed the same tests
  and SHA.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the qualified candidate SHA; final release-SHA
  qualification remains due, and FINDING-067 remains OPEN.

### FINDING-169: Functional cache peer can remain at genesis during CI qualification

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release qualification failure; the artifact gate fails closed.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-13 at `7c0d43c85`, before a
  cache connectivity remedy or diagnostic regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Required functional security tests must run
  before release artifacts are accepted from a Build Raven run.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The failure occurs in the functional
  test framework before an asset or PQ functional assertion runs.
- **Root cause:** The precise cause of the original CI event is not
  established. Cache creation waited for RPC readiness but did not require
  P2P connectivity before mining. During the failed run, node 7 remained
  at genesis while nodes 0 through 6 reached height 25. The runner
  retained no node debug logs as CI artifacts, so the original missing
  connection or block acceptance cannot be distinguished from that log.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `test/functional/test_framework/test_framework.py:385-417`,
  cache node startup and synchronization;
  `test/functional/test_framework/util.py:490-512`, block sync timeout;
  `test/functional/test_runner.py:442-455`, cache failure reporting;
  `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml:85-86`, required behavioral gate.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `38366146893` on
  `7c0d43c85550afc310a7a3a2e458fda3a274fc4f` failed in
  `create_cache.py`: seven nodes had the same height-25 block hash, while
  node 7 reported height 0 for the full 60-second wait. The five artifact
  jobs were skipped. Final Gate run `38366157600` passed the behavioral
  and required functional suites on the identical SHA.
- **Expected behavior:** Cache creation establishes usable peer connections
  before mining, fails with enough node evidence to diagnose any persistent
  disconnect, and never reports a green artifact gate if its tests did not run.
- **Regression required:** Reproduce a missing cache peer deterministically,
  show the failure before remediation, then show cache creation recovering
  or diagnosing it after remediation. Rerun mandatory `make check`, Build
  Raven and Final Gate at one exact SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `e6d033883` requires each cache follower to
  establish a versioned peer connection to node 0 before mining, retries
  a missing connection, and includes peer state when later block sync
  fails. The real eight-node regression is part of both CI behavioral
  gates through `check-rip25-v48-invariants.sh`.
- **Verification:** Before the repair, the regression withheld node 7's
  startup connection and reproduced the observed height pattern: nodes
  0 through 6 reached 25, node 7 remained at genesis, and sync timed
  out. After the repair the same eight-node test passed in 3.1 seconds;
  a fresh complete `create_cache.py` run passed in 4.4 seconds. Local
  `make -j8 check` passed. The full behavioral gate, including the new
  regression and nine functional cases, passed under Python 3.10. The
  original CI cause remains uncertain because its node logs were lost.
  On exact SHA `06f9d90fd0d213ad33b078c87fd1e9f4fd05edde`, the
  required security job in Build Raven `38395943288` and the security
  job plus all seven builds in Final Gate `38395942637` passed. Both
  security jobs ran the cache regression successfully. Build Raven as a
  whole failed only in its ARM64 FreeType fetch, recorded in FINDING-171.
  On exact versioned SHA
  `bfa2efbb3f47dca3e32df3c73b74625d5c64b5ee`, Build Raven
  `38433449366` and Final Gate `38433455013` both completed successfully.
  The required security jobs passed the cache regression and mandatory
  `make check`; Build Raven completed all five release targets and Final
  Gate all seven builds.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the paired versioned candidate SHA. The
  final release SHA still requires the same two wholly green workflows
  after the later optional-tool repair in FINDING-172.

### FINDING-170: Standalone cache peer regression cannot locate ravend in CI

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release qualification failure; the artifact gate fails closed.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-13 at `4078257ee`, before
  correcting the new standalone regression's binary path.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A required behavioral regression must run
  against the just-built node binary before artifacts are accepted.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This is a test harness integration
  defect introduced during FINDING-169 remediation.
- **Root cause:** The standalone unittest called the functional cache
  initializer without setting `RAVEND`. The initializer then attempted
  to execute the bare name `ravend`; the CI runner does not place the
  just-built binary on `PATH`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/test_framework/test_cache_peers.py:29-60`, standalone
  regression setup; `test/functional/test_framework/test_framework.py:385-395`,
  cache node command; `contrib/devtools/check-rip25-v48-invariants.sh:807`,
  required invocation.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `38394096626` on exact SHA
  `4078257ee2862586eb7a0c07ed79b7ee4635f5f3` passed mandatory
  `make check`, wallet behavioral cases, fuzz smoke, and gate helper
  checks. Its new cache peer unittest then raised `FileNotFoundError`
  for `ravend` before starting any node; all five artifact jobs were skipped.
- **Expected behavior:** The standalone test resolves the built `src/ravend`
  binary independently of the runner's ambient `PATH`, while still
  withholding node 7's startup peer to test the recovery path.
- **Regression required:** Run the test with `RAVEND` unset and a `PATH`
  lacking the build tree; show the failure before correction and success
  afterward. Rerun mandatory `make check`, Build Raven, and Final Gate
  on one exact post-correction SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `d45aa5b44` resolves the built node binary
  from the regression test's source-tree location and supplies it as
  `RAVEND` only for that test.
- **Verification:** The isolated local command reproduced the same
  `FileNotFoundError` before correction. With the test deriving the
  binary path from its source location, it passed in 3.1 seconds under
  the same restricted environment. Local `make -j8 check` and the full
  behavioral invariant gate with nine focused functional cases passed
  under Python 3.10 after the correction. On exact SHA
  `06f9d90fd0d213ad33b078c87fd1e9f4fd05edde`, the required security
  jobs in Build Raven `38395943288` and Final Gate `38395942637` both
  passed the standalone cache regression. Final Gate passed all eight
  builds; Build Raven's ARM64 artifact failed for the separate FreeType
  source timeout in FINDING-171.
  On exact versioned SHA
  `bfa2efbb3f47dca3e32df3c73b74625d5c64b5ee`, Build Raven
  `38433449366` and Final Gate `38433455013` both completed successfully
  with mandatory `make check`, all five release targets, and all eight
  Final Gate builds.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the paired versioned candidate SHA. The
  final release SHA still requires the same two wholly green workflows
  after the later optional-tool repair in FINDING-172.

### FINDING-171: FreeType mirror timeout again blocks the ARM64 release artifact

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability; artifact qualification fails closed.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-13 at
  `06f9d90fd0d213ad33b078c87fd1e9f4fd05edde`, before another source
  mirror or fallback change.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** All five Build Raven release targets must
  succeed at the same exact SHA as a green Final Gate.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The FreeType 2.7.1 archive and SHA-256 pin
  are inherited from Core 4.8.0. FINDING-167 changed its primary host but
  retained a single reachable-host dependency.
- **Root cause:** The ARM64 runner could not connect to
  `download-mirror.savannah.gnu.org` during six curl attempts. The generic
  fallback path was empty, so the final request used an invalid URL.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `depends/packages/freetype.mk:1-5`,
  primary source and pinned digest; `depends/funcs.mk:22-35`, fetch and
  generic fallback; `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml`, ARM64 artifact job.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `38395943288`, job
  `115296217551`, passed checkout and security invariant checks but failed
  in Build Dependencies before configuring Ravencoin. The log shows six
  connection timeouts followed by `curl: (3)` for the empty fallback.
  The required security job had passed; this is an artifact-source
  availability failure, not evidence of an asset-consensus failure.
- **Expected behavior:** Fetch the exact pinned FreeType archive from a
  second official source if the primary host is unreachable, validate the
  unchanged SHA-256 before extraction, and reject any mismatch.
- **Regression required:** Preserve this red CI log; force primary-source
  failure in a clean local depends fetch and prove the secondary source
  downloads the exact pinned bytes. Then rerun Build Raven and Final Gate
  at a common post-remediation SHA with all release targets green.
- **Remediation commit:** `38b84a9c7` adds a FreeType-specific SourceForge
  fallback to the depends recipe. Each fetched archive is checked against
  the unchanged pinned digest before it reaches the source directory.
- **Verification:** With the primary URL forced to the closed local port
  `127.0.0.1:9`, a clean ARM64 depends fetch used the SourceForge fallback
  and accepted the archive with SHA-256
  `3a3bb2c4e15ffb433f2032f50a5b5a92558206822e22bfe8cbe339af4aa82f88`.
  The same forced primary failure followed by altered fallback bytes
  failed its hash check and left no source archive in place. The red CI
  evidence remains Build Raven `38395943288`, job `115296217551`.
  On exact versioned SHA
  `bfa2efbb3f47dca3e32df3c73b74625d5c64b5ee`, Build Raven
  `38433449366` passed all five release targets, including ARM64
  artifact `11837205453`, and Final Gate `38433455013` passed all
  eight builds and its required security job. Both security jobs passed
  mandatory `make check`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on the paired versioned candidate SHA;
  final release-SHA artifact qualification remains due. FINDING-067
  remains OPEN.

### FINDING-172: Optional raven-tx cannot link the asset and PQ helpers

- **Severity:** LOW release tooling failure; the default release artifact
  workflow does not enable `raven-tx`.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-13 at
  `bfa2efbb3f47dca3e32df3c73b74625d5c64b5ee`, before changing its
  link libraries.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The 4.8.1 release process regenerates
  manpages from current utilities. The optional `--with-tx` utility must
  link for the normal Automake build of that target.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Core 4.8.0 already omitted the asset/server
  library from `raven_tx_LDADD`. The current PQ-only asset helper adds
  another missing symbol to that existing link gap.
- **Root cause:** The target links common transaction objects, including
  `coins.cpp` and `script/sign.cpp`, but omits the server archive that
  defines the asset helpers they call. Other server dependencies may
  also be needed once the archive is included.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/Makefile.am:531-565`, optional
  target link list; `src/Makefile.am:247-270`, server asset sources;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:3719`, PQ-only helper.
- **Concrete scenario:** After configuring this checkout with
  `--with-tx --with-gui=no` and the pinned local depends prefix,
  `make -C src -j8 raven-tx` exited 2. Its linker reported unresolved
  `CAssetsCache`, `AreAssetsDeployed`, and `IsPQOnlyAssetScript` symbols.
  The default `--without-tx` local `make -j8 check` passed.
- **Expected behavior:** The optional utility links against the full
  dependency closure and its current `--help` can regenerate the manpage.
- **Regression required:** Preserve the red link result, then build
  `raven-tx` successfully with `--with-tx`, run help and transaction
  creation smoke checks, rerun mandatory `make check`, and exercise the
  optional target in the required CI security builds.
- **Remediation commits:** `09277a96b` places the mutually dependent
  common, server, and wallet objects in one aggregate static archive and
  adds the missing external libraries; `af0cc47d4` enables `--with-tx`
  in both required Linux security builds.
- **Verification:** Red local link log is private
  `evidence/f172-raven-tx-red.log`; CI release artifacts are unaffected
  because the Build Raven configure script does not pass `--with-tx`.
  Before the fix, libtool removed repeated core archives and moved GNU
  linker group flags ahead of them; a direct linker invocation supplied
  the current manpage from the same source. After the fix, local
  configuration with the pinned depends prefix, Qt 5.12.11, and
  `--with-tx` built `libraven_tx_core.a` and linked `raven-tx` through
  Automake. Its `--help` and `-create -json` smoke checks passed, as did
  `make -j4 check`. The aggregate archive contains 152 core objects.
  Both required Linux CI security jobs now exercise this optional target.
  Cross-platform execution of the optional tool is outside the release
  artifact matrix.
- **Final status:** FIXED. The required CI security jobs in Build Raven
  38475261912 and Final Gate 38475261551 both built the optional
  Automake raven-tx target and passed mandatory make check on exact
  SHA a16a05bdb925985bc33694455e582cd7d2261958. All five Build
  Raven artifacts and seven Final Gate builds also passed. The five
  release artifacts do not contain this optional utility.

### FINDING-173: XCB source timeout blocks Linux release artifacts

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability; exact-SHA artifact qualification fails closed.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-14 at
  `975ab4f4a49d6217fa213da87384950b4e21290d`, before changing either
  XCB depends recipe.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Build Raven must produce all five release
  targets on the same commit as a green Final Gate.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The pinned libxcb 1.10 and xcb-proto 1.10
  source URLs are inherited depends configuration. Their expected SHA-256
  digests remain the release integrity checks.
- **Root cause:** `xcb.freedesktop.org` timed out during dependency download
  in two release jobs. The generic fallback URL was empty, so the final
  curl request failed before configuration or compilation.
- **Affected files:** `depends/packages/libxcb.mk`,
  `depends/packages/xcb_proto.mk`, and `depends/funcs.mk` fetch fallback.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven `38546424615` at the exact SHA above
  passed its mandatory security job and produced Windows, ARM32, and macOS
  artifacts. Linux x86_64 job `115800868704` timed out fetching
  `libxcb-1.10.tar.bz2`; ARM64 job `115800868477` timed out fetching
  `xcb-proto-1.10.tar.bz2`. Both ended in the `Build Dependencies` step.
  Final Gate `38546434041` passed all eight jobs at the same SHA. The
  earlier exact-SHA Build Raven `38475261912` fetched both XCB archives
  successfully from the same host, so the failure is source availability,
  not evidence of a consensus or compiler defect.
- **Expected behavior:** A second pinned source supplies the exact same
  archive when the first source is unavailable. The fixed SHA-256 must be
  checked before extraction; a digest mismatch must fail closed.
- **Regression required:** Preserve both red CI jobs. Force the first
  source to fail in a clean local fetch, prove that the alternate source
  supplies both expected bytes, and prove that altered bytes are rejected.
  Then rerun Build Raven and Final Gate at one final SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `fecdcdaee865f9d95eb931a447ff9570562eafde`
  adds a package-specific HTTPS fallback for both pinned XCB archives.
  Their versions and SHA-256 digests are unchanged.
- **Verification:** The original `38546424615` attempt 1 remains the red
  artifact evidence. Its same-SHA attempt 2 passed all five release jobs
  once the original XCB host responded. In a clean local fetch with the
  primary URL forced to `127.0.0.1:9`, both fallback downloads matched
  their pinned digests. One-byte-altered fallback archives were rejected
  for both packages, without installing source archives. Private logs:
  `evidence/f173-xcb-fallback-green.log`,
  `evidence/f173-xcb-fallback-reject.log`, and
  `evidence/f173-xcb-proto-fallback-reject.log`. Build Raven
  `38592937851` passed its security job and all five release targets on
  exact `fecdcdaee`; the restored primary host served the XCB archives in
  that run, so the forced local test supplies direct fallback evidence.
  Final Gate `38592942408` failed separately at pinned ccache first use,
  recorded in FINDING-174.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the XCB availability defect. A wholly green
  Build Raven and Final Gate pair at the final release SHA is still due.

### FINDING-174: Parallel first use of pinned ccache can abort the test gate

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability; the mandatory security job
  fails closed before tests or artifact builds run.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-14 at
  `fecdcdaee865f9d95eb931a447ff9570562eafde`, before changing the
  CI cache initialization.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Mandatory `make check` and all release
  builds must complete at one exact candidate SHA.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The pinned native ccache 3.3.4 recipe
  is inherited; the first-use workflow setup is release CI configuration.
- **Root cause:** Two compiler processes can start ccache concurrently
  when its per-runner `ccache.conf` is absent. In ccache 3.3.4,
  `initialize` reads that path and then checks it with `stat` after a
  failed open. One process can create the file between the other
  process's failed open and `stat`, causing the latter to report the
  original missing-file error as fatal. Creating only the directory
  does not eliminate this race.
- **Affected files:** `.github/workflows/rip25-v48-final-gate.yml` and
  `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml`, the cache setup before parallel
  compilation; pinned ccache 3.3.4 `ccache.c:2980-3073`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Final Gate `38592942408`, security job
  `115952251224`, on the exact SHA above failed in `Build tests` at its
  first parallel C++ objects with
  `ccache: error: /home/runner/.ccache/ccache.conf: No such file or directory`.
  No `make check` result exists for that run. Build Raven `38592937851`
  passed its security job and all five artifact jobs at the same SHA;
  the independent failure is timing-dependent.
- **Expected behavior:** Initialize the pinned ccache configuration once
  serially before any parallel compile in each workflow job. Preserve
  existing configuration when present.
- **Regression required:** Retain the red Final Gate log, start the pinned
  ccache from an empty cache directory and prove serial initialization
  creates its configuration, then run parallel compiler wrappers without
  the missing-file error. Both exact-SHA workflows must pass afterward.
- **Remediation commit:** `8efa331e0` runs the pinned ccache
  `--print-config` once after creating its directory and before any
  parallel compile in both security jobs and both artifact matrices.
  Existing cache configuration is preserved.
- **Verification:** The red Final Gate log is private
  `evidence/f174-final-gate-38592942408-failed.log`. A clean local
  ccache directory gained `ccache.conf` from the serial invocation;
  16 subsequent parallel compiler wrappers all exited successfully
  and produced their object files. Its JSON record is
  `evidence/f174-ccache-first-use-local.json`. The required functional
  list gate and local `make -j4 check` passed after the workflow edit;
  log: `evidence/local-make-check-f174-candidate.log`. Build Raven
  `38713686026` and Final Gate `38713685824` passed on exact
  `0c33cc92c`; both mandatory security jobs ran `make check`, all five
  release targets and seven Final Gate matrix jobs passed, and the five
  downloaded release packages passed checksum and provenance review.
  The private verification record is
  `evidence/ci-0c33cc92c-verification.json`.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the ccache first-use race. Release
  qualification on the later final source SHA remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-175: xproto source timeout blocks the Linux release artifact

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability; the artifact job fails closed
  before building the Linux x86_64 package.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-15 at
  `ee30148e794ab5fec6c07d621e6bb4a581ef942f`, before changing the
  pinned xproto fetch recipe.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Build Raven must deliver all five release
  targets on the same exact SHA as a green Final Gate.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The xproto 7.0.26 package recipe and
  its HTTP primary source are inherited from Core 4.8.0. This is a
  dependency availability defect, not a consensus failure.
- **Root cause:** The primary xproto archive host timed out repeatedly.
  The generic fetch then attempted an unset fallback download path,
  producing a malformed URL and terminating the depends build.
- **Affected file/function:** `depends/packages/xproto.mk`, the pinned
  source recipe; `depends/funcs.mk`, generic fetch fallback.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven `38710185861`, Linux x86_64 job
  `116347291798`, failed in `Build Dependencies` after repeated
  `curl: (28) Connection timeout` for `xproto-7.0.26.tar.bz2` and
  `curl: (3) URL using bad/illegal format or missing URL` on the empty
  fallback. The mandatory security job at that SHA passed.
- **Expected behavior:** When the primary host is unavailable, an HTTPS
  alternate source supplies exactly the pinned archive. A SHA-256
  mismatch must reject the archive before extraction.
- **Regression required:** Retain the red CI log; force the primary
  source to fail in a clean local fetch, verify the alternate archive's
  existing SHA-256, and prove that altered bytes fail closed. Then rerun
  both workflows on one final SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `8552b2ca94255e1fd02f5411a7b2ae1ba7e6062a`
  adds a package-specific HTTPS fallback. The xproto version and pinned
  SHA-256 are unchanged.
- **Verification:** The red CI job log is private
  `evidence/build-raven-38710185861-linux-job.raw.log`. A clean local
  fetch with the primary URL forced to `127.0.0.1:9` reproduced the
  empty fallback failure before the fix. With the new recipe, the same
  forced-primary test fetched the alternate archive and matched its
  existing digest. A one-byte-altered alternate archive was rejected
  before installation. Private logs: `evidence/f175-xproto-red.log`,
  `evidence/f175-xproto-green.log`, and
  `evidence/f175-xproto-reject.log`. Local `make -j4 check` passed after
  the fix, including `test/test_raven`; log:
  `evidence/local-make-check-f175-candidate.log`.
- **Final status:** FIXED. Build Raven `38753089034` and Final Gate
  `38753089260` passed on `35fa04a40`; all five downloaded
  packages passed provenance, individual checksums, and common-source
  verification in
  `evidence/ci-35fa04a40/artifact-verification.json`.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-176: libXau source has the same empty fallback failure

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability; the Linux depends build can
  fail closed before packaging.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-15 at `6e0a2213e`, before
  changing the pinned libXau fetch recipe.

  targets after its mandatory security gate.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The libXau 1.0.8 package recipe and
  HTTP Xorg primary source are inherited from Core 4.8.0.

  that timed out in FINDING-175. It has no package-specific alternate
  source. The generic fallback URL is empty, so a primary failure ends
  with an invalid URL instead of trying an available mirror.
- **Affected file/function:** `depends/packages/libXau.mk`, pinned source
  recipe; `depends/funcs.mk`, generic fetch fallback.
- **Concrete scenario:** In an isolated local depends fetch with the
  primary path forced to `127.0.0.1:9`, the unmodified recipe failed
  with `curl: (3) URL rejected: No host part in the URL`. The prior
  Linux CI job stopped at xproto before reaching libXau. The same Xorg
  archive host timed out during a separate direct availability probe.
- **Expected behavior:** A second HTTPS source supplies the exact pinned
  libXau archive when the primary is unavailable. The unchanged SHA-256
  must reject altered bytes before extraction.
- **Regression required:** Preserve the red local fetch, force the
  primary source to fail after adding the alternate, verify the pinned
  digest, reject a one-byte-altered archive, and rerun `make check` and
  exact-SHA CI.
- **Remediation commit:** `f154953a8e554c7e32a2c878b41fb0795b014103`
  adds a package-specific HTTPS fallback. The libXau version and pinned
  SHA-256 are unchanged.
- **Verification:** `evidence/f176-libxau-red.log` records the missing
  fallback before the fix. With the primary source forced to fail, the
  new recipe downloaded the alternate archive with the pinned digest;
  `evidence/f176-libxau-green.log` records the passing fetch. A
  one-byte-altered archive failed its checksum before installation in
  `evidence/f176-libxau-reject.log`. Local `make -j4 check` passed after
  the fix, including `test/test_raven`; log:
  `evidence/local-make-check-f176-candidate.log`.
- **Final status:** FIXED. Build Raven `38753089034` and Final Gate
  `38753089260` passed on `35fa04a40`; all five downloaded
  packages passed provenance, individual checksums, and common-source
  verification in
  `evidence/ci-35fa04a40/artifact-verification.json`.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-177: Qt GUI crashes when wallet support is disabled at runtime

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability. The wallet-disabled GUI
  exits with a segmentation fault before it can serve as a node UI.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-15 at `35fa04a40`, before
  changing the GUI or its release workflow.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The pinned Qt release package must
  start in each supported runtime mode without dereferencing an
  absent wallet control.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Official Core 4.8.0 source
  `b60f50e04` has the same unconditional currency-control call
  and the same wallet-disabled construction path.
- **Observed failure:** Unmodified Linux release packages from
  `9151946ad` and `0c33cc92c` both exited 139 when started
  under Xvfb with a fresh testnet data directory and `-disablewallet`.
  The `0c33cc92c` package opened its main regtest window with an
  existing wallet, answered RPC, and shut down cleanly without that
  option. Private red logs are
  `evidence/gui-smoke-35fa/no-wallet-old-startup.log` and
  `evidence/gui-smoke-35fa/no-wallet-startup.log`.
- **Root cause:** `RavenGUI` omits `walletFrame` and
  `comboRvnUnit` when the wallet is disabled, but
  `setClientModel` still calls `onCurrencyChange`.
  That slot dereferences the null currency combo box after a valid
  initial index. The affected sites are
  `src/qt/ravengui.cpp:132-171,714-720,962-975,1864-1877`.
  A local GDB run bypassing the separately documented GCC 13
  pinned-Qt fortify issue for that diagnostic process stopped at
  `QComboBox::setCurrentIndex` from `RavenGUI::onCurrencyChange`.
  Its private stack is
  `evidence/gui-smoke-35fa/local-no-wallet-gdb.log`.
- **Expected behavior:** Wallet-disabled GUI startup reaches an RPC
  ready state, retains its non-wallet controls, and stops cleanly.
- **Regression required:** Keep the red package launch, run a
  focused Xvfb wallet-disabled startup and RPC smoke against the
  pinned Linux GUI after the fix, add that smoke to the Linux
  release build, and rerun `make check` and exact-SHA CI.
- **Remediation commits:** `4f6e6305c` adds the focused Xvfb
  startup, liveness, RPC, and clean-shutdown regression to the Linux
  Build Raven release job. `9861294c4` skips the absent wallet
  currency control in the node-only GUI.
- **Verification:** The final regression script failed against the
  unmodified `0c33cc92c` package with status 139 before RPC
  readiness; private log:
  `evidence/f177-red-final.log`. The local pinned-Qt GUI rebuilt
  after the guard; with the same diagnostic-only Qt fortify shim
  already required on this GCC 13 host, the script reached RPC
  readiness, remained alive for two seconds, and shut down cleanly
  through RPC. The shim is outside the repository and is not part of
  any release package. Private log:
  `evidence/f177-green-local-final.log`. Local
  `make -j4 check` passed all 511 unit cases after the fix;
  `evidence/local-make-check-f177.log` and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f177-unit.log` retain the results.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source `77d8f4c65`.
  Build Raven `38853895481`, Linux job `116826216457`, passed
  the unmodified pinned-Qt startup smoke without the diagnostic
  shim. Final Gate `38853895080` passed on the same SHA, and all
  five Build Raven packages passed the checks recorded in
  `evidence/ci-77d8f4c65/artifact-verification.json`.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-178: Wallet-disabled Qt GUI can crash when toolbar icons option changes

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability. The node-only GUI can
  dereference absent toolbar controls after startup.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-15 at `9061e119b`, before
  changing the affected GUI slot or its regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The pinned Qt release package must
  remain usable in wallet-disabled mode when available options change.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The wallet-only toolbar creation
  path and unconditional options signal connection are inherited from
  Core 4.8.0.
- **Observed failure path:** `RavenGUI::createToolBars` creates
  `labelToolbar` and `m_toolbar` only when `walletFrame` exists.
  `-disablewallet` omits that frame, while the Options dialog retains
  its Window tab and Icons only option. Changing the option emits
  `updateIconsOnlyToolbar`; the connected GUI slot dereferences both
  absent controls in `src/qt/ravengui.cpp`.
- **Observed failure:** The pinned-Qt source GUI at `9061e119b`
  reached RPC readiness with `-disablewallet` under Xvfb. The
  focused regression opened Options, selected Window and Icons only,
  then submitted the dialog. Qt exited with SIGSEGV, status 139.
  GDB stopped in `QLabel::setPixmap` from
  `RavenGUI::updateIconsOnlyToolbar` at
  `src/qt/ravengui.cpp:918`. The Options mapper also emits the
  toolbar signal on a normal OK submission even if the setting did
  not change.
- **Expected behavior:** Changing the option in node-only mode must
  not crash the GUI. The wallet-enabled toolbar must still update.
- **Regression:** Commit
  `068a87ba2067ab32ef2877ee39e3fce394926339` adds a
  real Xvfb Options interaction to the Linux Build Raven job.
  It verifies RPC readiness, opens the dialog, selects Icons only,
  submits, checks the stored setting and process liveness, then
  shuts down through RPC. It failed before the GUI fix; private
  evidence is `evidence/f178-options-red.log` and the GDB
  trace in `evidence/f178-options-gdb.log`.
- **Remediation commit:**
  `0441b38d34ab8622c28bd59e216c3386a0fc0ca9` skips the
  toolbar update when its wallet-only controls are absent.
- **Verification:** The same Xvfb regression passed after the guard,
  including the persisted Icons only setting and clean RPC
  shutdown; private log:
  `evidence/f178-options-green-local.log`. The local pinned
  Qt build required the previously documented GCC 13 diagnostic
  shim, which is outside the repository and package. Local
  `make -j4 check` passed all 511 unit cases after the fix;
  `evidence/f178-local-make-check-unit.log` preserves the result.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source `77d8f4c65`.
  Build Raven `38853895481`, Linux job `116826216457`, passed
  the unmodified pinned-Qt Options smoke without the diagnostic
  shim. Final Gate `38853895080` passed on the same SHA, and all
  five packages passed provenance and checksum checks in
  `evidence/ci-77d8f4c65/artifact-verification.json`.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-179: Bit-12 rollout notes omit automatic miner signaling and a go/no-go gate

- **Severity:** HIGH release safety. A pool can count toward bit-12 lock-in
  before its validating ecosystem is ready for the incompatible block rules.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-15 at `22b65545c`, before
  changing the release notes or adding a rollout documentation regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Operators must coordinate an upgrade of
  miners, pools, validators, and services before signaling can lock in the
  12 MWU and PQ witness rules.
- **Observed gap:** The 4.8.1 release notes require readiness before
  signaling but do not say that upgraded mining templates set bit 12
  automatically in STARTED. They do not define a recorded go/no-go decision.
  The notes also omit the need to deploy revised parameters to all miners
  and validators if a replacement release moves the start window.
- **Code behavior:** `CreateNewBlock` uses `ComputeBlockVersion`;
  `pq_hybrid` is a forced getblocktemplate version bit; the
  `-blockversion` override applies only on regtest. Mainnet operators
  cannot rely on that override to disable the bit in 4.8.1 templates.
- **Expected behavior:** Release instructions must state the automatic
  template signaling, identify the evidence and owner decision required
  before the STARTED window, and describe a qualified replacement release
  with coordinated deployment if readiness is absent.
- **Regression required:** Freeze a focused release-document contract
  check that fails against the current instructions and passes after the
  rollout instructions are corrected. Review the described behavior
  against the actual mining and versionbits code.
- **Remediation commit:** `fe274e28b84ad168245e633c9f5b2cdcc305bee0`
  explains automatic mainnet bit-12 signaling, the recorded
  pre-STARTED go/no-go decision, and the need to qualify and deploy
  a replacement release across affected operators if the schedule
  must move.
- **Verification:** The focused source-and-document contract in
  `contrib/devtools/test-rip25-release-contract.py` failed four
  documentation assertions against the frozen release notes, while
  its mining-source check passed. It passed all five checks after
  the documentation change. Private logs are
  `evidence/f179-f180-release-contract-red.log` and
  `evidence/f179-f180-release-contract-green.log`.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the release instructions. The actual
  network go/no-go decision and operator adoption remain OPEN
  under FINDING-067.

### FINDING-180: Generic tag-first process conflicts with 4.8.1 release qualification

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release process. A tag made before final artifact
  qualification could point to a candidate that later fails a mandatory gate.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-15 at `32eb10141`, before

  the source SHA whose mandatory tests and five packages passed review,

  before building binaries. The 4.8.1 remediation register instead
  requires final-SHA `make check`, paired CI, artifact checksum and

  pre-tag qualification and approval sequence, then bind the tag to
  that exact SHA. Any publication remains a separate owner decision.
- **Regression required:** A focused release-document contract check

- **2026-10-21 continuation:** The 4.8.1 notes still said the tag would

### FINDING-181: Build Raven loses the wallet RPC during rewrite-failure recovery

- **Severity:** HIGH release qualification until the process exit is
  explained. The mandatory Build Raven security step did not pass.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-16 at `22b65545c`
  after Build Raven run `38805825854` failed, before changing
  the wallet, test, or workflow.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A failed wallet rewrite must not
  silently lose wallet access or bypass recovery checks; the
  release security test must report and verify the actual result.
- **Observed failure:** In the required functional test step,
  `wallet_encryption_rewrite.py` raised
  `ConnectionRefusedError` instead of the expected RPC error
  while inducing a rewrite failure. Its node was unavailable
  during cleanup. The other 32 required functional tests passed.
  Build Raven skipped all artifact jobs. The paired Final Gate
  run `38805825878` passed its security job and all seven builds
  on the same `22b65545c` SHA, so the pair is not a release
  qualification.
- **Initial classification:** The CI log proves a process/RPC
  disappearance in this test, but does not yet establish whether
  the wallet exited on the intended failure path, crashed,
  or the test harness observed a timing race.
- **Root cause assessment:** On this induced failure path,
  `CDB::Rewrite` refuses the injected directory and
  `encryptwallet` calls `StartShutdown` before throwing
  RPC error -16. HTTP replies are dispatched asynchronously;
  the CI trace shows a disconnected reply followed by a
  refused retry. A clean shutdown can therefore outrun the
  expected RPC error. The failed runner did not publish its
  node debug log or exit status, so the exact historical
  process exit remains unproven.
- **Evidence:** Private retained CI output is
  `evidence/ci-22b65545-build-raven-failed.log`.
- **Regression required:** Identify the mechanism, demonstrate
  the failure against the frozen behavior, apply a narrow remedy,
  rerun the same test and mandatory `make check`, then obtain
  a fully green exact-SHA Build Raven and Final Gate pair with
  five checked release packages.
- **Remediation commit:**
  `9688f7e03e17dc9789039a1d2b0ea159a350a489` accepts only
  the exact RPC -16 error or a narrow set of shutdown transport
  errors. It then requires a clean node exit and the path-specific
  `CDB::Rewrite: Refusing stale non-regular path` log entry
  before the existing restart and key-recovery assertions.
- **Verification:** The pre-fix required Build Raven gate is
  the red regression. The revised focused test passed 11
  consecutive runs, the required static gate passed six
  checks, and the complete required functional set passed
  33 of 33 tests under Python 3.10, matching CI. Private
  green records are `evidence/f181-wallet-encryption-rewrite-focused-run1.log`,
  `evidence/f181-wallet-encryption-rewrite-focused-repeat10.log`,
  `evidence/f181-required-functional-gate-static.log`, and
  `evidence/f181-required-functional-gate-full-python310.log`.
  Local `make -j4 check` then passed all 511 unit cases;
  `evidence/local-make-check-f181-final.log` and
  `evidence/local-make-check-f181-final-unit.log` retain
  the result.
  A separate host Python 3.12 attempt could not import
  inherited `asyncore` in 13 unrelated functional tests;
  it is excluded from the green result and retained as
  `evidence/f181-required-functional-gate-full.log`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source `77d8f4c65`.
  Build Raven `38853895481`, security job `116820958622`,
  passed the wallet rewrite recovery test and all 33 required
  functional tests. Final Gate `38853895080` passed on the
  same SHA; the five Build Raven packages passed the checks in
  `evidence/ci-77d8f4c65/artifact-verification.json`.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-182: Linux Qt smoke lacks the pinned GUI XKB runtime

- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-16 at `e67b23c7b`,

  GUI, then failed `Test Qt startup without wallet`. The

  of branch head `22b65545c` into base `7dc448453`.
  The executable exited 127 because the runner lacked

  was a consequence of that startup failure. The package was

  `libxkbcommon-x11-0`, passed the dynamic-library preflight,
  passed both unmodified GUI smokes, and uploaded the Linux

  Final Gate `38853895080` passed on the same SHA. Final
  release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-183: dbus source timeout leaves ARM64 depends without a fallback

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability. The ARM64 package
  cannot build when its primary dbus source host times out.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-16 at `e67b23c7b`,

  be reproducible from pinned source archives on the exact SHA.

  `116672036056`, timed out repeatedly while fetching
  `dbus-1.10.18.tar.gz`. The generic fallback URL was empty,
  so curl failed with an invalid URL and Build Dependencies
  stopped. This job also checked out synthetic merge
  `614dbd5ed`. The run's mandatory security job passed, but
  no complete five-target release set existed.

  `evidence/ci-22b65545-build-raven-arm64-dep-failed.log`.
- **Expected behavior:** A second HTTPS source supplies the
  identical pinned archive when the primary is unavailable;
  the existing SHA-256 check must still reject altered bytes.
- **Regression required:** Force the primary dbus source to
  fail against the old recipe, demonstrate the missing
  fallback, then verify alternate download, pinned digest,
  rejection of altered bytes, `make check`, and exact-SHA
  paired CI with five checked packages.
- **Remediation commit:** `21962b1804b1ab5960b6042756cd5d2a2b3c155e`
  adds the package-specific HTTPS fallback at

  primary source to fail. The frozen recipe failed with an empty
  fallback URL; the remedy fetched a 1,986,589-byte archive from

  `6049ddd5f3f3e2618f615f1faeda0a115104423a7996b7aa73e2f36e38cc514a`.

  and `evidence/local-make-check-f182-f183-unit.log`.
  Build Raven `38853895481`, ARM64 job `116826216414`, built
  and uploaded its package. The primary dbus source succeeded in
  that CI job; the forced-primary-failure and altered-byte tests
  above are the fallback-path evidence. All five downloaded
  packages passed the provenance and checksum checks in
  `evidence/ci-77d8f4c65/artifact-verification.json`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source `77d8f4c65`.
  Final Gate `38853895080` passed on the same SHA. Final
  release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-184: Exact-multiple reward recipients create an empty payout batch

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A completed payout can be reported as a failed
  distribution, complicating safe operator reconciliation.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN before remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Asset reward retries must not manufacture a
  transaction with no recipients after committing all intended batches.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** `src/assets/rewards.cpp` computes the transaction count as
  `paymentDetails.size() / MAX_PAYMENTS_PER_TRANSACTION + 1`. When the
  recipient count is an exact multiple of 1,000, the final iteration starts
  at the end of the vector and calls `BuildTransaction` with no recipients.
- **Concrete scenario:** A reward has exactly 1,000 eligible addresses. The
  first batch can be committed, then an empty second batch fails creation and
  leaves a failure status for a distribution whose intended payout already
  occurred. A repeated request is rejected as a duplicate.
- **Expected behavior:** Use a checked ceiling count, skip empty batches,
  preserve each durable batch intent, and report completion only after all
  real batches are resolved.
- **Regression required:** Prove the old exact-multiple failure, then test
  recipient counts around the 1,000 boundary, including zero, 999, 1,000,
  1,001, and 2,000. Assert no empty transaction attempt and no duplicate
  payout on restart.
- **Remediation commit:** `c90814b1a` uses a ceiling batch count without
  manufacturing an empty batch at exact multiples of 1,000.
- **Verification:** `asset_tests/reward_batch_boundaries` failed six checks
  on the old calculation at zero, 1,000, and 2,000 recipients. It passed
  after the fix for zero, one, 999, 1,000, 1,001, 1,999, 2,000, and 2,001.
  `make -j4 check`, `feature_rewards.py`, and the separate
  `feature_rewards_restart.py` single-payout retry test passed. The boundary
  unit test proves that no empty batch is requested; the restart test checks
  that persisted batch intent does not duplicate a payout.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source 8e87426de in the paired
  exact-head CI workflows; final-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-185: ARM release builds lack a fontconfig source fallback

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release availability. Both ARM packages are absent from
  a Build Raven run that otherwise passed its security gate and other targets.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-19, before changing the pinned
  fontconfig depends recipe.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Every release target must package the same
  tested source SHA, with every downloaded dependency checked against its
  pinned digest.
- **Observed failure:** Build Raven `39143408033` at `54cd1befe` passed its
  required security tests, Windows, macOS, and Linux. ARM32 job
  `117725944066` and ARM64 job `117725944097` timed out six times fetching
  `fontconfig-2.12.1.tar.bz2` from `mirrors.mit.edu`, then called the generic
  fallback with an empty URL (`curl: (3)`). Final Gate `39143408017` passed
  at the same SHA, including its ARM builds, but does not replace the missing
  Build Raven packages.
- **Root cause:** `depends/packages/fontconfig.mk` has one download host and
  no package-specific fallback. The generic fallback in `depends/funcs.mk`
  has no configured `FALLBACK_DOWNLOAD_PATH` in these jobs.
- **Expected behavior:** Use an available pinned-source mirror if the primary
  times out, reject bytes that do not match the existing SHA-256, and produce
  both ARM packages in Build Raven on the final candidate SHA.
- **Regression required:** Force primary failure and prove the fallback fetch
  passes the pinned digest; prove altered bytes are rejected; re-run both ARM
  release builds and all other mandatory jobs at one exact SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `c70679b80` adds a package-specific MacPorts
  archive fallback while retaining the pinned version and SHA-256.
- **Verification:** A forced primary failure reached the empty fallback URL
  on the original recipe. With the fix, the same failure fetched the
  1,608,901-byte MacPorts archive and verified digest
  `b449a3e10c47e1d1c7a6ec6e2016cca73d3bd68fbbd4f0ae5cc6b573f7d6c7f3`.
  A deliberately wrong expected digest rejected the archive without
  installing a source file. Direct primary and fallback fetches passed.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source 8e87426de. Build Raven
  39233323040 produced both ARM packages and the other three targets;
  independent manifests and provenance checks passed. Final-SHA
  qualification after FINDING-187 remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-186: Legacy global message records become unreachable after wallet scoping

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. An operator upgrading a node can lose access through
  RPC to historical messages and subscriptions even though their LevelDB rows
  remain on disk.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN, found during independent review of the
  FINDING-102 wallet-scoping remedy before its commit.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet isolation must preserve a safe way to
  retrieve pre-upgrade notification history without guessing which wallet
  owned each global record.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Core 4.8.0 stored messages under global `Z`
  keys and subscribed channels under global `C` keys in separate LevelDBs.
- **Root cause:** The wallet-scoped message RPCs read only new `W` and `c`
  records. Existing global rows have no wallet identity or reliable manual
  subscription provenance, so automatically assigning them to a wallet would
  leak data or fabricate user preferences.
- **Expected behavior:** Offer an explicitly labeled, read-only export of
  persisted unscoped rows with each message outpoint and lossless payload.
  Fail rather than silently omit malformed rows. Never import, delete, or
  assign the exported rows to a wallet automatically.
- **Regression required:** Show the old RPC has no safe export, then test
  valid legacy rows, an empty legacy database, and malformed legacy values.
- **Remediation commit:** `212610f62` adds the explicit node-level
  `exportlegacymessagedata true` RPC. It returns persisted global messages
  with outpoint, raw payload bytes, time, expiration, height, and status,
  plus global channel names. It rejects wallet endpoints and never attributes
  or imports a legacy row. Legacy database loaders fail on malformed rows
  instead of returning a silently incomplete export.
- **Verification:** The functional export call failed with method-not-found
  before the RPC was added. Unit tests failed on malformed legacy message
  and channel rows before strict loading. The same functional and unit tests
  pass after the fix, including valid and empty exports, CLI boolean
  conversion, explicit acknowledgement, wallet-endpoint rejection, and
  malformed-row errors. `make check -j4` and the invariant gate passed.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source 8e87426de in both exact-head
  workflows. Final-SHA qualification after FINDING-187 remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-187: Asset anchor functional test misclassifies a native PQ output

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release assurance. A valid candidate can fail the
  mandatory artifact security gate before any release package is built.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-21, before changing the test.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** An anchor input and its asset output must be
  identified by script structure, so the mandatory test measures the asset
  rule rather than random program bytes.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This is a RIP-25 functional-test defect.
- **Observed failure:** Pull-request Build Raven run 39232389723 passed
  feature_pq_asset_anchor.py in the 14-test invariant gate, then failed
  the same test in the 38-test suite at line 205: two scripts matched where
  one asset output was expected. The paired pull-request Final Gate
  39232389599 passed. Exact-head workflow-dispatch runs
  39233323040 and 39233335348 both passed.
- **Root cause:** The test treats any script longer than 31 bytes with byte
  0xc0 at offset 25 as an asset output. A native witness-v2 anchor output
  is 34 bytes and has an arbitrary program byte at that offset, so it can
  satisfy the predicate without containing an asset payload. Other checks
  in the same test repeat the fixed-offset predicate.
- **Expected behavior:** Parse the allowed classical or PQ-only asset
  prefix and trailer before accepting the asset marker. A witness-v2
  program containing 0xc0 at offset 25 must remain a native anchor.
- **Regression required:** Construct a deterministic 34-byte witness-v2
  script with 0xc0 at offset 25, prove the old classifier accepts it,
  then prove the corrected classifier rejects it while recognizing both
  classical and PQ-only asset scripts. Re-run the full functional case,
  mandatory make check, and both exact-SHA workflows.
- **Remediation commit:** ebd12027864da7f625264ff910ef10729eaf2da9
  requires the classical or PQ-only asset prefix and trailer before the
  marker is accepted in short-output classifiers.
- **Verification:** The deterministic 34-byte native witness-v2 collision
  failed the old classifier with True where False was required. The corrected
  classifier rejects it and accepts classical and PQ-only asset examples.
  The full feature_pq_asset_anchor.py case, make check -j4, and the local
  structural and behavioral gate with all 14 required functional tests pass.
  The PR merge commit 8d331e70 has the same tree as tested source 8e87426de,
  confirming the failed PR run was not caused by different merged code.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source d9c5b5606. Build Raven
  39553065704 and Final Gate 39553079553 both passed on that exact SHA.
  Final release-SHA qualification remains FINDING-067.

### FINDING-188: Dismissing first-run mnemonic setup creates a different wallet

- **Severity:** HIGH wallet custody and recovery. A user who closes the setup
  window while attempting to restore an existing wallet can receive a new
  wallet with a newly generated mnemonic instead of an explicit cancellation.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-21, before changing GUI or wallet
  creation behavior.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** First-run wallet creation must not silently
  replace a user's recovery intent with unrelated keys. This also governs
  the root from which PQ wallet keys are derived.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774. Its RavenGUI::mnemonic
  ignored the dialog result, and its first-run path generated a seed after
  the modal prompt returned.
- **Root cause:** src/qt/ravengui.cpp RavenGUI::mnemonic invokes dlg.exec()
  but discards its Accepted or Rejected result. src/wallet/wallet.cpp
  CreateWalletFromFile invokes ShowMnemonic and then GenerateNewSeed even
  when no mnemonic was submitted. An empty mnemonic requests generation of
  a new random seed.
- **Concrete scenario:** Start with a new BIP44 wallet, select recovery of an
  old wallet, then close the HD Wallet Setup window with X or Esc. No pending
  recovery input is supplied, yet first-run creation continues. The old
  wallet's keys are not recovered, and the newly generated wallet may be
  mistaken for a successful recovery.
- **Expected behavior:** Distinguish accepted setup from dismissal. On
  dismissal, clear pending secrets and abort first-run wallet creation
  without persisting a new seed or keypool. A later retry must still accept
  the intended recovery mnemonic.
- **Regression required:** First show that a dismissed modal prompt creates
  a wallet on the old path. Then show a red-to-green first-run regression:
  dismissal leaves no committed seed or wallet, while accepted new-wallet
  and recovery paths still complete and persist the selected root. Run the
  Qt dialog path and mandatory make check.
- **Remediation commit:** d70c27578b8f79d2a3c9a0be08637524c8381bf5
  makes a handled, dismissed mnemonic prompt abort before seed generation.
  A daemon with no GUI prompt handler retains its normal first-run seed path.
- **Verification:** The new deterministic Boost test failed on the old
  implementation: the dismissed prompt returned a wallet, emitted a wallet
  load notification, and persisted an HD chain, BIP39 data, and key records.
  With the remedy, it passes: no wallet or key records are committed, a
  reopened wallet remains in first-run state, and a retry with the intended
  BIP39 mnemonic and passphrase derives the expected root. The focused test
  and full `make check -j4` pass. Private red and green logs are
  `evidence/f188-red-first-run.log`, `evidence/f188-green-first-run-retry.log`,
  and `evidence/f188-make-check-final.log`.
- **Final status:** FIXED on tested source d5dbc0d07. Build Raven
  39592731571 and Final Gate 39592731078 passed mandatory `make check` on
  that exact SHA. The pinned-Qt dialog regression passed all five cases in
  the Linux release job. FINDING-039 retains its separate memory-residency
  and manual-GUI limits.

  linkage or the release workflow.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The release must build and execute the
  mnemonic dialog regression against the same pinned Qt dependency used by
  the Linux package.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The existing test link list predates the
  RIP-25 liboqs dependency.
- **Observed failure:** A host build configured with the pinned Qt 5.12.11
  and --enable-gui-tests builds the new focused dialog test but fails
  to link the pre-existing qt/test/test_raven-qt target. Its linker reports
  unresolved OQS_SIG, OQS_MEM_cleanse, and selected ML-DSA backend
  symbols. Private red log: evidence/f039-qt-legacy-target-build.log.
- **Expected behavior:** Both GUI test executables link the selected
  verifier, the focused dialog cases execute under the minimal Qt platform,
  and the Linux package CI job fails if they regress.
- **Regression required:** Reproduce the linker failure on the old link
  list; add the selected liboqs library; rebuild both Qt test targets;
  run the focused dialog test and a source mutation that it must reject;
  then require the green test in the pinned Linux artifact job.
- **Remediation commit:** 00f873f10 adds LIBOQS_LIBS to the Qt test link
  list.
  The Linux release configuration enables GUI tests, and Build Raven's
  Linux package job runs the focused mnemonic test under Qt's minimal
  platform after its GUI smoke checks.
- **Verification:** The old target failed with unresolved selected
  verifier symbols. Both Qt test targets now link using pinned Qt 5.12.11;
  the focused dialog test and its Automake registration pass. A temporary
  source mutation that skipped pending-input cleanup failed the intended

### FINDING-190: Wallet cancellation guard trips the atomic-creation gate

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release assurance. The mandatory invariant script

  triggered by the FINDING-188 cancellation guard.

  count and then invokes that signal. The structural gate searches for
  the broad ShowMnemonic name as a unique line number, obtains two line
  numbers, and fails arithmetic parsing at its BIP44 boundary check.

  evidence/f190-final-gate-failed.log.

  unique boundary anchor. A handler-count query before it is legitimate.
  Removing the modal invocation must still fail the gate.

  prove the corrected script accepts it and rejects a source mutation

  as the unique boundary anchor. Its negative-control suite creates

### FINDING-191: Truncated witness fixture skipped the chunked read

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release assurance, within FINDING-020's missing
  mutation-sensitivity scope.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-22 before the test edit.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Untrusted witness elements must be read in
  bounded chunks, and a truncated element must not replace the prior
  witness stack.
- **Root cause:** The regression advertised an element of 0x02000000
  bytes. Its CompactSize prefix made the encoded element exceed
  `MAX_SIZE`, so the parser rejected it before its chunked read. The
  test's 64-KiB assertion observed zero read requests and passed even
  when the chunk limiter was disabled.
- **Observed failure:** A bait-preserving mutation changed the live
  `std::min<uint64_t>(remaining, READ_CHUNK_SIZE)` read size to
  `MAX_SIZE`. The old test still passed. This was a test escape, not a
  consensus bypass in the unmodified parser.
- **Expected behavior:** The truncated fixture must pass the encoded-size
  precheck, reach a read request, and reject any request above 64 KiB
  while keeping the original witness stack intact.
- **Remediation:** The fixture now advertises 0x01fffff0 bytes, leaving
  room for its CompactSize prefix. The source mutation matrix forces the
  affected transaction test translation unit to rebuild with the
  mutated header and again after restoration.
- **Red-green proof:** The old fixture passed under the disabled limiter
  in `evidence/f020-source-full-v2-07016ff16/`. With the corrected
  fixture, the mutant test failed at `max_read_request` and the
  restored test passed. All seven full-matrix mutations failed their
  intended assertions and then passed after restoration in
  `evidence/f020-source-full-v3-07016ff16/`.
- **Remediation commit:** `ecad996de3626785e53a7afe8056c52794413c36`.
- **CI proof:** Build Raven `39769743876` and RIP-25 v4.8 Final Gate
  `39769743645` passed on
  `9360f6e2040b6040441763c305d903ae6335d64c`, which contains
  the fixture correction. Both required security jobs passed the
  seven-case source mutation matrix, including the witness chunk-limit
  mutant and restored test. Final release-SHA qualification is tracked
  under FINDING-067.
- **Final status:** FIXED.

### FINDING-192: Downloaded artifact verifier accepts synthetic payloads

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release assurance, within FINDING-020's artifact scope.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-22, before the verifier fix.
- **Affected invariant:** Downloaded release bundles should contain
  structurally parseable target executables and readable installer
  containers, not only matching provenance text and plausible headers.
- **Root cause:** The verifier reads at most the first 4096 bytes of each
  primary binary. It accepts a 64-byte ELF or Mach-O header, a 256-byte PE
  header, a 1024-byte dummy DMG with a `koly` trailer, and arbitrary safe
  signing tar contents after their producer recomputes the manifest.
- **Red proof:** A five-target synthetic bundle using the real checkout source
  archive passed `verify()` with only those header fixtures and dummy
  secondary payloads. This is a verification false positive, not evidence
  that a published package was modified.
- **Expected behavior:** Reject truncated or skeletal binaries and
  installers; parse executable load and section records; test the NSIS
  installer and DMG container contents; require the macOS signing app
  binaries to match the primary macOS package; check required Windows
  signing-bundle contents. State clearly that structure, provenance, and
  checksums do not prove reproducible builds or native runtime behavior.
- **Remediation commit:** `47694cb151db0c1d7aad72771e45507c7459faa2`.
- **Red-green proof:** Eight newly added negative controls failed under the
  header-only verifier. The strengthened verifier passes all 24 tests,
  rejects skeletal ELF, Mach-O, PE, installer, and DMG fixtures, and passes
  all five real `07016ff16` bundles. The unsigned Windows installer and
  macOS DMG pass `7z t`; the three macOS signing-app binaries match the
  primary package byte for byte. Private logs are
  `evidence/f192-artifact-verifier-red.log`,
  `evidence/f192-artifact-verifier-green.log`, and
  `evidence/f192-real-artifact-verification.json`.
- **CI proof:** Build Raven `39810593933` and RIP-25 v4.8 Final Gate
  `39810592954` passed on exact SHA
  `0744508ca25cb7912b361eccbfeccb5592eca972`, which contains this
  verifier correction. Build Raven passed its required security job,
  all five target builds, and the aggregate job that downloaded and
  checked all five bundles. The Final Gate passed its required security
  job and all seven builds.
- **Current status:** FIXED. This does not attest reproducible compilation
  or native Windows/macOS GUI execution. Later package binding defects
  are tracked separately under FINDING-193 and FINDING-194.

### FINDING-193: Installers can be substituted across release commits

- **Severity:** HIGH release assurance, within FINDING-020's artifact scope.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-22, before remediation.
- **Affected invariant:** The Windows installer and macOS DMG downloaded
  for one release commit must install the same executable code as the
  corresponding primary packages from that commit.
- **Observed failure:** In a local adversarial check, valid Windows and
  macOS installers from an earlier real CI run replaced those in five
  otherwise authentic later bundles. After the affected bundle manifests
  and checksum sidecars were recomputed, the verifier accepted all five
  targets and reported the expected checkout source. This is a verifier
  false positive, not evidence of a modified published release.
- **Root cause:** Container integrity checks parse the NSIS installer and
  DMG, but do not bind their installed executable payloads to the primary
  Windows and macOS archives. Bundle-local checksums can attest the
  substituted files consistently without proving cross-package identity.
- **Expected behavior:** Extract the relevant installed executables and
  compare their code with the matching primary package executables.
  Reject missing, duplicate, unreadable, or mismatched members. Account
  explicitly for packaging-only metadata differences in Windows PE files.
- **Remediation commit:** `3eeed5463bc80cbf291ac03792ebaf3c346e4b74`.
- **Red-green proof:** Two new substitution tests failed under the old
  verifier and pass under the corrected one. In a separate real-package
  check, Windows and macOS installers from `8e87426de` replaced those
  in otherwise authentic `07016ff16` bundles with recalculated local
  manifests and sidecars. The old verifier accepted both replacements;
  the corrected verifier rejects both. All 29 verifier tests and all
  five authentic `07016ff16` bundles pass. Private logs are
  `evidence/f193-synthetic-red.log`,
  `evidence/f193-real-cross-sha-red.log`,
  `evidence/f193-unit-green.log`,
  `evidence/f193-real-cross-sha-green.log`, and
  `evidence/f193-real-07016ff16-verification.json`.
- **CI and package proof:** Build Raven `39939214218` and Final Gate
  `39939213883` passed on exact SHA
  `b1a67830c776893de2475a52d0fdb9c87bb5654c`. Build Raven's
  aggregate job and a separate local verification accepted all five
  downloaded bundles. The latter checked the Windows installer programs
  against the primary ZIP and the macOS app against its signing archive;
  its private record is `evidence/ci-b1a67830c/artifact-verification.json`.
- **Current status:** FIXED for executable identity. FINDING-194 separately
  tracks DMG non-executable resources.
  The NSIS launcher, bundled installer plug-ins, and uninstaller have no
  matching executable in the primary ZIP. Their full behavior is outside
  this cross-package identity proof; the verifier does not claim a
  reproducible build or native Windows installer execution.

### FINDING-194: DMG app resources are not bound to the signing archive

- **Severity:** HIGH release assurance, within FINDING-020's artifact scope.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-22, before remediation.
- **Affected invariant:** The macOS app installed from the unsigned DMG
  should contain the same files and safe links as the app in its matching
  unsigned signing archive, not only the same three executable files.
- **Observed gap:** The verifier compares the three app executables with
  the primary tar and checks that `Info.plist` exists in the signing tar.
  It does not compare the remaining DMG app files or links. For example,
  `Contents/Resources/qt.conf` controls Qt plugin lookup and is present in
  the signing app, but its DMG content is not read by the verifier.
- **Root cause:** The DMG inspection selects only three executable paths.
  A bundle-local checksum can still be made consistent with a changed
  DMG resource, while the signing archive remains unchanged.
- **Expected behavior:** Compare the complete installed app inventory
  with the signing archive's app tree, including regular-file contents,
  symlink targets, missing entries, and unexpected entries. Reject unsafe
  paths and ambiguous members. Preserve valid package metadata differences
  only when they are specifically demonstrated by real release artifacts.
- **Remediation commit:** `da383a053816bfa77ba1c2ec70f6898c7ef34360`.
- **Additional hardening commit:** `0553c1f7a21c171d7c4054dcb391287b2a73663f`.
- **Red-green proof:** A changed `qt.conf` and an extra app resource both
  passed the old verifier. The two new tests failed there and pass under
  the corrected verifier. A case-variant alias of the app path also passed
  the initial correction; its focused negative test failed before the
  hardening commit and passes afterward. All 32 verifier tests pass. The corrected
  verifier accepts all five authentic `07016ff16` bundles and compares
  23 entries of the installed DMG app with its signing archive. It checks
  file bytes, directory structure, and any symlink targets. Private records
  are `evidence/f194-synthetic-red.log`,
  `evidence/f194-case-alias-red.log`,
  `evidence/f194-unit-green-v2.log`,
  `evidence/f194-real-07016ff16-verification.json`, and
  `evidence/f194-real-0744508ca-verification-v2.json`.
- **CI and package proof:** Build Raven `39939214218` and Final Gate
  `39939213883` passed on exact SHA
  `b1a67830c776893de2475a52d0fdb9c87bb5654c`. The aggregate job
  and separate local verification accepted all five downloaded bundles.
  The local check compared all 23 entries of the DMG app with its signing
  archive; its private record is
  `evidence/ci-b1a67830c/artifact-verification.json`.
- **Current status:** FIXED. The package checks do not establish native
  macOS GUI behavior or reproducible builds.
