## 2026-10-26 KAWPOW header review of upstream PR #1329

### FINDING-195: A forged KAWPOW header can bypass full work verification

- **Severity:** HIGH consensus and network availability.
- **Review credit:** The forged-work shortcut report and suggested edge
  cases in [RavenProject PR #1329](https://github.com/RavenProject/Ravencoin/pull/1329)
  by `skareemullah8-a11y` prompted this review. The 4.8.1 correction
  preserves the inherited pre-activation height rule.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-26, before remediation.
- **Affected invariant:** A KAWPOW header outside the exact indexed ancestry
  of an enabled checkpoint must satisfy the full ProgPoW hash, including its
  declared `mix_hash`. A persisted invalid header must not become the best
  header or a candidate chain after restart.
- **Observed failure:** A header could claim a height below the last indexed
  checkpoint, supply an arbitrary `mix_hash` meeting the target, and skip
  full work. This also affected checkpoint-height siblings, indexed forks,
  and operation with checkpoints disabled. An earlier node could retain
  the forged branch in its block index.
- **Root cause:** The shortcut trusted the header's declared height instead
  of the header's identity on the indexed checkpoint chain. Block index
  loading did not recheck entries admitted under that rule. A disk index
  key mismatch could also load an entry under a different reconstructed hash.
- **Remediation commit:** `9802c6dc4141cfb3fda4cba3688c43d1fb9147aa`.
  The shortcut now requires exact checkpoint ancestry and a target check.
  Other KAWPOW headers require full work and a matching mix. Header lookup
  is under `cs_main`; known-parent height and old-checkpoint fork failures
  are rejected before expensive hashing. Startup audits saved off-chain
  headers in the former shortcut range, marks invalid branches and children
  failed, checks disk index keys, and refuses an invalid indexed coins tip.
  Pre-activation declared height remains distinct from actual chain height
  in newly written index records so the Core 4.8.0 rule survives restart.
- **Red-green proof:** Three forged-header cases failed on the old code.
  Additional red runs exposed indexed-fork reuse of the shortcut, saved
  forged-branch selection, disk key mismatch, early rejection ordering,
  and loss of an honest pre-activation header on restart. All 11 focused
  header and restart cases pass after the correction. The complete local
  `make check` passed with 535 `test_raven` cases; the
  `feature_chainstate_ahead.py` functional test passed, preserving the
  existing unpruned chainstate recovery path. Private proof is under
  `evidence/f195-f197/` in the audit report directory.
- **Current status:** FIXED for the tested path on upstream PR checkpoint
  `c63cad804e606211b3c87ff84d14ca3dff9c9c44`. Build Raven
  `40327113350` and Final Gate `40327113034` passed on that exact SHA;
  all five downloaded bundles passed independent source and package checks.
  A historical index written without the new
  declared-height field may require reindexing to recover an uncommon
  pre-activation header whose declared and actual heights differ.

### FINDING-196: Concurrent KAWPOW verification shares mutable epoch state

- **Severity:** HIGH network availability and verification reliability.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-26, before remediation.
- **Affected invariant:** Concurrent KAWPOW hash checks must not replace or
  free the epoch context used by another verification thread.
- **Observed failure:** Concurrent hashing across two epochs crashed with
  signal 139 in the focused regression before the correction.
- **Root cause:** `KAWPOWHash` cached a mutable epoch context in a function
  static shared by all threads.
- **Remediation commit:** `9a8ed2eddc5a16e10d100c31bd462454c591cdbb`.
  The cached context is thread local.
- **Red-green proof:** The new two-epoch concurrent regression crashed on
  the old implementation, then passed with the thread-local context. The
  original hash vectors and the complete local `make check` passed, with
  535 `test_raven` cases and no errors.
- **Current status:** FIXED for the tested path on upstream PR checkpoint
  `c63cad804e606211b3c87ff84d14ca3dff9c9c44`; its Build Raven
  `40327113350` and Final Gate `40327113034` both passed.

### FINDING-197: Extreme pre-activation KAWPOW heights can exhaust memory

- **Severity:** HIGH network availability; consensus-safe remediation is
  not yet defined.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-26.
- **Affected invariant:** An untrusted header should not force unbounded
  KAWPOW epoch-context allocation before cheap validation can reject it.
- **Observed gap:** Before the Core 4.8.0 header-height activation, a
  declared KAWPOW height need not equal the known parent height if full
  work validates. A very large declared height could therefore reach epoch
  context creation while syncing before the last checkpoint, on an
  off-checkpoint fork, or with `-checkpoints=0`. Before mitigation,
  allocation failure could return null to an unchecked dereference, and
  extreme epochs could enter signed integer calculations outside the
  tested range.
- **Disposition:** The focused pre-activation higher-height regression
  confirms that a height mismatch with full work remains valid under the
  inherited rule. A provisional height cap was removed because it would
  silently tighten historical consensus. A bounded verifier or an explicit
  activated rule needs design and historical-chain evidence before changing
  acceptance. A cheap target check before full hashing can reject some bad
  headers but is not a resource bound because an attacker can grind the
  submitted mix and nonce to pass it. Do not present FINDING-195's early
  known-parent check as a complete fix for this case.
- **Mitigation commit:** `3806d70ccc46f2457d61c4d61b10141df1aec01e`.
  The bundled verifier's signed arithmetic and a configurable local
  context-memory budget are checked before allocation. The default is
  256 MiB per context, adjustable with `-maxkawpowcontextmb`. Unsupported
  arithmetic or failed allocation returns a local validation error,
  without invalidating the header, penalizing its peer, or persisting
  `BLOCK_FAILED_VALID`. Header and compact-block network paths stop
  safely on that error; a saved-header audit stops startup without
  changing the saved header's validity status. Switching epochs releases
  the previous thread-local context before allocating the next one.
- **Red-green proof:** Before the change, the extreme-height regression
  exited 201 with `high-hash` and `IsInvalid()` true. After the change,
  it reports a local error with no new block index entry. A constrained
  budget test first errors on an otherwise valid pre-activation header,
  then accepts the same header after raising the budget, with no failed
  index status. The arithmetic-boundary tests and all 21 focused KAWPOW
  tests passed. Local `make check -j4` passed 538 `test_raven` cases;
  `feature_chainstate_ahead.py` and the mandatory structural lint passed.
- **Additional mitigation and evidence commits:**
  `bb2396130f5699ec4eecb90c6da34ee10320ecf7` checks the already
  required contextual KAWPOW difficulty before building an epoch context.
  With a 16 MiB local budget, the new bad-difficulty regression returned
  a resource error before that change and `bad-diffbits` afterward. All
  22 focused KAWPOW tests, structural lint, and local `make check -j4`
  with 539 `test_raven` cases passed. A correct difficulty can still
  force context work. `6d5e96964af1622c0633ef8b92a0008aab332223`
  adds a read-only canonical mainnet header scanner. It checks the
  pinned checkpoint, raw-header links, declared-height differences,
  and any resumed prefix against the node before claiming complete
  results. Parser, batch RPC, resume-integrity, and rejection tests
  passed. No mainnet scan result is claimed yet.
- **Full block precheck commit:** `1fd1b4628` applies the same known-parent
  difficulty and failed-parent checks before `ProcessNewBlock` calls
  `CheckBlock`. The earlier check in `AcceptBlockHeader` was too late for a
  complete block: `CheckBlockHeader` could first allocate a KAWPOW epoch
  context. The new complete-block regression, run under a 16 MiB local
  context budget, failed before the correction with exit 201 and local
  `kawpow-context-unsupported`; it passed afterward with `bad-diffbits`
  and no new block index entry. The complete local `make check -j4`
  passed 540 `test_raven` cases. The structural and behavioral invariant

### FINDING-198: Chainstate invariant gate rejects the safer tip classification

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release assurance. It blocks the mandatory security
  job before artifact builds.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-26, after exact-head Build Raven
  run `40325515170` and Final Gate run `40325517341` failed their structural
  security steps on `a38049a05a235c05c42944ceacac6e6d80dd42c5`.
- **Affected invariant:** Startup may automatically rebuild an unpruned
  chainstate whose best block is missing from the block index, while an
  indexed invalid tip and a pruned unknown tip must fail closed.
- **Observed failure:** The gate requires the old literal
  `!mapBlockIndex.count(pcoinsTip->GetBestBlock())`. FINDING-195 replaced it
  with a lookup that separately identifies a failed indexed tip and guards
  both unsafe rebuild cases. The gate reports `chainstate-ahead detection
  missing` before running the mandatory tests, even though the updated
  `feature_chainstate_ahead.py` passed locally.
- **Expected correction:** Check the active `LoadChainTip` failure path for
  the new lookup, failed-status classification, combined ahead condition,
  both fail-closed guards, and the automatic retry for an unpruned unknown
  tip. Negative controls must reject removal of those protections.
- **Remediation commit:** `887eb98f30853d32ffd4736f9c9c6b72c0997c12`.
- **Red-green proof:** The old structural gate failed on both exact-head
  workflows and on the local baseline. Focused mutation controls
  initially failed with that stale diagnostic. With the corrected scoped
  check, the baseline passes, all four mutations are rejected for their
  intended reasons, and all 27 invariant-gate negative controls pass.
  `bash -n` and `git diff --check` also pass.
- **Current status:** FIXED for the tested structural gate on upstream
  PR checkpoint `c63cad804e606211b3c87ff84d14ca3dff9c9c44`.
  Build Raven `40327113350` and Final Gate `40327113034` passed.

### FINDING-199: Windows consensus-library link depends on node arguments

- **Severity:** MEDIUM release assurance; the mandatory Windows Final
  Gate build cannot finish.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN on 2026-10-27 after Final Gate run
  `40455702781` failed on upstream PR head
  `30349977b90b8e1743a3948d3f613090b3e78523`.
- **Affected invariant:** The separately linked consensus library must
  not depend on the node's global argument manager. The local KAWPOW
  context budget must still apply in the node without changing block
  validity or the default limit.
- **Observed failure:** The Windows Final Gate job passed dependency
  build, source checks, and configure, then failed while linking
  `libravenconsensus.la`. The object built from `hash.cpp` referenced
  `gArgs` and `ArgsManager::GetArg` through the FINDING-197 budget
  check; those symbols are absent from the shared consensus library.
  Build Raven `40455702783` passed all five release targets and its
  aggregate bundle verifier on the same SHA. The Final Gate security
  job and its six other platform builds passed; only Windows failed.
- **Required correction and proof:** Decouple the standalone KAWPOW
  hash helper from `gArgs` while preserving the node's runtime option
  and the default 256 MiB limit for other callers. Reproduce the old link
  failure, then pass the same Windows consensus-library build, the
  focused budget regression, `make check`, and paired exact-SHA CI.
  Private CI evidence includes
  `evidence/ci-30349977b/final-gate-windows-job.log`.
- **Remediation commit:** `c4d1b20ad` keeps the existing `gArgs` read
  in node builds but uses the conservative 256 MiB default when
  `BUILD_RAVEN_INTERNAL` builds the standalone consensus library.
  The external library has no node argument manager and no override.
- **Red-green proof:** The pre-fix consensus-library object had unresolved
  `gArgs` and `ArgsManager::GetArg` symbols. The rebuilt object had
  neither. Local `libravenconsensus.la` linked, all 17 focused KAWPOW
  tests passed, and `make check -j4` passed 540 `test_raven` cases.
  The structural invariant gate and diff review passed. Private
  records are under `evidence/f199/`. On upstream PR head
  `aa5b128c565104c19c9ba9837797acaae4983f4c`, Build Raven
  [40499782364](https://github.com/ALENOC/Ravencoin/actions/runs/40499782364)
  passed its required tests, five release builds, and aggregate bundle
  verifier. Final Gate
  [40499782271](https://github.com/ALENOC/Ravencoin/actions/runs/40499782271)
  passed required tests and all seven build variants, including the
  formerly failing Windows consensus-library link. Both runs reported
  the same `aa5b128c5` checkout SHA. The local exact-head `make check -j4`
  passed 540 `test_raven` cases.
- **Follow-up hardening:** `16c2d00a1` removes the argument manager from
  `hash.cpp`. Startup validates `-maxkawpowcontextmb` and passes it to the
  hash code. ELF builds link the shared consensus library with
  `-Wl,-z,defs`, so an unresolved node symbol fails on Linux as it did on
  Windows. See the 2026-10-27 replay-bound checkpoint below. That check
  then failed both 32-bit ARM Final Gate variants on `c76826b36`:
  `algo/sha2.c` declared `sha256_transform` external on that target, but
  the tree has no implementation. `c86a18d96` compiles the existing C
  transform there. See the 2026-10-28 exact-head checkpoint below.
- **Current status:** FIXED on the tested upstream PR head
  `a756574d49c40ebc492399733e70d2f922c2b210`, where every Final Gate
  variant linked the consensus library and each ELF variant did so with
  `-Wl,-z,defs`. The first fix was confirmed on `aa5b128c5`. The failed
  `30349977b` and `c76826b36` Final Gate runs do not qualify release
  artifacts. Any later source or documentation change still requires its
  own final-SHA qualification under FINDING-067.

FINDING-067 remains HIGH and OPEN. Commits for FINDING-195 and FINDING-196
change the candidate source SHA and invalidate prior exact-head CI and
artifact qualification. Build Raven `40325515170` on upstream PR head
`a38049a05a235c05c42944ceacac6e6d80dd42c5` failed the stale invariant
check in FINDING-198 before artifact builds. Final Gate `40325517341`

green `make check`, paired

## 2026-10-27 FINDING-197 replay bound and FINDING-199 link guard

After `1fd1b4628`, three local FINDING-197 gaps remained. A header with
the correct difficulty could reach epoch-context allocation without
meeting its claimed target. The same header could be replayed to force
another context and ProgPoW run. The allocation-failure branch had no
deterministic test.

Commit `a147a994d` checks every KAWPOW header and block against its target
using the final hash computed from the claimed mix, before any epoch
context is built. A valid proof has the same final hash under full
verification, so this rejects only headers that full verification would
also reject. The checkpoint-ancestor shortcut keeps the same check. Under
a 16 MiB budget the new over-target regression returns `high-hash`, and
an under-target claim still needs full work and reports the local budget
error.

Commit `ccb576e13` stores completed full proof results in a bounded exact cache
of 4096 entries keyed by that claimed-mix final hash. The key commits to
every header field, the nonce, and the claimed mix, so a stored valid or
invalid result equals a fresh calculation. Local budget and allocation
errors are never stored, and contextual checks still run on every
submission. With the cache lookup disabled, the replayed invalid-proof
regression returned `kawpow-context-allocation-failed` instead of its
first `invalid-mix-hash`, and the replayed valid-proof regression returned
`kawpow-context-allocation-failed` instead of `time-too-new`. Both passed
with the lookup restored. A test-only allocation failure now exercises the
local-error branch: the honest header is neither indexed nor marked
invalid, and it is accepted once allocation succeeds. The startup thread
also releases its KAWPOW context after loading and verifying the block
index.

Peer messages reach KAWPOW verification only on the single
message-handler thread, and each thread caches one context. Peer input
can therefore hold at most one context within the configured budget.
Other threads verify only when a local import, startup, or authenticated
RPC call asks them to, and each also holds at most one context. Reaching
a context requires a claimed final hash that meets the required target, so
each new header costs its sender the expected target work in final-hash
attempts, although those attempts skip the memory-hard ProgPoW mix.
Replaying that header costs the node one cache lookup.

Commit `16c2d00a1` completes the FINDING-199 correction. `hash.cpp` no longer
reads the argument manager: startup validates `-maxkawpowcontextmb` and
passes it through `SetKAWPOWContextBudgetMiB`, and the standalone library
keeps the 256 MiB default without a preprocessor branch. A zero, negative,
or non-numeric value now stops startup instead of turning every KAWPOW
proof into a local error. Libtool's `-no-undefined` rejects unresolved
symbols only in a Windows DLL, which is why only the Windows Final Gate
caught FINDING-199. ELF builds now also link the shared consensus library
with `-Wl,-z,defs` when the linker accepts it, except sanitizer builds. A
local shared link of the consensus objects with the `3806d70cc`
`hash.cpp` succeeded without that flag and failed with it on undefined
`gArgs` and `ArgsManager::GetArg`. The objects from `16c2d00a1` and `ccb576e13` link
with it.

Local verification of the `ccb576e13` source on the fork candidate branch:
`make check -j6` passed 546 `test_raven` cases, all 29 focused KAWPOW
cases passed, the structural lint passed, and the behavioral invariant
gate passed under Python 3.10, including its negative-control test and
all 14 required functional tests. Commits `a147a994d` and `16c2d00a1` each built and
passed the focused KAWPOW cases on their own source. A regtest node
refused `-maxkawpowcontextmb` values `0`, `-5`, and `abc` at startup and
started with `64`. Regtest does not activate KAWPOW, so the runtime
budget path is covered by the unit regressions.

FINDING-197 remains HIGH and OPEN only for the canonical mainnet
declared-height evidence. The mainnet replay that feeds the scanner has
not yet reached the checkpoint height. FINDING-199 remains FIXED. These
commits change the source SHA, so paired exact-SHA CI and package review
are due under FINDING-067 before any release decision.

## 2026-10-27 FINDING-197 canonical mainnet declared-height evidence

The last FINDING-197 question was whether any canonical mainnet header
before the height gate declares an epoch above the default 256 MiB
context budget. Such a header would stop default-budget initial sync
until an operator raised `-maxkawpowcontextmb`.

Commit `3a5c1f232` adds `contrib/devtools/kawpow-declared-height-blockfiles.py`.
It reads raw block files and trusts neither their order nor a node index.
The walk starts at the pinned checkpoint hash for height 4,487,775 and
follows previous-block links. For every KAWPOW header on the walk, the
tool recomputes the final hash from the stored header fields, nonce, and
claimed mix and requires it to equal the expected hash. Each declared
height is therefore bound to the checkpoint by the hash chain. The
checkpoint is a consensus constant, so the walk identifies the canonical
ancestry without relying on transaction validation. The final hash does
not prove the ProgPoW mix, and this question does not need that proof.
The self-test checks the checkpoint header against its pinned hash.

Results on the 291 archived mainnet block files used by the replay:

- The walk reached 3,268,040 KAWPOW headers, heights 1,219,736 through
  4,487,775. Height 1,219,736 is the first KAWPOW block, timestamped
  2020-05-06 18:00:23 UTC, 23 seconds after activation. Its parent is a
  legacy-format block.
- Every canonical KAWPOW header declares exactly its actual height. There
  are no mismatches, and the largest declared epoch is 598.
- That epoch needs a 91 MiB context under the budget formula, well inside
  the 256 MiB default. Every declared height is also inside the verifier's
  signed-integer bound.
- The files hold exactly 1,219,736 legacy records, one for each height 0
  through 1,219,735. The 20,183 KAWPOW records off the walk are later
  blocks with declared heights 4,487,776 through 4,507,955 and timestamps
  from 2026-08-10. No other KAWPOW-format record exists near activation or
  below the checkpoint. The bytes skipped between records were zero
  padding.
- 41,312 walked heights also appear in the `UpdateTip` log of the
  validating replay, which runs with `-assumevalid=0` and
  `-checkpoints=0`. Every hash matched.

Since height 4,487,776 the 4.8.0 rule forces each declared height to equal
the actual height, so later contexts follow the chain's own epoch. That
epoch advances by about 70 per year. The default budget therefore covers
the chain until about epoch 1919, roughly 19 years after the checkpoint,
and operators can raise it earlier.

The RPC scanner on a fully validated chain remains a useful cross-check
once the replay reaches the checkpoint. It no longer blocks this finding,
because the checkpoint-anchored walk answers the same question for the
canonical ancestry. The private report is
`evidence/f197-canonical-scan/blockfile-scan.json`.

FINDING-197 is FIXED locally by `a147a994d`, `ccb576e13`, and this evidence.
FINDING-199 remains FIXED. The later commits need paired exact-SHA CI and
package review under FINDING-067 before release qualification.

## 2026-10-28 PR #1330 exact-head CI checkpoint after FINDING-197

The first CI cycle on the FINDING-197 replay bound and the FINDING-199

Commit `c86a18d96` removes the 32-bit ARM branch, so every target
compiles the C transform already in the file. No other source file
calls these functions. On x86_64 the preprocessed source, object text,

now matches x86_64. Under `src/`, this file is the only change after the

A local x86_64 build through the real libtool path, with shared
libraries enabled, linked `libravenconsensus.la` with `-Wl,-z,defs`.

failed that link on `gArgs` and `ArgsManager::GetArg`. Without the flag
the link succeeded and left both symbols undefined in the library.

`c86a18d96`. Its
[Build Raven run 40599833317](https://github.com/ALENOC/Ravencoin/actions/runs/40599833317)

ARM variants, with and without the wallet, configure enabled shared
libraries, accepted `-Wl,-z,defs`, and linked `libravenconsensus.la`
with no undefined reference. The Windows linker does not accept that
flag; its DLL relies on libtool's `-no-undefined`, and that link passed.
The fork PR runs
[Build Raven 40599829809](https://github.com/ALENOC/Ravencoin/actions/runs/40599829809)
and [Final Gate 40599829816](https://github.com/ALENOC/Ravencoin/actions/runs/40599829816)

- `contrib/devtools/verify_build_raven_artifacts.py`, run against the

  publishes eight unsigned distributions and `SHA256SUMS-unsigned.txt`.

The download review also inspected the published RC1 ARM32 package. Its
`libravenconsensus.so` has `sha256_transform` undefined and requests
immediate binding, so the library fails to load. This is the latent gap
corrected by `c86a18d96`. The RC1 node, Qt, and CLI executables do not
link that library, and the RC2 library defines the symbol. The RC1
release page now states that RC2 supersedes it.

RC2 is not a stable release. FINDING-067 remains HIGH and OPEN for
operator readiness, the bit-12 go/no-go, and the final signed release.
Native Windows and macOS GUI operation remains assigned to community
testing. This record changes the source SHA; it does not alter the RC2
tag or qualify a later head.
